  Malliavin Calculus*** (3rd ed., 2014, [Edizioni della Normale](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-88-7642-499-1); first ed. 2007). Gaussian
  analysis on Hilbert space and the Malliavin calculus, in a short
  course.
* **Terry Lyons and Zhongmin Qian, *System Control and Rough
  Paths***. The original systematic account of rough path theory.
* **Terry J. Lyons, Michael Caruana, and Thierry Lévy, *Differential
  Equations Driven by Rough Paths*** (2007,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-71285-5), Lecture Notes in
  Mathematics). Lyons's own short course on rough paths, from Young
  integration to the extension theorem and the universal limit theorem.
* **Giuseppe Da Prato and Jerzy Zabczyk, *Stochastic Equations in
  Infinite Dimensions***. The basic analytic reference for SPDE via
  semigroups and infinite-dimensional stochastic integration.
* **Giuseppe Da Prato and Jerzy Zabczyk, *Ergodicity for Infinite
  Dimensional Systems***. Invariant measures and long-time behaviour
  of stochastic evolution equations.
* **Claudia Prévôt and Michael Röckner, *A Concise Course on
  Stochastic Partial Differential Equations***. The variational
  approach, short and usable.
* **S. Peszat and J. Zabczyk, *Stochastic Partial Differential
  Equations with Lévy Noise***. The standard reference beyond Gaussian
  forcing.
* **John B. Walsh, *An Introduction to Stochastic Partial Differential
  Equations***. The Saint-Flour notes that introduced the random-field
  approach.
* **Robert C. Dalang, Davar Khoshnevisan, Carl Mueller, David Nualart,
  and Yimin Xiao, *A Minicourse on Stochastic Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2009, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-85994-9), Lecture Notes in Mathematics). Six
  short courses that still orient the subject.
* **Helge Holden, Bernt Øksendal, Jan Ubøe, and Tusheng Zhang, *Stochastic
  Partial Differential Equations: A Modeling, White Noise Functional
  Approach*** (2nd ed., 2010,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-89488-1); first ed. 1996). White-noise
  distributions and Wick products as tools for stochastic PDE. Included for an
  approach substantially different from Hilbert-space stochastic evolution.
* **Hui-Hsiung Kuo, *Gaussian Measures in Banach Spaces***. Abstract
  Wiener spaces and the calculus on them; the standard short entry point
  to infinite-dimensional Gaussian analysis.

### More recent works

#### Malliavin calculus and Gaussian analysis

* **David Nualart and Eulalia Nualart, *Introduction to Malliavin
  Calculus*** (2018, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781139856485)). A graduate textbook,
  shorter and more introductory than Nualart's research monograph.
* **Vladimir I. Bogachev, *Differentiable Measures and the Malliavin
  Calculus*** (2010, American Mathematical Society). The best single
  book for the measure-theoretic foundations, and the natural companion
  to Nualart.
* **Yaozhong Hu, *Analysis on Gaussian Spaces*** (2016, [World
  Scientific](https://doi.org/10.1142/10094)). Gaussian measures, chaos
  expansions, Malliavin calculus, and hypercontractivity, with applications
  to stochastic equations.
* **Hiroyuki Matsumoto and Setsuo Taniguchi, *Stochastic Analysis*** (2016,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781316492888)). Itô
  and Malliavin calculus developed together from the viewpoint of path space.
  Includes Wiener-space distributions and applications to semiclassical
  analysis.
* **Ivan Nourdin and Giovanni Peccati, *Normal Approximations with Malliavin
  Calculus: From Stein's Method to Universality*** (2012, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139084659)). The
  Malliavin–Stein method, Gaussian approximation on Wiener chaos, and
  universality. A research monograph connecting stochastic calculus with
  quantitative limit theory.
* **Horst Osswald, *Malliavin Calculus for Lévy Processes and
  Infinite-Dimensional Brownian Motion*** (2012, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139060110)). Malliavin calculus beyond
  finite-dimensional Brownian motion, including jump processes. A specialised
  complement to the standard Wiener-space textbooks.
* **Vlad Bally, Lucia Caramellino, and Rama Cont, *Stochastic Integration by
  Parts and Functional Itô Calculus*** (2016, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-27128-6)). Integration by
  parts for jump processes, and the functional Itô calculus.
* **Nicolas Bouleau and Laurent Denis, *Dirichlet Forms Methods for Poisson
  Point Measures and Lévy Processes: With Emphasis on the
  Creation–Annihilation Techniques*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-25820-1)). Dirichlet-form
  methods for differentiating Poisson and Lévy functionals. A research
  treatment of the jump-process counterpart of Gaussian Malliavin calculus.

#### Rough paths and singular stochastic PDE

* **Peter K. Friz and Nicolas B. Victoir, *Multidimensional Stochastic
  Processes as Rough Paths*** (2010, Cambridge University Press). The
  comprehensive reference, with the Gaussian theory developed in
  detail.
* **Peter K. Friz and Martin Hairer, *A Course on Rough Paths*** (2014,
  Springer; 2nd ed. 2020). The standard modern course, including an
  introduction to regularity structures.

#### Stochastic PDE

* **Wei Liu and Michael Röckner, *Stochastic Partial Differential
  Equations: An Introduction*** (2015, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-22354-4)). The variational
  approach to SPDE, as a textbook continuing Prévôt–Röckner.
* **Sergey V. Lototsky and Boris L. Rozovsky, *Stochastic Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2017, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-58647-2)).
  A systematic graduate account of stochastic PDE, including variational and
  Wiener-chaos methods. Broadens the approaches available beside the Da
  Prato–Zabczyk and Liu–Röckner texts.
* **Davar Khoshnevisan, *Analysis of Stochastic Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2014, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cbms/119), CBMS Regional
  Conference Series). The random-field approach: intermittency, peaks,
  and the parabolic Anderson model.
* **Feng-Yu Wang, *Harnack Inequalities for Stochastic Partial
  Differential Equations*** (2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-7934-5)). Coupling and Harnack
  inequalities for stochastic evolution equations.
* **Lorenzo Zambotti, *Random Obstacle Problems*** (2017, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-52096-4),
  Lecture Notes in Mathematics). Reflected SPDEs and the contact set,
  from the Saint-Flour lectures.
* **Tadahisa Funaki, *Lectures on Random Interfaces*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-981-10-0849-8)).
  Listed in Section 8. Interface models as singular SPDEs.
* **Sergei Kuksin and Armen Shirikyan, *Mathematics of Two-Dimensional
  Turbulence*** (2012, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139137119)). Listed in Section
  34. The stochastic Navier–Stokes equation as a dynamical system.

---

## 10. Ergodic Theory and Measurable Dynamics

Walters, Einsiedler–Ward, Host–Kra, and Katok–Hasselblatt are central
references. The more recent books fill in smooth ergodic theory,
homogeneous random walks, entropy, symbolic dynamics, billiards, and
several textbook routes.

### Foundational works

* **Peter Walters, *An Introduction to Ergodic Theory***. The standard
  concise graduate introduction to measure-preserving dynamics,
  entropy, and topological dynamics.
* **Karl Petersen, *Ergodic Theory***. A classical systematic
  treatment of measure-preserving transformations.
* **I. P. Cornfeld, S. V. Fomin, and Ya. G. Sinai, *Ergodic
  Theory***. The large classical reference for entropy, K-systems, and
  measurable dynamics.
* **Ulrich Krengel, *Ergodic Theorems***. The definitive
  functional-analytic treatment of pointwise, mean, and subadditive
  ergodic theorems.
* **Harry Furstenberg, *Recurrence in Ergodic Theory and Combinatorial
  Number Theory***. The founding text of the ergodic-theoretic
  approach to Szemerédi-type problems.
* **Anatole Katok and Boris Hasselblatt, *Introduction to the Modern
  Theory of Dynamical Systems***. The standard large graduate
  reference for smooth and hyperbolic dynamics.
* **Jon Aaronson, *An Introduction to Infinite Ergodic Theory***. The
  standard monograph for conservative transformations with infinite
  invariant measure.
* **Eli Glasner, *Ergodic Theory via Joinings***. A research monograph
  organized around joinings and structural theory.
* **M. G. Nadkarni, *Basic Ergodic Theory***. A compact first course
  emphasizing the spectral and measurable viewpoint.
* **Douglas Lind and Brian Marcus, *An Introduction to Symbolic Dynamics and
  Coding*** (2nd ed., 2021, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108899727); first ed. 1995). Shifts of finite type,
  sofic shifts, entropy, and conjugacy problems. The standard introduction.
* **David Ruelle, *Thermodynamic Formalism***. The foundational
  monograph on transfer operators, Gibbs states, and equilibrium
  measures.
* **Rufus Bowen, *Equilibrium States and the Ergodic Theory of Anosov
  Diffeomorphisms***. Short, foundational, and still the cleanest
  treatment of symbolic dynamics for hyperbolic systems.
* **William Parry and Mark Pollicott, *Zeta Functions and the Periodic
  Orbit Structure of Hyperbolic Dynamics***. The standard bridge from
  symbolic dynamics to zeta functions and orbit counting.
* **Viviane Baladi, *Positive Transfer Operators and Decay of
  Correlations***. The functional-analytic theory of transfer
  operators; its successor on anisotropic Banach spaces, *Dynamical Zeta
  Functions and Dynamical Determinants for Hyperbolic Maps*, is listed
  under More recent works.
* **Luis Barreira and Yakov Pesin, *Nonuniform Hyperbolicity: Dynamics of
  Systems with Nonzero Lyapunov Exponents*** (2007, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781107326026)). Pesin theory in full:
  stable manifolds, absolute continuity, and the entropy formula. The
  research-level complement to their *Introduction to Smooth Ergodic
  Theory*, listed under More recent works.
* **Christian Bonatti, Lorenzo J. Díaz, and Marcelo Viana, *Dynamics
  Beyond Uniform Hyperbolicity: A Global Geometric and Probabilistic
  Perspective*** (2005, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/b138174)). The research monograph on partial
  hyperbolicity and robust transitivity.
* **Nikolai Chernov and Roberto Markarian, *Chaotic Billiards*** (2006,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/127),
  Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). Hyperbolicity, ergodicity, and decay
  of correlations for dispersing billiards. The working monograph.
* **Robert J. Zimmer, *Ergodic Theory and Semisimple
  Groups***. Rigidity, cocycles, and group actions; the origin of a
  large part of modern measurable group theory.
* **M. Bachir Bekka and Matthias Mayer, *Ergodic Theory and Topological
  Dynamics of Group Actions on Homogeneous Spaces*** (2000, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511758898)). The
  Howe–Moore theorem, Moore's ergodicity theorem, and horocycle flows on
  homogeneous spaces, with the representation theory supplied.
* **Dave Witte Morris, *Ratner's Theorems on Unipotent Flows*** (2005,
  [University of Chicago Press](https://press.uchicago.edu/ucp/books/book/chicago/R/bo3621083.html)). The readable account of Ratner theory.
* **Robert J. Zimmer and Dave Witte Morris, *Ergodic Theory, Groups,
  and Geometry*** (2008, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cbms/109), CBMS). Cocycle
  superrigidity in a short conference monograph.
* **Mark Pollicott and Michiko Yuri, *Dynamical Systems and Ergodic
  Theory***. A readable modern textbook bridging topological and
  measurable dynamics.
* **Michel Coornaert, *Topological Dimension and Dynamical Systems***
  (French original 2005). Dimension theory, mean dimension, and topological dynamics. Useful for
  analysts studying infinite-dimensional phenomena in dynamical systems.
* **Yakov G. Sinai, *Topics in Ergodic Theory***. Short lectures on
  entropy, mixing, Gibbs measures, and billiards from one of the founders
  of the modern subject.
* **Gerhard Keller, *Equilibrium States in Ergodic Theory***. A compact
  introduction to the variational principle, equilibrium states, and
  thermodynamic formalism.
* **Klaus Schmidt, *Dynamical Systems of Algebraic Origin***.
  Multidimensional actions by automorphisms of compact groups; entropy,
  mixing, and rigidity.

### More recent works

#### Textbooks and general theory

* **Manfred Einsiedler and Thomas Ward, *Ergodic Theory with a View
  Towards Number Theory*** (2011, Springer). The best modern route
  into homogeneous dynamics and its arithmetic uses.
* **Marcelo Viana and Krerley Oliveira, *Foundations of Ergodic Theory***
  (2016, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781316422601)). A modern graduate course from
  invariant measures to entropy and the ergodic theorem.
* **Steven Kalikow and Randall McCutcheon, *An Outline of Ergodic Theory***
  (2010, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511801600)). An exercise-driven
  graduate account of ergodic theorems, entropy, and isomorphism theory.
  Particularly useful for learning how proofs in measurable dynamics are
  assembled.
* **Yves Coudène, *Ergodic Theory and Dynamical Systems*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-7287-1)). A short course bridging topological and measurable
  dynamics.
* **Tanja Eisner, Bálint Farkas, Markus Haase, and Rainer Nagel,
  *Operator Theoretic Aspects of Ergodic Theory*** (2015, Springer).
  The modern functional-analytic reformulation, and the natural sequel
  to Krengel.
* **David Kerr and Hanfeng Li, *Ergodic Theory: Independence and
  Dichotomies*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-49847-8)). Entropy, sofic and combinatorial
  independence, and the measurable structure of group actions.
* **Tomasz Downarowicz, *Entropy in Dynamical Systems*** (2011, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511976155)).
  Measure-theoretic and topological entropy, symbolic extensions, and
  entropy structure. A research monograph.
* **Valerio Lucarini, Davide Faranda, Ana Cristina Moreira Freitas, Jorge
  Milhazes Freitas, Mark Holland, Tobias Kuna, Matthew Nicol, Mike Todd, and
  Sandro Vaienti, *Extremes and Recurrence in Dynamical Systems*** (2016,
  [Wiley](https://doi.org/10.1002/9781118632321)). Extreme-value laws and
  hitting-time statistics for dynamical systems. Connects probabilistic limit
  theory with recurrence, mixing, and applications to chaotic evolution.

#### Smooth and hyperbolic dynamics

* **Luis Barreira and Yakov Pesin, *Introduction to Smooth Ergodic
  Theory*** (2013, American Mathematical Society). Lyapunov exponents,
  entropy, and Pesin theory.
* **Marcelo Viana, *Lectures on Lyapunov Exponents*** (2014, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139976602)). The multiplicative ergodic theorem and its
  consequences, in a concise graduate account.
* **Luis Barreira, *Lyapunov Exponents*** (2017, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-71261-1)). A more
  expansive treatment of the same circle, including dimension.
* **Luis Barreira, *Ergodic Theory, Hyperbolic Dynamics and Dimension
  Theory*** (2012, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-28090-0)).
  Ergodic methods, hyperbolicity, and dimension estimates developed together.
  A course-length bridge from measure-preserving transformations to geometric
  dynamics.
* **Todd Fisher and Boris Hasselblatt, *Hyperbolic Flows*** (2019,
  [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/200), Zurich Lectures in Advanced
  Mathematics). Continuous-time hyperbolic dynamics, shadowing, and
  structural stability.
* **José F. Alves, *Nonuniformly Hyperbolic Attractors: Geometric and
  Probabilistic Aspects*** (2020, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-62814-7)). Statistical properties of
  attractors with singularities, in the Viana school.
* **Viviane Baladi, *Dynamical Zeta Functions and Dynamical Determinants for
  Hyperbolic Maps: A Functional Approach*** (2018,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-77661-3)). Transfer operators
  on anisotropic Banach spaces and their consequences for zeta functions and
  correlations. The successor to her *Positive Transfer Operators*.

#### Group actions, homogeneous dynamics, and combinatorics

* **Bernard Host and Bryna Kra, *Nilpotent Structures in Ergodic
  Theory*** (2018, American Mathematical Society). The definitive
  account of characteristic factors, nilsystems, and higher-order
  recurrence.
* **Yves Benoist and Jean-François Quint, *Random Walks on Reductive
  Groups*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-47721-3), Ergebnisse der Mathematik). Random walks
  on homogeneous spaces, stationary measures, and stiffness.
* **Anatole Katok and Viorel Niţică, *Rigidity in Higher Rank Abelian
  Group Actions***, volume I (2011, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511803550)). KAM
  and measure rigidity for higher-rank abelian actions.
* **Alexander S. Kechris, *Global Aspects of Ergodic Group Actions***
  (2010, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/160)). Listed in Section 3.
* **Terence Tao, *Higher Order Fourier Analysis*** (2012, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/142)). Listed in full in Section 27. Gowers norms,
  nilsequences, and the ergodic-theoretic form of higher-order Fourier
  analysis.
* **Michael Baake and Uwe Grimm, *Aperiodic Order*** (2 volumes, 2013–2017,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139025256); [volume II](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/aperiodic-order/500A4C153E539E73A8146865D78737D6)).
  Tilings, quasicrystals, and their diffraction theory. Volume II,
  *Crystallography and Almost Periodicity*, is where Fourier analysis and
  dynamics meet.

---

# Part III. Inequalities, Convexity, and Order

The literatures are genuinely different: the classical inequality
tradition, the convex-analytic tradition, the matrix tradition, and
the functional-inequality tradition have distinct canons and largely
disjoint citation patterns.

---

## 11. Classical Inequalities, Means, Majorization, and Rearrangement

Hardy–Littlewood–Pólya, Marshall–Olkin–Arnold, and Lieb–Loss remain the
central references. The more recent books treat Hardy inequalities,
symmetrization, and the stability of matter.

### Foundational works

* **G. H. Hardy, J. E. Littlewood, and G. Pólya, *Inequalities***. The
  founding monograph for means, convexity, majorization,
  rearrangements, and classical integral inequalities; still the book
  whose table of contents defines the subject.
* **E. F. Beckenbach and Richard Bellman, *Inequalities***. The
  complementary classical source, with differential, integral, and
  matrix inequalities and a distinctly mid-century flavour.
* **D. S. Mitrinović, *Analytic Inequalities***. The single most
  useful compendium for locating a named classical inequality and its
  variants.
* **D. S. Mitrinović, J. E. Pečarić, and A. M. Fink, *Classical and
  New Inequalities in Analysis*** and ***Inequalities Involving
  Functions and Their Integrals and Derivatives***. Large systematic
  compendia of refinements, converses, and generalizations.
* **A. W. Marshall, I. Olkin, and B. C. Arnold, *Inequalities: Theory
  of Majorization and Its Applications***. The standard modern
  reference for majorization, Schur convexity, doubly stochastic maps,
  and their remarkably wide applications.
* **P. S. Bullen, *Handbook of Means and Their Inequalities***. The
  systematic reference for classical and generalized means, power
  means, and the relations among them.
* **P. S. Bullen, D. S. Mitrinović, and P. M. Vasić, *Means and Their
  Inequalities***. The earlier and more theorem-oriented companion to
  the *Handbook*.
* **Elliott H. Lieb and Michael Loss, *Analysis***. Rearrangement
  inequalities, Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev, sharp constants, and a
  distinctively physical motivation throughout; the best modern book
  on sharp inequalities.
* **Bernd Kawohl, *Rearrangements and Convexity of Level Sets in
  PDE***. The standard entry point to symmetrization as a PDE
  technique.
* **Yu. D. Burago and V. A. Zalgaller, *Geometric
  Inequalities***. Isoperimetry, mixed volumes, and comparison
  inequalities from the geometric side.
* **Rolf Schneider, *Convex Bodies: The Brunn–Minkowski Theory***. The
  definitive reference for the Brunn–Minkowski inequality and its many
  descendants.
* **Richard J. Gardner, *Geometric Tomography***. The natural
  continuation for anyone whose inequalities are geometric.
* **G. Pólya and G. Szegő, *Problems and Theorems in Analysis
  I–II***. An inexhaustible source of classical inequalities together
  with the techniques that prove them.
* **G. Pólya and G. Szegő, *Isoperimetric Inequalities in Mathematical
  Physics***. The classical source for the shape-optimization
  tradition in eigenvalue and capacity inequalities.
* **Nicholas D. Kazarinoff, *Analytic Inequalities***. A short and
  pedagogically excellent introduction.
* **Constantin P. Niculescu and Lars-Erik Persson, *Convex Functions
  and Their Applications: A Contemporary Approach***. Modern,
  well-organized, and good on Jensen-type inequalities in their many
  forms.
* **Josip Pečarić, Frank Proschan, and Y. L. Tong, *Convex Functions,
  Partial Orderings, and Statistical Applications***. Majorization and
  convex ordering in a form useful well beyond statistics.
* **D. J. H. Garling, *Inequalities: A Journey into Linear Analysis***
  (2007, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511755217)). Classical inequalities
  developed into the tools of Banach-space theory: interpolation, summing
  operators, and Grothendieck's inequality.
* **Alois Kufner, Lars-Erik Persson, and Natasha Samko, *Weighted
  Inequalities of Hardy Type*** (2nd ed., 2017, [World Scientific](https://doi.org/10.1142/10052); first ed.,
  by Kufner and Persson, 2003). The updated reference for weighted
  Hardy inequalities and their constants.
* **Vladimir Maz'ya, *Sobolev Spaces***. Listed again in Part V, but
  indispensable here for sharp constants, capacitary inequalities, and
  necessary-and-sufficient conditions.
* **Dorin Bucur and Giuseppe Buttazzo, *Variational Methods in Shape
  Optimization Problems***. Where inequalities for eigenvalues and
  capacities become a variational subject.
* **Wolfgang Walter, *Differential and Integral Inequalities***.
  Comparison principles, Gronwall-type inequalities, and maximum
  principles for ordinary and partial differential equations.
* **Luis A. Santaló, *Integral Geometry and Geometric Probability***. The
  classical treatise on kinematic formulas, Crofton formulas, and
  geometric probability.
* **Rolf Schneider and Wolfgang Weil, *Stochastic and Integral
  Geometry***. Integral geometry and random closed sets, point processes,
  and random tessellations.
* **Helmut Groemer, *Geometric Applications of Fourier Series and Spherical
  Harmonics***. Fourier and spherical harmonic methods for convex bodies,
  stability of geometric inequalities, and characterizations.
* **Georges Matheron, *Random Sets and Integral Geometry***. The founding
  monograph on random closed sets, Choquet capacities, and their integral
  geometry.

### More recent works

* **Elliott H. Lieb and Robert Seiringer, *The Stability of Matter in
  Quantum Mechanics*** (2010, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511819681)). Lieb–Thirring
  inequalities, electrostatic inequalities, and the analytic proof of
  stability of matter.
* **Albert Baernstein II, *Symmetrization in Analysis***, with David
  Drasin and Richard S. Laugesen (2019, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781139020244)).
  Symmetrization, polarization, and the \*-function, completed after
  Baernstein's death. The modern monograph on the method.
* **Alexander A. Balinsky, W. Desmond Evans, and Roger T. Lewis, *The
  Analysis and Geometry of Hardy's Inequality*** (2015, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-22870-9)).
  Hardy inequalities on domains and manifolds, with the geometric
  conditions made explicit.
* **Michael Ruzhansky and Durvudkhan Suragan, *Hardy Inequalities on
  Homogeneous Groups*** (2019, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-02895-4)). Hardy inequalities in the
  subelliptic and homogeneous-group setting.
* **Rupert L. Frank, Ari Laptev, and Timo Weidl, *Schrödinger Operators:
  Eigenvalues and Lieb–Thirring Inequalities*** (2023, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009218436)). Listed in full in Section 22. Sharp spectral inequalities and
  their constants.

---

## 12. Convexity, Convex Analysis, and Variational Analysis

Finite- and infinite-dimensional convexity, conjugate duality, monotone
operators, and nonsmooth and variational analysis. Rockafellar and
Rockafellar–Wets set the language; the more recent books extend the
calculus to Banach spaces and record the first-order methods that now sit
next to it.

### Foundational works

* **R. Tyrrell Rockafellar, *Convex Analysis***. The foundational
  modern monograph: convex sets, conjugacy, subgradients, and duality
  in finite dimensions.
* **R. Tyrrell Rockafellar and Roger J.-B. Wets, *Variational
  Analysis***. The research-level extension to nonsmooth, set-valued,
  and epigraphical analysis; dense but definitive.
* **Jean-Baptiste Hiriart-Urruty and Claude Lemaréchal, *Convex
  Analysis and Minimization Algorithms I–II*** and the abridged
  ***Fundamentals of Convex Analysis***. The most readable systematic
  treatment of finite-dimensional convexity.
* **Jonathan M. Borwein and Adrian S. Lewis, *Convex Analysis and
  Nonlinear Optimization***. Convex duality, cones, entropy, and
  modern applications, with unusually good exercises.
* **Ivar Ekeland and Roger Temam, *Convex Analysis and Variational
  Problems***. The classic bridge from convex duality to PDE and the
  calculus of variations.
* **Hedy Attouch, Giuseppe Buttazzo, and Gérard Michaille, *Variational
  Analysis in Sobolev and BV Spaces: Applications to PDEs and
  Optimization*** (2006; 2nd ed., 2014, [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611973488)). Convex and nonsmooth analysis
  in the function spaces used for PDE.
* **Viorel Barbu and Teodor Precupanu, *Convexity and Optimization in
  Banach Spaces***. The Banach-space theory, carefully done.
* **Constantin Zălinescu, *Convex Analysis in General Vector Spaces***
  (2002, [World Scientific](https://doi.org/10.1142/5021)). Convex analysis
  in locally convex and general vector spaces, with duality and
  well-conditioning results stated in their natural generality. A research
  reference.
* **Frank H. Clarke, *Optimization and Nonsmooth Analysis***. The
  foundational monograph on generalized gradients and nonsmooth
  calculus.
* **Jonathan M. Borwein and Qiji J. Zhu, *Techniques of Variational
  Analysis*** (2005, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/0-387-28271-8), CMS
  Books in Mathematics). Variational principles and subdifferential calculus
  in Banach spaces, built on smooth and nonsmooth versions of Ekeland's
  principle.
* **Boris S. Mordukhovich, *Variational Analysis and Generalized
  Differentiation I–II***. The comprehensive modern treatment of
  limiting subdifferentials and their calculus.
* **Asen L. Dontchev and R. Tyrrell Rockafellar, *Implicit Functions and
  Solution Mappings: A View from Variational Analysis*** (2009; 2nd ed., 2014,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4939-1037-3)). The implicit-function theorem for variational inequalities
  and generalised equations.
* **Aleksandr D. Alexandrov's convexity work, and Herbert Busemann,
  *Convex Surfaces***. The classical geometric strand, still relevant
  to second-order differentiability results.
* **Peter M. Gruber, *Convex and Discrete Geometry*** (2007,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-71133-9), Grundlehren).
  Convex functions and bodies, mixed volumes, the geometry of numbers, and
  packing. A large reference written from a single viewpoint.
* **Roger Webster, *Convexity***. A clean elementary account of
  finite-dimensional convexity.
* **Steven R. Lay, *Convex Sets and Their Applications***. Convex sets
  at the same elementary level, with geometric applications.
* **Alexander Barvinok, *A Course in Convexity*** (2002, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/054), Graduate Studies
  in Mathematics). Convex sets, duality, ellipsoids, lattices, and
  semidefinite relaxations. A problem-rich graduate text.
* **A. Ben-Tal and A. Nemirovski, *Lectures on Modern Convex
  Optimization***. Included because conic duality and the structure of
  convex cones have become common analytic vocabulary.
* **Stephen Boyd and Lieven Vandenberghe, *Convex Optimization*** (2004,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511804441)). The standard course in finite-dimensional
  convex optimisation, and still the book analysts open for conic
  formulations and duality in applications.
* **Robert R. Phelps, *Lectures on Choquet's Theorem*** and ***Convex
  Functions, Monotone Operators and Differentiability***. The two
  standard short monographs on integral representation and on
  differentiability of convex functions in Banach spaces.
* **Stephen Simons, *From Hahn–Banach to Monotonicity*** (2nd ed., 2008,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4020-6919-2), Lecture Notes in Mathematics). Monotone operators approached
  through a refined Hahn–Banach theory.
* **Regina S. Burachik and Alfredo N. Iusem, *Set-Valued Mappings and
  Enlargements of Monotone Operators*** (2008, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-69757-4)). Enlargements,
  the Fitzpatrick function, and the geometry of monotone operators.
* **Gustave Choquet, *Lectures on Analysis I–III***. Idiosyncratic and
  wide-ranging, with the original treatment of integral representation
  and capacities.
* **Jean-Pierre Aubin and Hélène Frankowska, *Set-Valued Analysis***.
  Limits, continuity, derivatives, and measurable selections of set-valued
  maps. The standard reference.

### More recent works

* **Osman Güler, *Foundations of Optimization*** (2010,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-68407-9)). Convexity,
  separation, conjugate duality, and optimality conditions, developed as
  mathematical foundations. Useful to analysts seeking the theory behind
  optimization methods.
* **Fatma Kılınç-Karzan and Arkadi Nemirovski, *Essential Mathematics for
  Convex Optimization*** (2025, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009510561)). Convex sets, separation,
  duality, conic representations, and optimality conditions. A recent graduate
  synthesis of the mathematical foundations of convex optimization.
* **Juan Peypouquet, *Convex Optimization in Normed Spaces: Theory,
  Methods and Examples*** (2015, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-13710-0)). A short Banach-space course
  aimed at evolution equations and algorithms.
* **Frank H. Clarke, *Functional Analysis, Calculus of Variations and
  Optimal Control*** (2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-4820-3), Graduate Texts in Mathematics). A
  graduate course joining nonsmooth calculus, critical-point theory, and
  control.
* **Daniel Hug and Wolfgang Weil, *Lectures on Convex Geometry*** (2020,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-50180-8), Graduate Texts in Mathematics). A modern graduate course in
  convex geometry, shorter than Schneider.
* **Heinz H. Bauschke and Patrick L. Combettes, *Convex Analysis and
  Monotone Operator Theory in Hilbert Spaces*** (2011; 2nd ed., 2017,
  Springer). The modern Hilbert-space synthesis of convexity, monotone
  operators, projections, and proximal methods.
* **Jonathan M. Borwein and Jon D. Vanderwerff, *Convex Functions:
  Constructions, Characterizations and Counterexamples*** (2010, Cambridge
  University Press). The infinite-dimensional theory and its pathologies.
* **Barry Simon, *Convexity: An Analytic Viewpoint*** (2011, Cambridge
  University Press). Choquet theory, Krein–Milman, Loewner and Bernstein
  theorems, and convexity as it is actually used by analysts.
* **Yurii Nesterov, *Lectures on Convex Optimization*** (2nd ed., 2018,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-91578-4)). Complexity of first-order methods, self-concordance, and
  the structure of smooth convex problems.
* **Amir Beck, *First-Order Methods in Optimization*** (2017, [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611974997)).
  Proximal methods, acceleration, and the convex-analytic facts those
  algorithms use.
* **Jean-Paul Penot, *Calculus Without Derivatives*** (2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-4538-8),
  Graduate Texts in Mathematics). A systematic nonsmooth calculus in
  Banach spaces.
* **Boris S. Mordukhovich, *Variational Analysis and Applications***
  (2018, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-92775-6)). A later, application-facing synthesis, distinct from
  the 2006 two-volume treatise.
* **Alexander D. Ioffe, *Variational Analysis of Regular Mappings:
  Theory and Applications*** (2017, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-64277-2)). Metric regularity, error
  bounds, and the inverse-function theory of set-valued maps.
* **J. Frédéric Bonnans, *Convex and Stochastic Optimization*** (2019,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-14977-2)). Convex duality,
  optimality, and stochastic optimization in a common framework. A graduate
  continuation connecting variational analysis with probabilistic models.
* **Grigoriy Blekherman, Pablo A. Parrilo, and Rekha R. Thomas (eds.),
  *Semidefinite Optimization and Convex Algebraic Geometry*** (2013,
  [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611972290)). Positivity, sums of
  squares, moments, and convex duality in polynomial optimization. Included
  for the analytic and convex-geometric foundations of the semidefinite
  methods.
* **Jaroslav Lukeš, Jan Malý, Ivan Netuka, and Jiří Spurný, *Integral
  Representation Theory: Applications to Convexity, Banach Spaces and
  Potential Theory*** (2010, [De
  Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110203219)). Choquet theory and its
  function-space generalisations, applied to Banach spaces and potential
  theory. The modern successor to Phelps.

---

## 13. Matrix Analysis, Operator Monotonicity, and Noncommutative Inequalities

Matrix inequalities, majorization and unitarily invariant norms, operator
monotone and convex functions, trace ideals and s-numbers, and the
noncommutative entropy inequalities. Bhatia, Horn–Johnson, and Hiai–Petz
are the standard references; the more recent books are the
quantum-information companions, matrix functions, and total positivity.

### Foundational works

* **Rajendra Bhatia, *Matrix Analysis***. The standard graduate
  monograph on matrix inequalities, majorization, singular values,
  unitarily invariant norms, and perturbation of eigenvalues.
* **Rajendra Bhatia, *Positive Definite Matrices***. Matrix means, the
  geometry of the positive cone, operator convexity, and the Loewner
  order.
* **Roger A. Horn and Charles R. Johnson, *Matrix Analysis*** and
  ***Topics in Matrix Analysis***. Large references for matrix
  inequalities, spectral comparison, norms, canonical forms, and
  Hadamard and Kronecker products.
* **Fuzhen Zhang, *Matrix Theory: Basic Results and Techniques*** (1999;
  2nd ed., 2011, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-1099-7)). A concrete reference for inequalities, canonical
  forms, and the Schur complement.
* **Masanori Ohya and Dénes Petz, *Quantum Entropy and Its
  Use***. Relative entropy, monotonicity under completely positive
  maps, and the operator-inequality background to quantum statistical
  mechanics.
* **Karl Löwner's original work, as presented in Bhatia and in
  Donoghue**: **William F. Donoghue, Jr., *Monotone Matrix Functions
  and Analytic Continuation***. The classical monograph linking
  operator monotonicity to Pick functions and Nevanlinna
  representation.
* **Barry Simon, *Trace Ideals and Their Applications***. Schatten
  classes, determinants, and trace inequalities; the standard
  reference for the infinite-dimensional case.
* **I. C. Gohberg and M. G. Krein, *Introduction to the Theory of
  Linear Nonselfadjoint Operators***. Singular numbers, s-number
  inequalities, and the classical theory of trace-class perturbation.
* **Albrecht Pietsch, *Eigenvalues and s-Numbers***. The systematic
  theory of approximation numbers and eigenvalue estimates for
  operator ideals.
* **Peter Lancaster and Miron Tismenetsky, *The Theory of
  Matrices***. A large general reference for matrix theory.
* **Nicholas J. Higham, *Functions of Matrices: Theory and Computation***
  (2008, [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717778)). Square roots,
  logarithms, exponentials, and sign functions of matrices, with their
  theory, conditioning, and algorithms.
* **Alexander Markus, *Introduction to the Spectral Theory of
  Polynomial Operator Pencils***. Spectral theory of polynomial
  pencils, for when a matrix question is really spectral.
* **Ingemar Bengtsson and Karol Życzkowski, *Geometry of Quantum
  States: An Introduction to Quantum Entanglement*** (2006; 2nd ed., 2017,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781139207010)). The geometry of density matrices, from
  majorization to entanglement.
* **Gerd Grubb and Joachim Weidmann** for the differential-operator
  analogues, and **Michael Sh. Birman and Michael Solomyak** for
  eigenvalue asymptotics; see Part IV.
* **Xingzhi Zhan, *Matrix Inequalities***. Short lecture notes on
  majorization, operator monotone functions, and norm inequalities, with
  many recent results.
* **Richard S. Varga, *Geršgorin and His Circles***. Eigenvalue
  inclusion regions from Geršgorin's theorem to Brauer, Ostrowski, and
  minimal Geršgorin sets.
* **Karl E. Gustafson and Duggirala K. M. Rao, *Numerical Range: The Field
  of Values of Linear Operators and Matrices***. Convexity, spectral
  inclusion, and numerical radius inequalities.

### More recent works

* **Fumio Hiai and Dénes Petz, *Introduction to Matrix Analysis and
  Applications*** (2014, Springer). Matrix monotonicity, convexity,
  entropy, and the noncommutative inequalities used in quantum
  information.
* **Eric Carlen, *Trace Inequalities and Quantum Entropy*** (2010,
  American Mathematical Society). A set of lecture notes that has become
  the standard modern introduction.
* **Barry Simon, *Loewner's Theorem on Monotone Matrix Functions***
  (2019, Springer). A modern book-length treatment with many proofs of the
  central theorem and its surroundings.
* **Shaun M. Fallat and Charles R. Johnson, *Totally Nonnegative Matrices***
  (2011, [Princeton University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400839018)). Factorisations, spectral
  properties, and determinantal inequalities for totally nonnegative
  matrices. Complements Karlin and Pinkus.
* **Miroslav Fiedler, *Matrices and Graphs in Geometry*** (2011, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511973611)). Matrix methods
  for simplex geometry, distance matrices, and geometric inequalities. A
  specialised complement where matrix positivity has a concrete geometric
  interpretation.
* **John Watrous, *The Theory of Quantum Information*** (2018, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781316848142)). Channels, norms, semidefinite formulations, and
  entropy inequalities, written as mathematics.
* **Guillaume Aubrun and Stanisław J. Szarek, *Alice and Bob Meet
  Banach: The Interface of Asymptotic Geometric Analysis and Quantum
  Information Theory*** (2017, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/223)).
  Concentration, random states, and the analyst's route into quantum
  information.
* **Erling Størmer, *Positive Linear Maps of Operator Algebras*** (2013,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-34369-8)). Positive and
  completely positive maps, decomposability, and their structure. A
  specialised monograph connecting operator inequalities with
  operator-algebraic quantum theory.
* **Sabine Burgdorf, Igor Klep, and Janez Povh, *Optimization of Polynomials
  in Non-Commuting Variables*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-33338-0)). Noncommutative
  positivity, sums of squares, and semidefinite optimization. A concise
  introduction to the meeting point of operator inequalities and polynomial
  optimization.
* **Mihály Bakonyi and Hugo J. Woerdeman, *Matrix Completions, Moments, and
  Sums of Hermitian Squares*** (2011, Princeton University Press). Positive
  definite completions, moment problems, and sums of squares, treated
  together.

---

## 14. Functional, Isoperimetric, and Geometric Inequalities

Sobolev, Poincaré, logarithmic Sobolev, and isoperimetric inequalities,
their sharp constants, and the geometric and spectral inequalities built on
them. Maz'ya, Bakry–Gentil–Ledoux, and Maggi are the central references;
the more recent additions are stability, symmetrization, and the geometric
inequalities that feed spectral theory.

### Foundational works

* **Vladimir Maz'ya, *Sobolev Spaces, with Applications to Elliptic
  Partial Differential Equations***. The encyclopedic source for
  Sobolev, capacitary, and isoperimetric-type inequalities, with
  necessary and sufficient conditions rather than merely sufficient
  ones.
* **Laurent Saloff-Coste, *Aspects of Sobolev-Type
  Inequalities***. Sobolev, Nash, Poincaré, and heat-kernel
  inequalities on manifolds and graphs.
* **Alexander Grigor'yan, *Heat Kernel and Analysis on
  Manifolds***. Functional inequalities as they control heat kernels
  and volume growth.
* **Thierry Aubin, *Nonlinear Analysis on Manifolds: Monge–Ampère
  Equations*** and ***Some Nonlinear Problems in Riemannian
  Geometry***. Sharp Sobolev constants, best-constant problems, and
  the Yamabe circle of ideas.
* **Emmanuel Hebey, *Nonlinear Analysis on Manifolds: Sobolev Spaces
  and Inequalities***. The functional-analytic complement to Aubin,
  careful about sharp constants and concentration.
* **Cédric Villani, *Optimal Transport: Old and New***. Among other
  things, the most complete account of transport proofs of functional
  inequalities.
* **Michel Ledoux, *The Concentration of Measure Phenomenon***. Listed
  again: isoperimetry and concentration are two views of one subject.
* **Christer Borell's work on Gaussian isoperimetry, and Bogachev's
  *Gaussian Measures***. The Gaussian case, where the sharp results
  are known.
* **Alexander Koldobsky, *Fourier Analysis in Convex Geometry*** (2005,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/116),
  Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). Intersection bodies, the
  Busemann–Petty problem, and embeddings in L^p, through positive definite
  distributions.
* **Antoine Henrot, *Extremum Problems for Eigenvalues of Elliptic
  Operators***. Faber–Krahn, Szegő–Weinberger, and the modern
  shape-optimization treatment of spectral inequalities.
* **Isaac Chavel, *Isoperimetric Inequalities: Differential Geometric
  and Analytic Perspectives***. The geometric side, written for
  analysts.

### More recent works

* **Dominique Bakry, Ivan Gentil, and Michel Ledoux, *Analysis and
  Geometry of Markov Diffusion Operators*** (2014, Springer,
  Grundlehren). Γ-calculus, curvature–dimension conditions, logarithmic
  Sobolev, Poincaré, and Nash inequalities in a single framework; the
  central modern reference.
* **Nicola Fusco, Francesco Maggi, Aldo Pratelli, and others on
  quantitative isoperimetry**, best approached through **Fusco's
  surveys** and **Francesco Maggi, *Sets of Finite Perimeter and
  Geometric Variational Problems*** (2012, Cambridge University Press).
* **Nassif Ghoussoub and Amir Moradifam, *Functional Inequalities: New
  Perspectives and New Applications*** (2013, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/187), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). Hardy, Rellich, and Caffarelli–Kohn–Nirenberg inequalities,
  their improved forms, and best constants.
* **Elliott H. Lieb and Robert Seiringer, *The Stability of Matter in
  Quantum Mechanics*** (2010, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511819681)). Listed in
  Section 11. Sharp functional inequalities with physical constants.
* **Albert Baernstein II, *Symmetrization in Analysis*** (2019,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781139020244)). Listed in Section 11.
* **Antoine Henrot (ed.), *Shape Optimization and Spectral Theory*** (2017,
  [De Gruyter Open](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110550887)). Survey chapters
  on extremal eigenvalue problems for the Laplacian and related operators.
  Open access, and an update to Henrot's *Extremum Problems* above.
* **Rolf Schneider, *Convex Cones: Geometry and Probability*** (2022,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-15127-9)). Conic integral
  geometry, intrinsic volumes, and probabilistic questions about convex cones.
  A later, distinct synthesis beside Schneider's classical convex-body
  treatise.
* **Gabor Toth, *Measures of Symmetry for Convex Sets and Stability*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-23733-6)). Quantitative
  measures of asymmetry and stability for convex bodies. A specialist account
  of how geometric inequalities record departure from symmetry.
* **Luis J. Alías, Paolo Mastrolia, and Marco Rigoli, *Maximum Principles and
  Geometric Applications*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-24337-5)). Weak maximum
  principles, Omori–Yau methods, and geometric consequences. Connects
  differential inequalities with curvature, immersion, and completeness
  questions.
* **Sylvia Serfaty, *Coulomb Gases and Ginzburg–Landau Vortices***
  (2015, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/152), Zurich Lectures in Advanced
  Mathematics). Listed in full in Section 36. Large-deviation and
  isoperimetric structure of Coulomb systems.
* **Bozhidar Velichkov, *Regularity of the One-phase Free Boundaries***
  (2023, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-13238-4)). Listed in full in Section 33. A later lecture
  account of one free-boundary model, following on quantitative isoperimetry.

---

# Part IV. Functional Analysis, Operator Theory, and Operator Algebras

---

## 15. Core Banach and Hilbert Space Theory

Banach and Hilbert spaces, bounded operators, weak topologies, duality,
and the first steps of spectral theory. Conway, Rudin, Brezis, and Lax
remain the standards; the more recent books are alternative graduate
courses, several of them spectral from the start.

### Foundational works

* **John B. Conway, *A Course in Functional Analysis***. The standard
  graduate course: Banach and Hilbert spaces, operators, weak
  topologies, compactness, Banach algebras, and spectral theory.
* **Walter Rudin, *Functional Analysis***. Fast, elegant, and broad;
  particularly strong on distributions, Fourier transforms, and
  locally convex methods.
* **Haim Brezis, *Functional Analysis, Sobolev Spaces and Partial
  Differential Equations***. The modern analysis–PDE interface,
  emphasizing weak convergence, monotonicity, Sobolev spaces, and
  variational methods.
* **Peter D. Lax, *Functional Analysis***. A distinctive analyst's
  course, strong on operators, PDE applications, and the history of
  the ideas.
* **Gert K. Pedersen, *Analysis Now***. Compact and high-level, moving
  rapidly from Banach spaces through spectral theory to C*-algebras.
* **Kôsaku Yosida, *Functional Analysis***. A classical comprehensive
  treatment with semigroups, distributions, and evolution equations.
* **Barbara MacCluer, *Elementary Functional Analysis***. Short and
  clear, and useful for filling gaps quickly.
* **Nicholas Young, *An Introduction to Hilbert Space***. A short
  Hilbert-space course at the same elementary level.
* **Michael Reed and Barry Simon, *Methods of Modern Mathematical
  Physics I: Functional Analysis***. One of the best short routes to
  the functional analysis actually used in spectral theory.
* **Erwin Kreyszig, *Introductory Functional Analysis with
  Applications***. More elementary than the rest, but a useful bridge
  for readers arriving from differential equations or applied
  analysis.
* **Vladimir Kadets, *A Course in Functional Analysis and Measure Theory***
  (Russian original 2006; English translation 2018,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-92004-7)). A substantial
  graduate course with many problems, unifying measure theory and Banach-space
  methods. Particularly useful for developing working knowledge through
  exercises.
* **Robert E. Megginson, *An Introduction to Banach Space Theory***. A
  systematic graduate reference emphasizing weak topologies, duality,
  and Banach-space structure.
* **Robert E. Edwards, *Functional Analysis: Theory and
  Applications***. Large, concrete, and rich in examples and classical
  operator theory.
* **F. Riesz and B. Sz.-Nagy, *Functional Analysis***. A historical
  classic whose Hilbert-space viewpoint remains influential and whose
  treatment of monotone functions and ergodic theorems is still worth
  reading.
* **N. I. Akhiezer and I. M. Glazman, *Theory of Linear Operators in
  Hilbert Space***. Classical detailed operator theory, especially for
  unbounded operators, extensions, and spectral theory.
* **Serge Lang, *Real and Functional Analysis***. A transitional
  graduate text unifying measure, locally convex methods, and
  differential calculus in Banach spaces.
* **Rajendra Bhatia, *Notes on Functional Analysis***. Short lectures
  on Banach and Hilbert space and spectral theory, with the matrix
  analyst's eye for the essential example.
* **John von Neumann, *Functional Operators I–II***. The Princeton
  lectures on measures and integrals and on the geometry of orthogonal
  spaces; a primary source for projection lattices and alternating
  projections.

### More recent works

* **Christopher Heil, *Metrics, Norms, Inner Products, and Operator
  Theory*** (2018, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-65322-8)). A bridge from metric spaces to bounded
  operators, useful before a full graduate course.
* **Markus Haase, *Functional Analysis: An Elementary Introduction***
  (2014, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/156), Graduate Studies in Mathematics).
  A short Hilbert-and-Banach course, distinct from Haase's functional
  calculus monograph.
* **James C. Robinson, *An Introduction to Functional Analysis*** (2020,
  [Cambridge University
  Press](https://www.cambridge.org/highereducation/books/an-introduction-to-functional-analysis/261D9C94C952E5FD68B5A5C21973B27B)).
  A first graduate course with detailed proofs and worked exercises,
  culminating in compact-operator spectral theory and weak compactness. Useful
  for preparation for variational methods and PDE.
* **Manfred Einsiedler and Thomas Ward, *Functional Analysis, Spectral
  Theory, and Applications*** (2017, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-58540-6), Graduate Texts in
  Mathematics). A large graduate course that reaches spectral theory,
  unitary representations, and dynamics.
* **Theo Bühler and Dietmar A. Salamon, *Functional Analysis*** (2018,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/191), Graduate Studies in Mathematics). A
  careful modern course, strong on unbounded operators and the
  functional calculus.
* **Adam Bowers and Nigel J. Kalton, *An Introductory Course in
  Functional Analysis*** (2014, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4939-1945-1)). Kalton's last graduate
  course, published posthumously: bases, operators, and the geometric
  facts a first course usually omits.
* **Douglas Farenick, *Fundamentals of Functional Analysis*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-45633-1)). A graduate course
  through Banach and Hilbert spaces, operators, and spectral theory. Offers a
  modern operator-oriented route to the core material.
* **Jan van Neerven, *Functional Analysis*** (2022, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009232487)). A graduate course that
  integrates measure theory, spectral theory, semigroups, and applications
  to PDE and probability. Written by one of the authors of *Analysis in
  Banach Spaces*.
* **Milivoje Lukić, *A First Course in Spectral Theory*** (2022,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/226)). Listed in full in Section 22.
* **Michel Willem, *Functional Analysis: Fundamentals and Applications***
  (2013, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-7004-5)). A concise course aimed at variational methods.
* **Philippe G. Ciarlet, *Linear and Nonlinear Functional Analysis with
  Applications*** (2013, [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611972597)). A very large course from linear
  functional analysis through nonlinear equations, written for applied
  analysts.
* **Marián Fabian, Petr Habala, Petr Hájek, Vicente Montesinos, and
  Václav Zizler, *Banach Space Theory: The Basis for Linear and
  Nonlinear Analysis*** (2011, Springer). A very large modern
  textbook-reference hybrid, useful for the structural facts most courses
  omit.

---

## 16. Locally Convex Spaces, Duality, and Distributions

Locally convex topology, duality, barrelledness, nuclearity and tensor
products, and the theory of distributions built on them. Trèves,
Schaefer–Wolff, and Meise–Vogt remain the structural references; the more
recent books include locally convex and non-Archimedean analysis, uniform
measures, and distribution-theory courses written for PDE.

### Foundational works

* **François Trèves, *Topological Vector Spaces, Distributions and
  Kernels***. The canonical analyst's bridge from locally convex
  spaces to distributions, kernels, nuclearity, and PDE.
* **Helmut H. Schaefer and Manfred P. Wolff, *Topological Vector
  Spaces***. The standard structural reference: duality, Mackey
  topologies, barrelledness, bornological spaces, and ordered TVS.
* **Gottfried Köthe, *Topological Vector Spaces I–II***. The
  encyclopedic classical source for locally convex theory, sequence
  spaces, and duality.
* **Hans Jarchow, *Locally Convex Spaces***. A systematic modern
  reference with strong coverage of barrelledness, bornologies,
  nuclearity, and operator ideals.
* **Reinhold Meise and Dietmar Vogt, *Introduction to Functional
  Analysis***. The best modern textbook route into Fréchet spaces,
  splitting theory, and the structure theory used in PDE and complex
  analysis.
* **John Horváth, *Topological Vector Spaces and Distributions***. A
  careful large-scale treatment closely tied to generalized functions.
* **Lawrence Narici and Edward Beckenstein, *Topological Vector Spaces***
  (1985; 2nd ed., 2011, [CRC Press](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781584888673)). A large updated reference for the
  point-set and duality theory.
* **Alexander Grothendieck, *Topological Vector Spaces*** and the
  *Produits tensoriels topologiques* memoir. Foundational for nuclear
  spaces, tensor products, and approximation properties.
* **Raymond A. Ryan, *Introduction to Tensor Products of Banach
  Spaces***. The readable modern account of the projective and
  injective tensor products.
* **Andreas Defant and Klaus Floret, *Tensor Norms and Operator
  Ideals***. The systematic reference for the Grothendieck programme.
* **Nicolas Bourbaki, *Topological Vector Spaces***. The structural
  reference for uniform, bornological, locally convex, and duality
  language.
* **Pedro Pérez Carreras and José Bonet, *Barrelled Locally Convex
  Spaces***. The specialist reference for barrelledness and its many
  relatives.
* **Jochen Wengenroth, *Derived Functors in Functional Analysis*** (2003,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/b80165), Lecture Notes in Mathematics).
  Homological methods for Fréchet and (LF)-spaces: the functor Proj¹,
  surjectivity of operators, and splitting theory.
* **Laurent Schwartz, *Théorie des distributions***. The original
  monograph; historically fundamental and still mathematically
  instructive.
* **I. M. Gel'fand and G. E. Shilov, *Generalized Functions***
  (multi-volume, with Graev and Vilenkin). A broad classical corpus on
  distributions, transforms, generalized eigenfunction expansions, and
  representation theory.
* **Robert S. Strichartz, *A Guide to Distribution Theory and Fourier
  Transforms***. A concise modern first route for analysts.
* **F. G. Friedlander and Mark Joshi, *Introduction to the Theory of
  Distributions***. Short, clean, and PDE-facing.
* **V. S. Vladimirov, *Methods of the Theory of Generalized
  Functions***. Compact, with emphasis on transforms and applications.
* **Dorothee D. Haroske and Hans Triebel, *Distributions, Sobolev
  Spaces, Elliptic Equations*** (2008, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/042)).
  A compact route from distributions to elliptic boundary-value
  problems.
* **Gerd Grubb, *Distributions and Operators*** (2009,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-84895-2), Graduate Texts in
  Mathematics). Distributions, Sobolev spaces, and pseudodifferential
  operators up to boundary problems. A bridge from distribution theory to
  microlocal methods.
* **J. F. Colombeau, *New Generalized Functions and Multiplication of
  Distributions***. For the nonlinear theory, where the standard
  framework breaks down.
* **Serge Lang, *Real and Functional Analysis***. Included here for
  its locally convex methods and Banach-valued calculus.
* **Joseph Diestel and J. J. Uhl, Jr., *Vector Measures***. The
  reference for Banach-valued integration.
* **John L. Kelley and Isaac Namioka, *Linear Topological Spaces***. The
  classical graduate treatment of locally convex spaces, duality, and the
  Krein–Milman and Mackey–Arens theorems.

### More recent works

* **M. Scott Osborne, *Locally Convex Spaces*** (2014,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-02045-7)). A modern graduate
  textbook on locally convex topology, duality, completeness, and nuclear
  spaces. A more recent course-length entry beside Köthe and Trèves.
* **Vladimir I. Bogachev and Oleg G. Smolyanov, *Topological Vector Spaces and
  Their Applications*** (2017,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-57117-1)). A large treatment of
  locally convex spaces, duality, generalized functions, and applications.
  Useful for analysts needing a modern reference beyond Banach-space theory.
* **Johannes J. Duistermaat and Johan A. C. Kolk, *Distributions: Theory
  and Applications*** (2010, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4675-2)). A substantial course from
  distributions through Fourier transforms and fundamental solutions.
* **Dorina Mitrea, *Distributions, Partial Differential Equations, and
  Harmonic Analysis*** (2nd ed., 2018, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-03296-8)). Distributions,
  Sobolev spaces, and layer potentials in one graduate text.
* **Gerrit van Dijk, *Distribution Theory: Convolution, Fourier
  Transform, and Laplace Transform*** (2013, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110298512)). A short,
  explicit account of the transform calculus.
* **C. Pérez-García and W. H. Schikhof, *Locally Convex Spaces over
  Non-Archimedean Valued Fields*** (2010, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511729959)). Locally convex and
  duality theory over valued fields such as the p-adics. Included as a
  substantial extension of familiar functional-analytic constructions to a
  different scalar setting.
* **Jan Pachl, *Uniform Spaces and Measures*** (2013,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-5058-0)). Uniform measures,
  compactification, convolution, and topological groups. A research monograph
  extending measure theory beyond the standard metric and locally compact
  frameworks.

---

## 17. Banach Space Geometry, Bases, and Operator Ideals

Bases and basic sequences, the classical sequence and function spaces,
local theory, type and cotype, operator ideals, and the nonlinear and
smooth geometry of Banach spaces. Albiac–Kalton, Lindenstrauss–Tzafriri,
and the Johnson–Lindenstrauss handbook are the standard references; the
more recent monographs treat differentiability, embeddings, the
descriptive theory, renormings, nonseparable spaces, and tensor products.

### Foundational works

* **Fernando Albiac and Nigel J. Kalton, *Topics in Banach Space
  Theory***. The standard modern graduate introduction to Banach-space
  geometry, bases, and classical sequence spaces.
* **J. Lindenstrauss and L. Tzafriri, *Classical Banach Spaces
  I–II***. The classic structural reference for sequence spaces,
  function spaces, local theory, and complemented subspaces.
* **Joseph Diestel, *Sequences and Series in Banach Spaces***. Weak
  convergence, unconditional convergence, basic sequences, and
  compactness, taught through the classical theorems.
* **Przemysław Wojtaszczyk, *Banach Spaces for Analysts***. Short,
  concrete, and oriented to users rather than specialists.
* **Daniel Li and Hervé Queffélec, *Introduction to Banach Spaces: Analysis
  and Probability*** (French original 2004; expanded English translation,
  2 volumes, 2018, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781316675762); [volume II](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781316677391)). A broad synthesis of
  Banach-space geometry, operators, and probabilistic methods. A substantial
  graduate-to-research continuation of a first functional-analysis course.
* **Ivan Singer, *Bases in Banach Spaces I–II***. The comprehensive
  reference on Schauder bases and basic sequences.
* **Gilles Pisier, *The Volume of Convex Bodies and Banach Space
  Geometry***. Local theory, type and cotype, volume ratios, and
  asymptotic convex geometry.
* **Gilles Pisier, *Factorization of Linear Operators and Geometry of
  Banach Spaces***. Operator ideals, factorization theorems, and the
  local viewpoint.
* **Nicole Tomczak-Jaegermann, *Banach–Mazur Distances and
  Finite-Dimensional Operator Ideals***. A central research monograph
  in local theory.
* **Vitali D. Milman and Gideon Schechtman, *Asymptotic Theory of
  Finite Dimensional Normed Spaces***. Concentration and the
  asymptotic geometry of high-dimensional convex bodies.
* **Michel Ledoux and Michel Talagrand, *Probability in Banach
  Spaces***. Probability as a tool for geometry, and geometry as a
  constraint on probability.
* **J. Diestel, H. Jarchow, and A. Tonge, *Absolutely Summing
  Operators***. The main reference for summing ideals and
  Grothendieck's theorem.
* **Albrecht Pietsch, *Operator Ideals***. The classical systematic
  treatment of ideals of linear operators.
* **Joe Diestel, Jan H. Fourie, and Johan Swart, *The Metric Theory of
  Tensor Products: Grothendieck's Résumé Revisited*** (2008, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/mbk/052)). Grothendieck's
  *Résumé* read in modern language: tensor norms, operator ideals, and
  Grothendieck's inequality.
* **William B. Johnson and Joram Lindenstrauss (eds.), *Handbook of
  the Geometry of Banach Spaces I–II***. A large research reference
  covering local theory, asymptotic geometry, nonlinear questions, and
  much that is otherwise scattered.
* **Yoav Benyamini and Joram Lindenstrauss, *Geometric Nonlinear
  Functional Analysis***. Lipschitz and uniform classification of
  Banach spaces; the standard reference for the nonlinear theory.
* **Robert Deville, Gilles Godefroy, and Václav Zizler, *Smoothness
  and Renormings in Banach Spaces***. Renorming theory, smooth
  variational principles, and their applications.
* **Petr Hájek, Vicente Montesinos Santalucía, Jon Vanderwerff, and Václav
  Zizler, *Biorthogonal Systems in Banach Spaces*** (2008,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-68915-9), CMS Books in
  Mathematics). Markushevich bases and the structure theory that replaces
  Schauder bases in nonseparable spaces.
* **Joseph Diestel and J. J. Uhl, Jr., *Vector Measures***. The
  Radon–Nikodým property and its geometric equivalents.
* **Spiros A. Argyros and Stevo Todorčević, *Ramsey Methods in
  Analysis*** (2005, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-7643-7360-1)). Ramsey theory as a construction
  method for Banach spaces.
* **Bernard Beauzamy, *Introduction to Banach Spaces and Their
  Geometry***. A problem-rich entry point, valuable as much for its
  examples as its theory.
* **Per Enflo's counterexamples and the surrounding literature**, best
  approached through Lindenstrauss–Tzafriri and Albiac–Kalton, remain
  the reference point for what can fail.
* **Mahlon M. Day, *Normed Linear Spaces***. The classical survey of
  Banach space geometry: convexity, smoothness, bases, and
  isomorphic invariants.
* **N. L. Carothers, *A Short Course on Banach Space Theory***. Classical
  sequence spaces, bases, and C(K) spaces at a first graduate level.
* **Mikhail I. Kadets and Vladimir M. Kadets, *Series in Banach Spaces:
  Conditional and Unconditional Convergence***. Riemann's rearrangement
  theorem in infinite dimensions and the Dvoretzky–Rogers theorem.
* **James H. Wells and Lynn R. Williams, *Embeddings and Extensions in
  Analysis***. Isometric embeddings into Hilbert and L^p spaces,
  Kirszbraun-type extension theorems, and negative-definite kernels.

### More recent works

* **Joram Lindenstrauss, David Preiss, and Jaroslav Tišer, *Fréchet
  Differentiability of Lipschitz Functions and Porous Sets in Banach
  Spaces*** (2012, [Princeton University Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400842698)). Listed in Section 5.
  The nonlinear geometry of Banach spaces, at research level.
* **Petr Hájek and Michal Johanis, *Smooth Analysis in Banach Spaces***
  (2014, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110258998)). Smoothness, polynomials, and higher-order
  differentiability.
* **Antonio J. Guirao, Vicente Montesinos, and Václav Zizler, *Renormings in
  Banach Spaces: A Toolbox*** (2022,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-08655-7)). Smooth and
  rotund renormings, nonseparable spaces, and the methods behind them. The
  update to Deville–Godefroy–Zizler.
* **Gilles Pisier, *Martingales in Banach Spaces*** (2016, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781316480588)). Type, cotype, UMD, and martingale inequalities as
  the probabilistic skeleton of the geometry.
* **Mikhail I. Ostrovskii, *Metric Embeddings: Bilipschitz and Coarse
  Embeddings into Banach Spaces*** (2013, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110264012)). The research
  monograph on embeddability.
* **Pandelis Dodos, *Banach Spaces and Descriptive Set Theory*** (2010,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-12153-1)). Listed in Section 3.
* **Antonio Avilés, Félix Cabello Sánchez, Jesús M. F. Castillo, Manuel
  González, and Yolanda Moreno, *Separably Injective Banach Spaces***
  (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-14741-3), Lecture Notes in Mathematics). A research monograph
  on a classical isomorphism problem.
* **Félix Cabello Sánchez and Jesús M. F. Castillo, *Homological Methods in
  Banach Space Theory*** (2023, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108778312)). Twisted sums, quasilinear
  maps, extension of operators, and exact sequences. A research synthesis of
  the homological viewpoint on Banach-space structure.
* **H. Garth Dales, Frederick K. Dashiell, Jr., Anthony To-Ming Lau, and Dona
  Strauss, *Banach Spaces of Continuous Functions as Dual Spaces*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-32349-7)). When spaces C(K) are
  dual spaces, their preduals, and the corresponding topological and
  measure-theoretic structure. A specialised research reference in
  Banach-space theory.
* **Silouanos Brazitikos, Apostolos Giannopoulos, Petros Valettas, and
  Beatrice-Helen Vritsiou, *Geometry of Isotropic Convex Bodies***
  (2014, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/196), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). The isotropic-constant problem and the analytic
  inequalities around it.
* **Guillaume Aubrun and Stanisław J. Szarek, *Alice and Bob Meet
  Banach*** (2017, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/223)). Listed in Section 13.
  Asymptotic convex geometry in its quantum-information form.
* **Christian Rosendal, *Coarse Geometry of Topological Groups*** (2021,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108903547)). Listed in Section 3. Coarse embeddings
  and the geometry of Polish groups.
* **Andreas Defant, Domingo García, Manuel Maestre, and Pablo Sevilla-Peris,
  *Dirichlet Series and Holomorphic Functions in High Dimensions*** (2019,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108691611)).
  Bohr's problem, Bohnenblust–Hille inequalities, and holomorphy on
  infinite-dimensional polydiscs. Banach-space theory meeting analytic
  number theory.

---

## 18. Vector-Valued Analysis and Analysis in Banach Spaces

A body of technique that did not exist as a unified subject when most
of the older references were written, and that now underpins maximal
regularity, stochastic analysis in Banach spaces, and much of modern
harmonic analysis. The three-volume treatise of Hytönen, van Neerven,
Veraar, and Weis is the central reference; Pisier's monograph supplies
the martingale inequalities, and the Denk–Hieber–Prüss memoir records
the multiplier method.

### Foundational works

* **Joseph Diestel and J. J. Uhl, Jr., *Vector Measures***. The
  measure-theoretic foundation.
* **Herbert Amann, *Linear and Quasilinear Parabolic
  Problems***. Where vector-valued function spaces become an
  indispensable PDE tool.
* **Alessandra Lunardi, *Interpolation Theory*** and ***Analytic
  Semigroups and Optimal Regularity in Parabolic Problems***. Real
  interpolation for vector-valued spaces, done carefully.
* **Wolfgang Arendt, Charles J. K. Batty, Matthias Hieber, and Frank
  Neubrander, *Vector-Valued Laplace Transforms and Cauchy
  Problems*** (2001; 2nd ed., 2011,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0087-7)). The
  Laplace-transform theory in the vector-valued setting, with
  asymptotics. The positive and evolution-equation continuations are in
  Section 24.
* **Markus Haase, *The Functional Calculus for Sectorial
  Operators***. The standard reference for holomorphic functional
  calculus, and the natural companion to Hytönen et al.
* **Robert Denk, Matthias Hieber, and Jan Prüss, *R-boundedness, Fourier
  Multipliers and Problems of Elliptic and Parabolic Type*** (2003,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/memo/0788),
  Memoirs). Operator-valued Fourier multipliers and maximal L^p-regularity
  for elliptic and parabolic boundary problems. The memoir that made the
  method a working tool.

### More recent works

* **Tuomas Hytönen, Jan van Neerven, Mark Veraar, and Lutz Weis,
  *Analysis in Banach Spaces I–III*** (*Martingales and
  Littlewood–Paley Theory*; *Probabilistic Methods and Operator
  Theory*; *Harmonic Analysis and Spectral Theory*) (2016, 2017, 2023,
  Springer). The definitive modern treatise: UMD spaces, R-boundedness,
  vector-valued singular integrals, H-infinity calculus, and maximal
  regularity.
* **Gilles Pisier, *Martingales in Banach Spaces*** (2016, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781316480588)). Listed in Section 17. The martingale inequalities
  that underlie UMD and vector-valued singular integrals.
* **Adam Osękowski, *Sharp Martingale and Semimartingale Inequalities***
  (2012, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0370-0)). Sharp
  constants in martingale inequalities, including inequalities central to
  Banach-valued probability. A specialist continuation of the Burkholder and
  Bellman-function techniques.

---

## 19. Banach Lattices, Positive Operators, and Ordered Structures

Meyer-Nieberg, Schaefer, and Aliprantis–Burkinshaw are the standard
references for Banach lattices and positive operators. More recent books
treat positive semigroups, nonlinear Perron–Frobenius theory, and cones
and duality.

### Foundational works

* **Peter Meyer-Nieberg, *Banach Lattices***. The standard structural
  monograph: order continuity, duality, and positive operators.
* **Helmut H. Schaefer, *Banach Lattices and Positive Operators***. A
  classical reference emphasizing spectral and semigroup aspects of
  positivity.
* **C. D. Aliprantis and O. Burkinshaw, *Positive Operators***. A
  large systematic treatment of lattice methods and positive linear
  maps.
* **C. D. Aliprantis and O. Burkinshaw, *Locally Solid Riesz Spaces
  with Applications to Economics***. The locally convex-ordered
  theory.
* **C. D. Aliprantis and K. C. Border, *Infinite Dimensional Analysis:
  A Hitchhiker's Guide***. Order, convexity, correspondences,
  measurable selection, and functional analysis; far more useful to
  analysts than its subtitle suggests.
* **W. A. J. Luxemburg and A. C. Zaanen, *Riesz Spaces I*** and
  **A. C. Zaanen, *Riesz Spaces II***. The classical large references
  for vector lattices, order duality, and positive operators.
* **A. C. Zaanen, *Introduction to Operator Theory in Riesz
  Spaces***. A more accessible route into the same material.
* **Helmut H. Schaefer, *Topological Vector Spaces***. Also the
  reference for ordered locally convex spaces and positive
  functionals.
* **Klaus-Jochen Engel and Rainer Nagel, *One-Parameter Semigroups for
  Linear Evolution Equations***, and **Rainer Nagel (ed.),
  *One-parameter Semigroups of Positive Operators***. Positivity as a
  tool in evolution equations.
* **Yuri A. Abramovich and Charalambos D. Aliprantis, *An Invitation
  to Operator Theory***. A modern text where order-theoretic methods
  sit alongside classical operator theory.
* **Charalambos D. Aliprantis and Rabee Tourky, *Cones and Duality*** (2007,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/084), Graduate
  Studies in Mathematics). Ordered vector spaces, cones, and duality, with
  lattice-subspaces and applications to economics.

### More recent works

* **András Bátkai, Marjeta Kramar Fijavž, and Abdelaziz Rhandi,
  *Positive Operator Semigroups: From Finite to Infinite Dimensions***
  (2017, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-42813-0)). A modern course on positive semigroups, from
  matrices to infinite-dimensional generators. Listed again in Section
  24.
* **Bas Lemmens and Roger Nussbaum, *Nonlinear Perron–Frobenius Theory***
  (2012, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139026079)). Order-preserving
  homogeneous maps, cone spectral theory, and projective metrics. A
  substantial monograph connecting positive-operator ideas with nonlinear
  dynamics and nonexpansive mappings.
* **Delio Mugnolo, *Semigroup Methods for Evolution Equations on
  Networks*** (2014, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-04621-1)). Listed in Section 24. Positivity on
  metric graphs.

---

## 20. Banach Algebras and Commutative Harmonic Analysis of Algebras

Bonsall–Duncan, Dales, Palmer, and Kaniuth are the standard references
for the structure theory of Banach algebras. More recent books treat
amenability in its later form, Lipschitz algebras, Arens regularity, and
stable ranks.

### Foundational works

* **Frank F. Bonsall and John Duncan, *Complete Normed
  Algebras***. The standard structural reference for Banach algebras,
  numerical range, and representation theory.
* **Charles E. Rickart, *General Theory of Banach Algebras***. The
  classical comprehensive treatment.
* **Theodore W. Gamelin, *Uniform Algebras***. The definitive book on
  uniform algebras, maximal ideal spaces, and the interaction with
  function theory.
* **Andrew Browder, *Introduction to Function Algebras***. A shorter
  route into the same material.
* **H. Garth Dales, *Banach Algebras and Automatic
  Continuity***. Enormous and definitive on homomorphisms,
  derivations, radicals, and automatic continuity.
* **Theodore W. Palmer, *Banach Algebras and the General Theory of
  *-Algebras I–II***. The systematic modern reference, and the place
  to check a general fact.
* **Lynn H. Loomis, *An Introduction to Abstract Harmonic
  Analysis***. The classical short bridge from Banach algebras to
  harmonic analysis on groups.
* **I. M. Gelfand, D. A. Raikov, and G. E. Shilov, *Commutative Normed
  Rings***. The founding monograph of the whole subject.
* **Ronald Larsen, *An Introduction to the Theory of
  Multipliers***. The standard reference for multiplier algebras and
  Fourier multipliers in the abstract setting.
* **Eberhard Kaniuth, *A Course in Commutative Banach Algebras***. A
  clean modern graduate text.
* **H. Garth Dales, Pietro Aiena, Jörg Eschmeier, Kjeld Laursen, and
  George A. Willis, *Introduction to Banach Algebras, Operators, and
  Harmonic Analysis*** (2003, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511615429)). A
  multi-author graduate bridge from Banach algebras to harmonic
  analysis, and still a useful single volume.
* **Jean-Paul Pier, *Amenable Locally Compact Groups***. The large
  reference on amenability of locally compact groups.
* **Volker Runde, *Lectures on Amenability***. A concise account of
  amenability, from groups through Banach algebras.
* **Alan L. T. Paterson, *Amenability***. A large reference on the
  same circle of ideas.
* **Ronald G. Douglas, *Banach Algebra Techniques in Operator
  Theory***. Banach-algebra methods for Toeplitz operators and
  Fredholm theory.

### More recent works

* **Graham R. Allan, *Introduction to Banach Spaces and Algebras***,
  edited by H. Garth Dales (2011, [Oxford University Press](https://global.oup.com/academic/product/introduction-to-banach-spaces-and-algebras-9780199206544)). Allan's
  graduate course, published posthumously: a clean entry to the
  structural theory.
* **Eberhard Kaniuth and Anthony To-Ming Lau, *Fourier and
  Fourier–Stieltjes Algebras on Locally Compact Groups*** (2018,
  American Mathematical Society). The modern reference for the algebras
  attached to nonabelian groups.
* **Volker Runde, *Amenable Banach Algebras: A Panorama*** (2020,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-0716-0351-2), Springer Monographs in Mathematics). A greatly expanded
  successor to Runde's *Lectures on Amenability*, now the working
  monograph.
* **H. Garth Dales and Ali Ülger, *Banach Function Algebras, Arens Regularity,
  and BSE Norms*** (2024,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-44532-3)). Semisimple Banach
  algebras, bidual multiplication, and Bochner–Schoenberg–Eberlein norms. A
  recent research synthesis with examples from harmonic analysis and function
  theory.
* **Nik Weaver, *Lipschitz Algebras*** (2nd ed., 2018, [World
  Scientific](https://doi.org/10.1142/9911)). Lipschitz algebras, de Leeuw maps, and the nonlinear
  functional analysis of metric spaces. The second edition is a
  substantial rewriting.
* **Raymond Mortini and Rudolf Rupp, *Extension Problems and Stable
  Ranks: A Space Odyssey*** (2021, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-73872-3)). A research treatise in
  two volumes, issued as one book, on the Bass and topological stable
  ranks, the corona problem, and extension of functions.
* **Pietro Aiena, *Fredholm and Local Spectral Theory II*** (2018,
