  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-02266-2)). Listed in Section 21.

---

## 21. Operator Theory: Bounded Operators, Spectra, and Model Theory

Douglas, Sz.-Nagy–Foiaş, Nikolski, and Agler–McCarthy–Young are central
references. More recent books are textbooks and monographs on model
spaces, de Branges–Rovnyak spaces, the invariant-subspace problem, local
spectral theory, and linear dynamics.

### Foundational works

* **Ronald G. Douglas, *Banach Algebra Techniques in Operator
  Theory***. Toeplitz operators, Fredholm theory, and C*-algebra
  methods in classical operator theory; still the most efficient entry
  point.
* **John B. Conway, *A Course in Operator Theory***. A natural
  continuation of the author's functional-analysis text into
  Hilbert-space operators.
* **Paul R. Halmos, *A Hilbert Space Problem Book***. Operator theory
  learned through problems; still one of the best ways to acquire the
  subject's instincts.
* **William Arveson, *A Short Course on Spectral Theory***. Concise,
  algebraic, and elegant.
* **Rubén A. Martínez-Avendaño and Peter Rosenthal, *An Introduction to
  Operators on the Hardy–Hilbert Space*** (2007,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-48578-2), Graduate Texts in
  Mathematics). Shift, Toeplitz, Hankel, and composition operators on H², at
  a first-year graduate level.
* **John B. Conway, *The Theory of Subnormal Operators***. The
  definitive treatment of a class where function theory and operator
  theory meet.
* **Israel Gohberg, Seymour Goldberg, and Marinus A. Kaashoek, *Basic
  Classes of Linear Operators*** and ***Classes of Linear Operators
  I–II***. Broad and concrete, with Fredholm, compact, and structured
  operator classes.
* **I. C. Gohberg and M. G. Krein, *Introduction to the Theory of
  Linear Nonselfadjoint Operators***. A classical research monograph
  on compact and nonselfadjoint operators, singular numbers, and
  triangular models.
* **Béla Sz.-Nagy, Ciprian Foiaş, Hari Bercovici, and László Kérchy,
  *Harmonic Analysis of Operators on Hilbert Space***. Dilation theory
  and the functional model for contractions; the central text of the
  subject.
* **Nikolai Nikolski, *Operators, Functions, and Systems: An Easy
  Reading*** (2 volumes: *Hardy, Hankel, and Toeplitz*; *Model
  Operators and Systems*). A broad modern synthesis of function
  spaces, model theory, interpolation, and control.
* **Nikolai Nikolski, *Treatise on the Shift Operator***. The earlier
  and more concentrated account of the shift and its invariant
  subspaces.
* **Marvin Rosenblum and James Rovnyak, *Hardy Classes and Operator
  Theory***. The function-theoretic side of contraction and Toeplitz
  theory.
* **Albrecht Böttcher and Bernd Silbermann, *Analysis of Toeplitz
  Operators***. The large reference for Toeplitz and Wiener–Hopf
  operators, symbols, and asymptotics of determinants.
* **Kehe Zhu, *Operator Theory in Function Spaces***. Composition,
  Toeplitz, and Hankel operators on Bergman and related spaces.
* **Heydar Radjavi and Peter Rosenthal, *Invariant Subspaces***. The
  classical reference for the invariant subspace problem and
  reducibility.
* **Kjeld B. Laursen and Michael M. Neumann, *An Introduction to Local
  Spectral Theory*** (2000, [Oxford University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/oso/9780198523819.001.0001)). Decomposable
  operators, the single-valued extension property, and Bishop's property
  (β). The standard reference before Aiena's volumes.
* **Vladimir Müller, *Spectral Theory of Linear Operators and Spectral
  Systems in Banach Algebras*** (2nd ed., 2007,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8265-0)). Spectral
  systems, the Taylor spectrum, essential spectra, and the joint spectral
  theory of commuting tuples.
* **Frédéric Bayart and Étienne Matheron, *Dynamics of Linear Operators***
  (2009, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511581113)). Hypercyclicity,
  frequent hypercyclicity, and the ergodic theory of linear operators. One
  of the two founding monographs of linear dynamics.
* **János Bognár, *Indefinite Inner Product Spaces***. The standard
  reference on Krein and Pontryagin spaces and operators on them.
* **Joseph A. Ball, Israel Gohberg, and Leiba Rodman, *Interpolation of
  Rational Matrix Functions***. Nevanlinna–Pick and related interpolation
  problems for matrix functions, via state-space realizations.

### More recent works

#### Courses and textbooks

* **Barry Simon, *Operator Theory*** (Part 4 of *A Comprehensive
  Course in Analysis*) (2015, American Mathematical Society). A modern
  graduate-to-research survey with wide coverage.
* **Stephan Ramon Garcia, Javad Mashreghi, and William T. Ross,
  *Operator Theory by Example*** (2023, [Oxford University Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/oso/9780192863867.001.0001)). A
  graduate course in which the examples do the teaching: shifts,
  Toeplitz operators, and model spaces.
* **Piotr Sołtan, *A Primer on Hilbert Space Operators*** (2018,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-92061-0)). A short course on
  operator topologies, spectral theory, compact operators, and basic
  C\*-algebras. Useful as preparation for the larger operator-algebra texts.
* **V. S. Sunder, *Operators on Hilbert Space*** (2016 Springer edition; first
  published by Hindustan Book Agency in 2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-981-10-1816-9)). A concise graduate
  treatment of Hilbert-space operators, spectral theory, and operator
  algebras. Offers a short route to the subject's structural results.
* **Carlos S. Kubrusly, *Spectral Theory of Operators on Hilbert
  Spaces*** (2012, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-8328-3)). A compact spectral-theory course for
  bounded and unbounded operators.
* **Carlos S. Kubrusly, *Spectral Theory of Bounded Linear Operators*** (2020,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-33149-8)). Spectral sets,
  decompositions, and the spectral theory of bounded operators on Banach and
  Hilbert spaces. Distinct from the author's Hilbert-space spectral text
  listed above.
* **Milivoje Lukić, *A First Course in Spectral Theory*** (2022,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/226)). Listed in full in Section 22.

#### Function-theoretic operator theory and model spaces

* **Jim Agler, John E. McCarthy, and Nicholas Young, *Operator
  Analysis: Hilbert Space Methods in Complex Analysis*** (2020,
  Cambridge University Press). Reproducing kernels, interpolation, and
  several-variable operator theory.
* **Vern I. Paulsen and Mrinal Raghupathi, *An Introduction to the
  Theory of Reproducing Kernel Hilbert Spaces*** (2016, Cambridge
  University Press). The modern standard on RKHS, now used far outside
  operator theory.
* **Nikolai Nikolski, *Toeplitz Matrices and Operators*** (2020, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108182577)). A graduate
  account of Toeplitz operators, symbols, and finite-section questions. A
  shorter and more recent entrance than the Böttcher–Silbermann treatise.
* **Emmanuel Fricain and Javad Mashreghi, *The Theory of H(b) Spaces***
  (2 volumes, 2016, Cambridge University Press; [I](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139226752), [II](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139226769)). The research treatise
  on de Branges–Rovnyak spaces.
* **Isabelle Chalendar and Jonathan R. Partington, *Modern Approaches to
  the Invariant Subspace Problem*** (2011, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511862434)).
  Universal operators, functional models, and what is known about
  invariant subspaces.
* **Alexei Poltoratski, *Toeplitz Approach to Problems of the
  Uncertainty Principle*** (2015, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cbms/121), CBMS).
  The Toeplitz form of the Beurling–Malliavin theory. Listed again in
  Section 41.
* **Kehe Zhu (ed.), *Handbook of Analytic Operator Theory*** (2019, [CRC
  Press](https://www.routledge.com/Handbook-of-Analytic-Operator-Theory/Zhu/p/book/9781138486416)). A survey handbook of operators on analytic function spaces.

#### Spectral theory, Fredholm theory, and linear dynamics

* **Pietro Aiena, *Fredholm and Local Spectral Theory II: Perturbation
  Theory and Topological Uniform Descent*** (2018, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-02266-2)). The
  continuation of Aiena's local spectral theory, on descent and
  perturbation.
* **Aref Jeribi, *Spectral Theory and Applications of Linear Operators and
  Block Operator Matrices*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-17566-9)). Essential spectra,
  Fredholm methods, perturbations, and block operator matrices. Useful for
  spectral problems that do not reduce to a single self-adjoint operator.
* **Marinus A. Kaashoek and Sjoerd M. Verduyn Lunel, *Completeness Theorems
  and Characteristic Matrix Functions: Applications to Integral and
  Differential Operators*** (2022,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-04508-0)). Completeness of
  generalized eigenvectors and characteristic matrix functions for integral
  and differential operators. A research synthesis joining operator theory
  with functional differential equations.
* **Karl-G. Grosse-Erdmann and Alfredo Peris Manguillot, *Linear Chaos***
  (2011, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-2170-1),
  Universitext). The other founding monograph of linear dynamics, written as
  a graduate text with exercises.

---

## 22. Unbounded Operators, Spectral Theory, and Mathematical Physics

Kato, Reed–Simon, Schmüdgen, and Teschl are the working references for
unbounded operators and their spectral theory. More recent books are new
graduate courses, monographs on random and ergodic operators, the theory
of boundary triplets and Lieb–Thirring inequalities, mathematical quantum
field theory, and inverse problems.

### Foundational works

* **Tosio Kato, *Perturbation Theory for Linear Operators***. The
  fundamental monograph on closed operators, analytic perturbation,
  quadratic forms, and spectral stability; still consulted daily.
* **Michael Reed and Barry Simon, *Methods of Modern Mathematical
  Physics I–IV***. The working corpus for self-adjointness, Fourier
  analysis, scattering, and spectral analysis of Schrödinger
  operators.
* **Joachim Weidmann, *Linear Operators in Hilbert Spaces*** and
  ***Spectral Theory of Ordinary Differential Operators***. Detailed
  and reliable on unbounded operators and Sturm–Liouville theory.
* **E. Brian Davies, *Spectral Theory and Differential Operators***. A
  readable bridge from functional analysis to elliptic and Schrödinger
  spectral theory.
* **E. Brian Davies, *Linear Operators and Their Spectra***. Broad
  modern operator theory including nonselfadjoint phenomena,
  pseudospectra, and semigroups.
* **Gerald Teschl, *Mathematical Methods in Quantum Mechanics***. A
  modern rigorous introduction with Schrödinger operators as the
  central application; freely available and well suited to self-study.
* **Peter D. Hislop and Israel M. Sigal, *Introduction to Spectral
  Theory***. Spectral analysis with applications to Schrödinger
  operators and scattering.
* **Stephen J. Gustafson and Israel Michael Sigal, *Mathematical Concepts of
  Quantum Mechanics*** (first ed. 2003; 3rd ed., 2020,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-59562-3), Universitext).
  Spectral theory, scattering, path integrals, and many-body and
  quantum-field topics, at a graduate level.
* **Leon A. Takhtajan, *Quantum Mechanics for Mathematicians*** (2008,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/095), Graduate
  Studies in Mathematics). Classical mechanics, quantization, spectral
  theory, and path integrals, written for mathematicians.
* **M. Sh. Birman and M. Z. Solomyak, *Spectral Theory of Self-Adjoint
  Operators in Hilbert Space***. A research-level reference for
  perturbation and eigenvalue estimates.
* **Hans Cycon, Richard Froese, Werner Kirsch, and Barry Simon,
  *Schrödinger Operators***. The standard monograph on spectral and
  scattering theory, including many-body and random cases.
* **Barry Simon, *Trace Ideals and Their Applications***. Schatten
  classes, determinants, and trace formulas.
* **Lloyd N. Trefethen and Mark Embree, *Spectra and
  Pseudospectra***. The standard reference for nonnormal operators and
  the failure of spectral intuition.
* **Michael Demuth and Maddaly Krishna, *Determining Spectra in
  Quantum Theory***. A compact account of spectral classification.
* **Gerald B. Folland, *Quantum Field Theory: A Tourist Guide for
  Mathematicians*** (2008, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/149), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). Perturbative quantum field theory and renormalisation
  explained in mathematical language.
* **Vladimir A. Marchenko, *Sturm–Liouville Operators and
  Applications***. Inverse spectral theory of one-dimensional
  Sturm–Liouville operators.
* **B. M. Levitan and I. S. Sargsjan, *Introduction to Spectral
  Theory***. Spectral theory of self-adjoint ordinary differential
  operators, including the inverse problem.
* **E. C. Titchmarsh, *Eigenfunction Expansions Associated with
  Second-Order Differential Equations I–II***. The classical analytic
  treatment, still valuable.
* **Anton Zettl, *Sturm–Liouville Theory***. The modern systematic
  reference.
* **David Borthwick, *Spectral Theory of Infinite-Area Hyperbolic
  Surfaces*** (first ed. 2007; 2nd ed., 2016, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-33877-4)). Resonances and scattering on
  hyperbolic surfaces. Listed again in Section 53.
* **Victor Isakov, *Inverse Problems for Partial Differential
  Equations*** (first ed. 1998; 3rd ed., 2017, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-51658-5)). The research reference for
  uniqueness and stability in inverse boundary problems.
* **Andreas Kirsch, *An Introduction to the Mathematical Theory of
  Inverse Problems*** (first ed. 1996; 3rd ed., 2021, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-63343-1)). A graduate course in
  regularisation and inverse eigenvalue and scattering problems.
* **Werner O. Amrein, Anne Boutet de Monvel, and Vladimir Georgescu,
  *C₀-Groups, Commutator Methods and Spectral Theory of N-Body
  Hamiltonians***. Mourre theory and its applications. The standard
  reference for commutator methods.
* **Hellmut Baumgärtel and Manfred Wollenberg, *Mathematical Scattering
  Theory***. Wave operators, completeness, and the abstract theory of
  scattering for pairs of self-adjoint operators.
* **F. A. Berezin and M. A. Shubin, *The Schrödinger Equation***. Spectral
  theory, scattering, and quasiclassical asymptotics for Schrödinger
  operators.
* **Barry Simon, *Quantum Mechanics for Hamiltonians Defined as Quadratic
  Forms***. Quadratic-form methods for singular potentials; the source of
  the form approach to Schrödinger operators.
* **Bernd Thaller, *The Dirac Equation***. Self-adjointness, spectral
  theory, and scattering for the Dirac operator.
* **Jiří Blank, Pavel Exner, and Miloslav Havlíček, *Hilbert Space
  Operators in Quantum Physics***. A full course in operator theory with
  its quantum-mechanical applications.
* **Khosrow Chadan and Pierre C. Sabatier, *Inverse Problems in Quantum
  Scattering Theory***. Gel'fand–Levitan and Marchenko theory and its
  extensions.
* **Peter Kuchment, *Floquet Theory for Partial Differential
  Equations***. Periodic operators, Bloch waves, and the analytic Fredholm
  theory behind band structure.
* **Jean Bourgain, *Green's Function Estimates for Lattice Schrödinger
  Operators and Applications***. Multiscale analysis for quasi-periodic
  operators and its applications to nonlinear Hamiltonian PDE.

### More recent works

#### Courses and spectral theory

* **Konrad Schmüdgen, *Unbounded Self-adjoint Operators on Hilbert
  Space*** (2012, Springer). The modern systematic reference, careful
  about the issues older texts skate over.
* **Milivoje Lukić, *A First Course in Spectral Theory*** (2022,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/226), Graduate Studies in Mathematics).
  Self-adjoint operators through spectral measures and Herglotz
  functions, then Jacobi and one-dimensional Schrödinger operators.
* **Bernard Helffer, *Spectral Theory and Its Applications*** (2013,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139505727)). A graduate course from the spectral
  theorem to semiclassical and discrete spectra.
* **David Borthwick, *Spectral Theory: Basic Concepts and Applications***
  (2020, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-38002-1)). A graduate
  introduction to unbounded operators with applications to Laplacians and
  Schrödinger operators. Distinct from the author's hyperbolic-surface
  monograph listed above.
* **Brian C. Hall, *Quantum Theory for Mathematicians*** (2013,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-7116-5), Graduate Texts in Mathematics). Quantum mechanics from
  unbounded operators, the Fourier transform, and the spectral theorem,
  written as a mathematics course.
* **Valter Moretti, *Spectral Theory and Quantum Mechanics*** (2nd ed.,
  2017, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-70706-8)). A long, careful course on unbounded operators and the
  foundations of quantum mechanics.
* **Klaas Landsman, *Foundations of Quantum Theory: From Classical
  Concepts to Operator Algebras*** (2017, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-51777-3)). Operators,
  C\*-algebras, and the conceptual structure, freely available.
* **David E. Edmunds and W. Desmond Evans, *Elliptic Differential Operators
  and Spectral Analysis*** (2018,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-02125-2)). Spectral estimates
  and elliptic operators, with attention to the underlying functional
  analysis. A later specialised work beyond their classical *Spectral Theory
  and Differential Operators*.
* **Jussi Behrndt, Seppo Hassi, and Henk de Snoo, *Boundary Value Problems,
  Weyl Functions, and Differential Operators*** (2020,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-36714-5)). Boundary
  triplets, Weyl functions, and self-adjoint extensions, applied to
  Sturm–Liouville operators, canonical systems, and Schrödinger operators.
  Open access.
* **Fritz Gesztesy, Roger Nichols, and Maxim Zinchenko, *Sturm–Liouville
  Operators, Their Spectral Theory, and Some Applications*** (2024, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://www.ams.org/books/coll/067/)). Weyl–Titchmarsh
  theory, self-adjoint realizations, oscillation, and singular Sturm–Liouville
  problems. A comprehensive modern research reference for one-dimensional
  differential operators.
* **D. R. Yafaev, *Mathematical Scattering Theory: Analytic Theory*** (2010,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/158),
  Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). Scattering for differential
  operators: high- and low-energy asymptotics, long-range potentials, and
  the scattering matrix. The companion to his 1992 *General Theory*.
* **Johannes Sjöstrand, *Non-Self-Adjoint Differential Operators, Spectral
  Asymptotics and Random Perturbations*** (2019,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-10819-9)). Pseudospectra,
  semiclassical asymptotics, and random perturbations of non-self-adjoint
  operators. A research continuation beyond the classical self-adjoint
  spectral theory.

#### Schrödinger operators, random and ergodic operators, and quantum graphs

* **David Damanik and Jake Fillman, *One-Dimensional Ergodic
  Schrödinger Operators***, volume I, *General Theory* (2022) and
  volume II, *Specific Classes* (2024, American Mathematical Society; [I](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/221), [II](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/249),
  Graduate Studies in Mathematics). The current graduate treatise on
  ergodic discrete Schrödinger operators.
* **Michael Aizenman and Simone Warzel, *Random Operators: Disorder
  Effects on Quantum Spectra and Dynamics*** (2015, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/168), Graduate Studies in Mathematics). Anderson
  localisation, fractality, and the spectral theory of random
  operators.
* **Rupert L. Frank, Ari Laptev, and Timo Weidl, *Schrödinger Operators:
  Eigenvalues and Lieb–Thirring Inequalities*** (2023, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009218436)). Weyl asymptotics, the
  Cwikel–Lieb–Rozenblum and Lieb–Thirring inequalities, and sharp constants.
  The definitive treatment of eigenvalue bounds for Schrödinger operators.
* **Elliott H. Lieb and Robert Seiringer, *The Stability of Matter in
  Quantum Mechanics*** (2010, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511819681)). Listed in
  Section 11.
* **Søren Fournais and Bernard Helffer, *Spectral Methods in Surface
  Superconductivity*** (2010,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4797-1)). Magnetic
  Schrödinger operators and Ginzburg–Landau problems studied through
  semiclassical spectral estimates. A research monograph connecting operator
  asymptotics with nonlinear variational theory.
* **Pavel Exner and Hynek Kovařík, *Quantum Waveguides*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-18576-7)). Spectral and
  scattering theory of waveguides, geometric effects, and bound states. A
  substantial reference for the influence of domain geometry on quantum
  spectra.
* **Pavel Exner and others on quantum graphs; Gregory Berkolaiko and
  Peter Kuchment, *Introduction to Quantum Graphs*** (2013, American
  Mathematical Society). A now-standard setting for spectral analysis.
  Later graph monographs are **Olaf Post, *Spectral Analysis on
  Graph-like Spaces*** (2012, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-23840-6), Lecture Notes in Mathematics)
  and **Delio Mugnolo, *Semigroup Methods for Evolution Equations on
  Networks*** (2014, Springer; listed in Section 24).

#### Quantum fields and many-body dynamics

* **Michel Talagrand, *What Is a Quantum Field Theory? A First
  Introduction for Mathematicians*** (2022, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108225144)).
  A mathematician's path into QFT, long, explicit, and written without
  assuming the physics curriculum.
* **Jan Dereziński and Christian Gérard, *Mathematics of Quantization and
  Quantum Fields*** (2013; open-access reissue, 2023, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/mathematics-of-quantization-and-quantum-fields/BC8E26AA1E83767EAFAEDE7ACDCB4FBE)).
  Fock spaces, canonical commutation and anticommutation relations, and the
  operator theory of quantization. A systematic mathematical treatment of free
  fields and selected interacting-field methods.
* **Roland Bauerschmidt, David C. Brydges, and Gordon Slade,
  *Introduction to a Renormalisation Group Method*** (2019, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-981-32-9593-3),
  Lecture Notes in Mathematics). The Brydges–Kennedy renormalisation
  group, in a form analysts can read.
* **Niels Benedikter, Marcello Porta, and Benjamin Schlein, *Effective
  Evolution Equations from Quantum Dynamics*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-24898-1)). Derivation of
  mean-field and related effective equations from many-body quantum evolution.
  A compact research introduction to the estimates behind these limits.
* **Monique Combescure and Didier Robert, *Coherent States and
  Applications in Mathematical Physics*** (2nd ed., 2021, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-70845-0)).
  Coherent states, the metaplectic representation, and semiclassical
  estimates.

#### Inverse problems and scattering

* **Fioralba Cakoni and David Colton, *A Qualitative Approach to Inverse
  Scattering Theory*** (2014, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-8827-9)). The factorization and linear
  sampling methods.
* **Joel Feldman, Mikko Salo, and Gunther Uhlmann, *The Calderón
  Problem: An Introduction*** (2025, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/253),
  Graduate Studies in Mathematics). A graduate introduction to the
  inverse conductivity problem.
* **Gabriel P. Paternain, Mikko Salo, and Gunther Uhlmann, *Geometric
  Inverse Problems, with Emphasis on Two Dimensions*** (2023, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009039901)). Geodesic X-ray transforms, boundary rigidity, and
  the Calderón problem in two dimensions.
* **Andreas Kirsch and Frank Hettlich, *The Mathematical Theory of
  Time-Harmonic Maxwell's Equations: Expansion-, Integral-, and Variational
  Methods*** (2015, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-11086-8)).
  Maxwell boundary and scattering problems through integral equations,
  variational formulations, and spectral expansions. A rigorous analytic
  companion to inverse-scattering texts.

---

## 23. Operator Algebras, Operator Spaces, Free Probability, and Noncommutative Geometry

Kadison–Ringrose, Takesaki, Brown–Ozawa, Pisier, and Mingo–Speicher are
among the standard references. More recent books explain the
classification programme, higher index theory, the set theory of
C\*-algebras, groupoids, quantum groups, free probability, and
noncommutative geometry.

### Foundational works

* **Gerard J. Murphy, *C*-Algebras and Operator Theory***. The most
  direct graduate route from functional analysis into C*-algebras.
* **William Arveson, *An Invitation to C*-Algebras***. Elegant,
  influential, and strongly operator-theoretic.
* **Kenneth R. Davidson, *C*-Algebras by Example***. Concrete
  structure theory organized around illuminating classes of examples.
* **Richard V. Kadison and John R. Ringrose, *Fundamentals of the
  Theory of Operator Algebras I–IV***. The large standard reference,
  with two volumes of exercises that constitute a course in
  themselves.
* **Masamichi Takesaki, *Theory of Operator Algebras I–III***. The
  definitive research treatise on von Neumann algebras, modular
  theory, and crossed products.
* **Jacques Dixmier, *C*-Algebras*** and ***Von Neumann
  Algebras***. Classical references, historically decisive for
  representation theory and Type I structure.
* **Bruce Blackadar, *Operator Algebras: Theory of C*-Algebras and von
  Neumann Algebras***. Broad, structural, and the best single
  orientation to the modern classification programme.
* **Şerban Strătilă and László Zsidó, *Lectures on von Neumann
  Algebras***. The standard alternative when Takesaki's treatment is too
  compressed.
* **Allan M. Sinclair and Roger R. Smith, *Finite von Neumann Algebras and
  Masas*** (2008, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511666230)). II₁ factors, maximal
  abelian subalgebras, and perturbation and singularity results.
* **Bruce Blackadar, *K-Theory for Operator Algebras***. The standard
  reference for the K-theoretic machinery.
* **Mikael Rørdam, Flemming Larsen, and Niels Jakob Laustsen, *An
  Introduction to K-Theory for C\*-Algebras*** (2000, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511623806)). The short textbook companion to Blackadar.
* **Dana P. Williams, *Crossed Products of C\*-Algebras*** (2007,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/134)). The standard graduate reference for
  crossed products.
* **Iain Raeburn, *Graph Algebras*** (2005, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cbms/103), CBMS). The short introduction to graph C\*-algebras.
* **Mikael Rørdam and Erling Størmer, *Classification of Nuclear
  C\*-Algebras. Entropy in Operator Algebras*** (2002,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-662-04825-2), Encyclopaedia of
  Mathematical Sciences). Rørdam's account of the Elliott programme through
  Kirchberg–Phillips, and Størmer's survey of noncommutative entropy.
* **Ola Bratteli and Derek W. Robinson, *Operator Algebras and Quantum
  Statistical Mechanics I–II***. C*-dynamical systems, KMS states, and
  the operator-algebraic foundations of statistical mechanics.
* **Nathanial P. Brown and Narutaka Ozawa, *C*-Algebras and
  Finite-Dimensional Approximations***. The modern reference for
  nuclearity, exactness, amenability, and approximation properties.
* **Vaughan F. R. Jones and Vlada Sunder, *Introduction to
  Subfactors***. A concise introduction to subfactor theory.
* **David E. Evans and Yasuyuki Kawahigashi, *Quantum Symmetries on
  Operator Algebras***. Subfactors and their links to statistical
  mechanics and conformal field theory.
* **Thomas Timmermann, *An Invitation to Quantum Groups and Duality***
  (2008, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/043)). Hopf
  algebras, compact and locally compact quantum groups, and multiplicative
  unitaries.
* **Gilles Pisier, *Introduction to Operator Space Theory***. The
  standard entry to operator spaces and completely bounded maps.
* **Vern I. Paulsen, *Completely Bounded Maps and Operator
  Algebras***. The readable complement to Pisier, strong on dilation
  theory.
* **Edward G. Effros and Zhong-Jin Ruan, *Operator Spaces***. The
  other standard reference, with a more structural emphasis.
* **David P. Blecher and Christian Le Merdy, *Operator Algebras and
  Their Modules: An Operator Space Approach*** (2004, [Oxford University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/acprof:oso/9780198526599.001.0001)). Operator modules and the Haagerup tensor product. The
  standard monograph.
* **Alexandru Nica and Roland Speicher, *Lectures on the Combinatorics
  of Free Probability***. The standard combinatorial introduction to
  freeness and free cumulants.
* **Dan Voiculescu, Ken Dykema, and Alexandru Nica, *Free Random
  Variables***. The founding monograph.
* **Alain Connes, *Noncommutative Geometry***. The founding text;
  difficult, idiosyncratic, and still the reference for the programme
  as a whole.
* **José M. Gracia-Bondía, Joseph C. Várilly, and Héctor Figueroa,
  *Elements of Noncommutative Geometry***. A more systematic route
  into spectral triples and the index-theoretic machinery.
* **Masoud Khalkhali, *Basic Noncommutative Geometry*** (first ed. 2009;
  2nd ed., 2013, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/128)). A graduate introduction to spectral
  triples and Hopf algebras, shorter than Connes.
* **Alain Connes and Matilde Marcolli, *Noncommutative Geometry,
  Quantum Fields and Motives*** (2008, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/coll/055)).
  The long research monograph joining noncommutative geometry with
  quantum field theory and motives.
* **Nigel Higson and John Roe, *Analytic K-Homology*** (2000, [Oxford
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/oso/9780198511762.001.0001)). The standard textbook for analytic K-homology and
  the index pairing. Still the book.
* **John Roe, *Lectures on Coarse Geometry*** (2003, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/ulect/031)). The short geometric introduction that higher
  index theory assumes.
* **Shôichirô Sakai, *C\*-Algebras and W\*-Algebras***. The classical
  treatise on the abstract theory of C\*- and W\*-algebras.
* **Paul-André Meyer, *Quantum Probability for Probabilists***. Fock
  spaces, quantum stochastic calculus, and their relation to classical
  stochastic integration.

### More recent works

#### C\*-algebras, classification, and dynamics

* **Karen R. Strung, *An Introduction to C\*-algebras and the
  Classification Program***, edited by Francesc Perera (2021,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-47465-2), Advanced Courses in Mathematics CRM Barcelona). The
  graduate route from the basic theory to the Elliott invariant and the
  Jiang–Su algebra.
* **Ilijas Farah, *Combinatorial Set Theory of C\*-algebras*** (2019,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-27093-3)). Forcing, ultrapowers, and the set-theoretic rigidity and
  non-rigidity of C\*-algebras.
* **Ian F. Putnam, *Cantor Minimal Systems*** (2018, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/ulect/070), University Lecture Series). Bratteli diagrams,
  dimension groups, and the C\*-algebras of Cantor minimal systems.
* **Ruy Exel, *Partial Dynamical Systems, Fell Bundles and Applications***
  (2017, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/224),
  Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). Partial actions, their crossed
  products, and applications to graph and semigroup C\*-algebras.
* **Dana P. Williams, *A Tool Kit for Groupoid C\*-Algebras*** (2019,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://www.ams.org/books/surv/241/)).
  Locally compact groupoids, their C\*-algebras, disintegration, and
  equivalence. A graduate-to-research reference for the analytic machinery
  behind groupoid constructions.
* **Joachim Cuntz, Siegfried Echterhoff, Xin Li, and Guoliang Yu,
  *K-Theory for Group C\*-Algebras and Semigroup C\*-Algebras*** (2017,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-59915-1), Oberwolfach Seminars). Crossed products, Baum–Connes, and
  semigroup C\*-algebras, in lecture form.

#### Operator spaces, tensor products, and free probability

* **Gilles Pisier, *Tensor Products of C*-Algebras and Operator
  Spaces*** (2020, Cambridge University Press). The modern account of
  the Connes embedding circle of problems.
* **A. Ya. Helemskii, *Quantum Functional Analysis: Non-Coordinate
  Approach*** (2010, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/ulect/056), University Lecture Series).
  Operator spaces and their tensor products, developed without matrix
  coordinates.
* **James A. Mingo and Roland Speicher, *Free Probability and Random
  Matrices*** (2017, Springer). The bridge between free probability and
  asymptotic random matrix theory.
* **Dan-Virgil Voiculescu, Nicolai Stammeier, and Moritz Weber (eds.), *Free
  Probability and Operator Algebras*** (2016, [European Mathematical
  Society](https://ems.press/books/mlm/133)). Lecture courses on free
  probability and its operator-algebraic applications. A compact collection
  for moving from the combinatorial foundations to contemporary research
  questions.

#### Index theory, quantum symmetry, and noncommutative geometry

* **Rufus Willett and Guoliang Yu, *Higher Index Theory*** (2020,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108867351)). Coarse geometry, assembly maps, and the
  higher index, written as a graduate monograph.
* **Pavel Etingof, Shlomo Gelaki, Dmitri Nikshych, and Victor Ostrik,
  *Tensor Categories*** (2015, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/205),
  Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). The algebraic companion to
  subfactor theory and quantum symmetry.
* **Sergey Neshveyev and Lars Tuset, *Compact Quantum Groups and Their
  Representation Categories*** (2013, [Société Mathématique de
  France](https://smf.emath.fr/publications/compact-quantum-groups-and-their-representation-categories)).
  Compact quantum groups, unitary representations, and tensor categories. A
  concise specialist account joining operator-algebraic and categorical
  approaches to quantum symmetry.
* **Walter D. van Suijlekom, *Noncommutative Geometry and Particle
  Physics*** (2015, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-94-017-9162-5)). Spectral triples as particle-physics
  models, with the analysis kept visible.
* **Edwin J. Beggs and Shahn Majid, *Quantum Riemannian Geometry*** (2020,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30294-8)). Connections,
  metrics, curvature, and differential calculus on noncommutative spaces. A
  substantial research treatise complementing spectral-triple approaches to
  noncommutative geometry.

---

## 24. Semigroups, Evolution Equations, and Functional Calculus

Strongly continuous and analytic semigroups, abstract evolution equations,
maximal regularity, and the functional calculus of sectorial operators. The
recent books add positive semigroups, control and infinite-dimensional
systems, heat equations on domains, and nonlinear monotone evolution.

### Foundational works

* **Amnon Pazy, *Semigroups of Linear Operators and Applications to
  Partial Differential Equations***. The standard first graduate book
  on C0-semigroups for PDE.
* **Klaus-Jochen Engel and Rainer Nagel, *One-Parameter Semigroups for
  Linear Evolution Equations***. The comprehensive modern treatment of
  generation, spectral theory, positivity, and asymptotics, with an
  excellent shorter companion volume.
* **Klaus-Jochen Engel and Rainer Nagel, *A Short Course on Operator
  Semigroups*** (2006, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/0-387-36619-9),
  Universitext). The condensed version of their large treatise, suitable for
  a one-semester course.
* **Einar Hille and Ralph S. Phillips, *Functional Analysis and
  Semi-Groups***. The foundational classical treatise.
* **E. Brian Davies, *One-Parameter Semigroups***. A concise analytic
  account, effective for operator theorists.
* **Jerome A. Goldstein, *Semigroups of Linear Operators and
  Applications***. A broad classical treatment with PDE applications
  and unusually good notes.
* **Wolfgang Arendt, Charles J. K. Batty, Matthias Hieber, and Frank
  Neubrander, *Vector-Valued Laplace Transforms and Cauchy
  Problems***. Laplace-transform methods, generation theorems, and
  asymptotic behaviour.
* **El Maati Ouhabaz, *Analysis of Heat Equations on Domains*** (2005,
  [Princeton University Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400826483),
  London Mathematical Society Monographs). Sesquilinear forms, criteria for
  positivity and L^∞-contractivity, and Gaussian bounds for heat kernels of
  elliptic operators.
* **Herbert Amann, *Linear and Quasilinear Parabolic
  Problems***. Abstract parabolic evolution equations and
  interpolation at research level.
* **Alessandra Lunardi, *Analytic Semigroups and Optimal Regularity in
  Parabolic Problems***. Analytic semigroups, interpolation, Hölder
  spaces, and maximal regularity.
* **Jan Prüss, *Evolutionary Integral Equations and
  Applications***. Volterra equations, resolvents, and abstract
  evolution problems.
* **Markus Haase, *The Functional Calculus for Sectorial
  Operators***. The standard reference for sectorial operators and
  holomorphic functional calculus.
* **Marius Tucsnak and George Weiss, *Observation and Control for
  Operator Semigroups*** (2009, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8994-9)). Admissibility, observation,
  and exact controllability for semigroup systems.
* **Jean-Michel Coron, *Control and Nonlinearity*** (2007, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/136), Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). Return
  method, controllability of nonlinear PDE, and the analysis behind it.
* **Hiroki Tanabe, *Equations of Evolution***. A classical
  Japanese-school treatment, still useful for non-autonomous problems.
* **Odo Diekmann, Stephan A. van Gils, Sjoerd M. Verduyn Lunel, and
  Hans-Otto Walther, *Delay Equations: Functional-, Complex-, and Nonlinear
  Analysis***. Delay equations through sun-dual semigroups, spectral theory,
  and bifurcation.
* **Irena Lasiecka and Roberto Triggiani, *Control Theory for Partial
  Differential Equations: Continuous and Approximation Theories***. The
  semigroup theory of boundary control and Riccati equations for PDE.

### More recent works

* **David Applebaum, *Semigroups of Linear Operators: With Applications to
  Analysis, Probability and Physics*** (2019, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108672641)). A graduate semigroup course
  with diffusion, Markov, and quantum applications. Especially useful for
  connecting generator theory with concrete analytic models.
* **András Bátkai, Marjeta Kramar Fijavž, and Abdelaziz Rhandi,
  *Positive Operator Semigroups*** (2017, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-42813-0)). Listed in
  Section 19.
* **Atsushi Yagi, *Abstract Parabolic Evolution Equations and Their
  Applications*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-04631-5)). Quasilinear parabolic equations in
  the Amann–Yagi framework.
* **Jan Prüss and Gieri Simonett, *Moving Interfaces and Quasilinear
  Parabolic Evolution Equations*** (2016, Birkhäuser, Monographs in
  Mathematics). A modern research treatment built on maximal regularity.
* **Tuomas Hytönen, Jan van Neerven, Mark Veraar, and Lutz Weis,
  *Analysis in Banach Spaces II–III*** (2017 and 2023, Springer, Ergebnisse
  der Mathematik und ihrer Grenzgebiete). Where the functional calculus
  meets R-boundedness and vector-valued harmonic analysis.
* **Viorel Barbu, *Nonlinear Differential Equations of Monotone Types in
  Banach Spaces*** (2010,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4419-5542-5)). Maximal monotone
  and accretive operators and the nonlinear semigroups they generate.
  Updates Barbu's earlier *Nonlinear Semigroups and Differential Equations
  in Banach Spaces*.
* **Ruth F. Curtain and Hans Zwart, *Introduction to Infinite-Dimensional
  Systems Theory: A State-Space Approach*** (2020, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-0716-0590-5)). A new
  graduate text, replacing their 1995 book as the systems-theory
  reference.
* **Birgit Jacob and Hans J. Zwart, *Linear Port-Hamiltonian Systems on
  Infinite-dimensional Spaces*** (2012, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0399-1)). Energy-based
  infinite-dimensional systems and their generators.
* **Delio Mugnolo, *Semigroup Methods for Evolution Equations on
  Networks*** (2014, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-04621-1)). Forms and semigroups on metric graphs.

---

## 25. Integral Equations and Classical Operator Methods

A subject that predates functional analysis, supplies many of its
motivating examples, and remains the working language of scattering
theory and boundary-value problems. The recent books include classical
integral-equation courses, boundary-integral monographs, system-theoretic
inverse problems, and modern layer-potential theory.

### Foundational works

* **Rainer Kress, *Linear Integral Equations***. The standard modern
  reference: Riesz–Fredholm theory, singular integral equations,
  boundary integral methods, and regularization.
* **Solomon G. Mikhlin, *Integral Equations*** and ***Multidimensional
  Singular Integrals and Integral Equations***. The classical
  Russian-school treatments.
* **N. I. Muskhelishvili, *Singular Integral Equations***. The
  foundational monograph on Cauchy-type singular integrals and the
  Riemann–Hilbert problem.
* **F. D. Gakhov, *Boundary Value Problems***. The companion classic
  on Riemann–Hilbert boundary problems in the plane.
* **Israel Gohberg and Naum Krupnik, *One-Dimensional Linear Singular
  Integral Equations I–II***. The modern operator-theoretic treatment
  of singular integral operators and their symbols.
* **David Colton and Rainer Kress, *Inverse Acoustic and
  Electromagnetic Scattering Theory***. The standard reference where
  integral equations meet inverse problems.
* **William McLean, *Strongly Elliptic Systems and Boundary Integral
  Equations***. The careful functional-analytic treatment of boundary
  integral methods on Lipschitz domains.
* **George C. Hsiao and Wolfgang L. Wendland, *Boundary Integral
  Equations*** (2nd ed., 2021, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71127-6); first edition 2008). The analyst's treatise on
  boundary integral operators, their mapping properties, and the
  associated boundary-value problems.
* **Carlos E. Kenig, *Harmonic Analysis Techniques for Second Order
  Elliptic Boundary Value Problems***. The Calderón-programme route:
  layer potentials, Rellich identities, and boundary value problems on
  nonsmooth domains.
* **Luca Capogna, Carlos E. Kenig, and Loredana Lanzani, *Harmonic
  Measure: Geometric and Analytic Points of View*** (2005, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/ulect/035), University Lecture Series). Harmonic measure on
  rough domains, between geometric measure theory and layer potentials.
* **Habib Ammari, Hyeonbae Kang, and Hyundae Lee, *Layer Potential
  Techniques in Spectral Analysis*** (2009, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/153), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). A generalised argument principle and Rouché theorem for
  operator-valued functions, applied to eigenvalue perturbation and
  resonances.
* **Athanassios S. Fokas, *A Unified Approach to Boundary Value
  Problems*** (2008, [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717068)). The unified transform method for linear
  boundary-value problems.
* **Ram P. Kanwal, *Linear Integral Equations***. A concrete
  classical treatment, useful for explicit solution techniques.
* **Harry Hochstadt, *Integral Equations***. A shorter classical
  account of the same explicit methods.
* **Gustaf Gripenberg, Stig-Olof Londen, and Olof Staffans, *Volterra
  Integral and Functional Equations***. The standard monograph on linear and
  nonlinear Volterra equations and their asymptotics.
* **Paul R. Halmos and V. S. Sunder, *Bounded Integral Operators on L²
  Spaces***. Characterizations of integral operators and Carleman
  operators.
* **Jean-Claude Nédélec, *Acoustic and Electromagnetic Equations: Integral
  Representations for Harmonic Problems***. Boundary integral equations for
  Helmholtz and Maxwell problems.

### More recent works

* **Stephen M. Zemyan, *The Classical Theory of Integral Equations: A Concise
  Treatment*** (2012,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-8349-8)). Fredholm and
  Volterra equations, kernels, and classical solution methods. A short
  graduate course complementing the larger operator and boundary-integral
  references.
* **Hermann Brunner, *Volterra Integral Equations: An Introduction to Theory
  and Applications*** (2017, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781316162491)). Linear and nonlinear
  Volterra equations, weakly singular kernels, and functional variants.
* **Dagmar Medková, *The Laplace Equation: Boundary Value Problems on
  Bounded and Unbounded Lipschitz Domains*** (2018,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-74307-3)). Dirichlet,
  Neumann, and Robin problems by layer potentials, with careful attention to
  function spaces.
* **Dorina Mitrea, Irina Mitrea, and Marius Mitrea, *Geometric Harmonic
  Analysis I–V*** (2022–2023,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-05950-6)). A five-volume
  treatise on singular integrals and boundary value problems in uniformly
  rectifiable domains, built on a divergence theorem for nontangential
  traces. The link is to volume I.
* **Dorina Mitrea, Irina Mitrea, Marius Mitrea, and Michael Taylor,
  *The Hodge-Laplacian: Boundary Value Problems on Riemannian
  Manifolds*** (2016, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110484380)). Layer potentials and boundary-value
  problems for the Hodge Laplacian on Lipschitz domains.
* **Pascal Auscher and Moritz Egert, *Boundary Value Problems and Hardy
  Spaces for Elliptic Systems with Block Structure*** (2023,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-29973-5), Progress in Mathematics). Well-posedness of boundary-value
  problems for elliptic systems, through adapted Hardy spaces and a
  functional calculus.
* **Harm Bart, Israel Gohberg, Marinus A. Kaashoek, and André C. M. Ran,
  *A State Space Approach to Canonical Factorization with Applications***
  (2010, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8753-2)). Wiener–Hopf factorization through state-space
  methods.
* **Damir Z. Arov and Harry Dym, *Bitangential Direct and Inverse Problems for
  Systems of Integral and Differential Equations*** (2012, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139093514)). Transfer
  functions, factorization, and inverse problems for integral and differential
  systems. A research monograph at the interface of function theory and
  operator systems.
* **Francisco-Javier Sayas, *Retarded Potentials and Time Domain Boundary
  Integral Equations: A Road Map*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-26645-9)). Wave propagation,
  retarded potentials, and boundary operators in time. Included for the
  analytic treatment of causal integral equations and energy estimates.
* **Thomas Schuster, Barbara Kaltenbacher, Bernd Hofmann, and Kamil S.
  Kazimierski, *Regularization Methods in Banach Spaces*** (2012, [De
  Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110255720)). Inverse problems, convex
  regularization, and convergence rates in Banach spaces. Develops the
  geometry-dependent theory needed beyond Hilbert-space least squares.

---

# Part V. Harmonic Analysis and Function Spaces

---

## 26. Sobolev Spaces, Smoothness Scales, and Interpolation

Sobolev spaces and their fine properties, the Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin
scales, real and complex interpolation, and Orlicz and variable-exponent
spaces. The recent books treat fractional and variable smoothness, Morrey
spaces, and nonlocal equations.

### Foundational works

* **Robert A. Adams and John J. F. Fournier, *Sobolev Spaces***. The
  standard general reference for embeddings, extension, traces, and
  compactness.
* **Giovanni Leoni, *A First Course in Sobolev Spaces***. A careful
  modern development beginning from absolutely continuous and BV
  functions; the best book for someone who wants the one-dimensional
  theory done honestly first.
* **Dorothee D. Haroske and Hans Triebel, *Distributions, Sobolev
  Spaces, Elliptic Equations*** (2008, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/042)).
  Listed in Section 16.
* **Françoise Demengel and Gilbert Demengel, *Functional Spaces for the Theory
  of Elliptic Partial Differential Equations*** (2012; English translation of
  the 2007 French book,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-2807-6)). Sobolev spaces,
  traces, embeddings, and elliptic applications developed as one graduate
  course. Particularly useful for learning why the function-space
  constructions are needed.
* **Vladimir G. Maz'ya, *Sobolev Spaces, with Applications to Elliptic
  Partial Differential Equations***. The encyclopedic reference:
  capacities, sharp constants, irregular domains, and necessary
  conditions.
* **Lawrence C. Evans and Ronald F. Gariepy, *Measure Theory and Fine
  Properties of Functions***. Fine properties of Sobolev and BV
  functions in compact form.
* **William P. Ziemer, *Weakly Differentiable Functions***. Sobolev
  spaces and BV with capacity and fine-topology methods throughout.
* **Hans Triebel, *Theory of Function Spaces I–III*** (and later
  volumes). The central research reference for the Besov and
  Triebel–Lizorkin scales.
* **Hans Triebel, *Interpolation Theory, Function Spaces, Differential
  Operators***. A major synthesis of interpolation theory and
  smoothness spaces.
* **Jöran Bergh and Jörgen Löfström, *Interpolation Spaces: An
  Introduction***. The standard starting point for real and complex
  interpolation.
* **Colin Bennett and Robert Sharpley, *Interpolation of
  Operators***. Rearrangement-invariant spaces, Lorentz spaces, and
  the Calderón operator.
* **Yu. A. Brudnyi and N. Ya. Krugljak, *Interpolation Functors and
  Interpolation Spaces***. The systematic abstract theory.
* **S. G. Krein, Yu. I. Petunin, and E. M. Semenov, *Interpolation of
  Linear Operators***. The classical Russian treatment, strong on
  symmetric spaces.
* **Alessandra Lunardi, *Interpolation Theory***. Short, precise, and
  written with evolution equations in mind.
* **Luc Tartar, *An Introduction to Sobolev Spaces and Interpolation
  Spaces***. Insightful, historically aware, and close to PDE
  practice.
* **David R. Adams and Lars Inge Hedberg, *Function Spaces and
  Potential Theory***. Capacities, Bessel and Riesz potentials,
  nonlinear potential estimates, and fine properties.
* **Thomas Runst and Winfried Sickel, *Sobolev Spaces of Fractional
  Order, Nemytskij Operators, and Nonlinear Partial Differential
  Equations***. Fractional smoothness and nonlinear composition
  operators.
* **Vladimir G. Maz'ya and Tatyana O. Shaposhnikova, *Theory of Sobolev
  Multipliers: With Applications to Differential and Integral Operators***
  (2009, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-69492-2),
  Grundlehren). Pointwise multipliers between Sobolev spaces, characterised
  by capacities, with applications to regularity.
* **S. M. Nikol'skii, *Approximation of Functions of Several Variables
  and Imbedding Theorems***. The classical source for the
  approximation-theoretic definition of smoothness spaces.
* **O. V. Besov, V. P. Il'in, and S. M. Nikol'skii, *Integral
  Representations of Functions and Imbedding Theorems***. The large
  Russian reference behind much of the Besov scale.
* **M. A. Krasnosel'skii and Ya. B. Rutickii, *Convex Functions and
  Orlicz Spaces***. The classical source for Orlicz spaces.
* **M. M. Rao and Z. D. Ren, *Theory of Orlicz Spaces***. A
  comprehensive reference for Orlicz structure and integration.
* **Emmanuel Hebey, *Sobolev Spaces on Riemannian
  Manifolds***. Sobolev theory in geometric settings.
* **Haim Brezis, *Functional Analysis, Sobolev Spaces and Partial
  Differential Equations***. A concise route from Banach-space methods
  into Sobolev theory.
* **Hans Triebel, *The Structure of Functions***. Atomic, wavelet, and
  local-means characterizations of Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, with
  fractal applications.

### More recent works

* **Giovanni Leoni, *A First Course in Fractional Sobolev Spaces***
  (2023, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/229), Graduate Studies in Mathematics).
  Fractional Sobolev spaces from the one-variable theory upward, with
  the fractional Laplacian as an application. The natural sequel to
  Leoni's *First Course in Sobolev Spaces*.
* **Claudia Bucur and Enrico Valdinoci, *Nonlocal Diffusion and
  Applications*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-28739-3)). A short course on nonlocal elliptic
  equations and the fractional Laplacian.
* **Yoshihiro Sawano, *Theory of Besov Spaces*** (2018, Springer,
  Developments in Mathematics). A large modern
  alternative to Triebel, with more detail on proofs and recent
  developments.
* **Wen Yuan, Winfried Sickel, and Dachun Yang, *Morrey and Campanato
  Meet Besov, Lizorkin and Triebel*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-14606-0), Lecture Notes in
  Mathematics). The identification of Morrey–Campanato spaces with
  smoothness scales.
* **Yoshihiro Sawano, Giuseppe Di Fazio, and Denny Ivanal Hakim, *Morrey
  Spaces: Introduction and Applications to Integral Operators and
  PDEs*** (2 volumes, 2020, Chapman and Hall/CRC; [I](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780429085925), [II](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003029076)). A large modern
  treatment of Morrey spaces and their operators.
* **David R. Adams, *Morrey Spaces*** (2015,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-26681-7)). Morrey estimates,
  potentials, capacities, and applications to PDE. A concise research course
  beside the larger Morrey-space reference already listed.
* **Vakhtang Kokilashvili, Alexander Meskhi, Humberto Rafeiro, and
  Stefan Samko, *Integral Operators in Non-Standard Function Spaces***
  (2 volumes, 2016, Birkhäuser; [I](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-21015-5), [II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-21018-6)). Singular integrals on Morrey, variable
  exponent, and grand Lebesgue spaces.
* **Luboš Pick, Alois Kufner, Oldřich John, and Svatopluk Fučík,
  *Function Spaces***, volume 1 (2nd ed., 2013, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110250428)), with later
  volumes. A systematic reference for rearrangement-invariant and
  smoothness spaces in the Czech tradition.
* **Lars Diening, Petteri Harjulehto, Peter Hästö, and Michael
  Růžička, *Lebesgue and Sobolev Spaces with Variable
  Exponents*** (2011, Springer, Lecture Notes in Mathematics). The
  standard treatment of variable-exponent spaces.
* **David V. Cruz-Uribe and Alberto Fiorenza, *Variable Lebesgue Spaces:
  Foundations and Harmonic Analysis*** (2013,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0548-3)).
  Variable-exponent spaces, the maximal operator, and extrapolation. The
  companion to Diening–Harjulehto–Hästö–Růžička.
* **Petteri Harjulehto and Peter Hästö, *Orlicz Spaces and Generalized
  Orlicz Spaces*** (2019, Springer, Lecture Notes in Mathematics). The
  modern reference for Musielak–Orlicz settings and the associated
  harmonic analysis.
* **Juha Kinnunen, Juha Lehrbäck, and Antti Vähäkangas, *Maximal Function
  Methods for Sobolev Spaces*** (2021, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/257), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). Pointwise inequalities, capacities, and Hardy inequalities
  for Sobolev functions through maximal functions, in Euclidean and metric
  settings.
* **Augusto C. Ponce, *Elliptic PDEs, Measures and Capacities: From the
  Poisson Equation to Nonlinear Thomas–Fermi Problems*** (2016,
  [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/140)). Sobolev capacities, measure data, and
  the borderline integrability of solutions.
* **Haïm Brezis and Petru Mironescu, *Sobolev Maps to the Circle: From the
  Perspective of Analysis, Geometry, and Topology*** (2021,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-0716-1512-6)). Lifting, degree,
  and density questions for Sobolev and VMO maps with values in the circle.

---

## 27. Fourier Analysis and Real-Variable Harmonic Analysis

Fourier series and integrals, maximal functions, singular integrals,
weights, Hardy spaces, Littlewood–Paley theory, and oscillatory integrals.
The recent books add graduate Fourier courses, Gaussian harmonic analysis,
sharp estimates, multi-parameter theory, polynomial methods, and
higher-order Fourier analysis.

### Foundational works

#### First and second courses

* **Elias M. Stein and Rami Shakarchi, *Fourier Analysis: An
  Introduction***. The best first graduate bridge from classical
  Fourier series to the analytic viewpoint.
* **Yitzhak Katznelson, *An Introduction to Harmonic Analysis***. A
  compact classic on Fourier series, Fourier transforms, and harmonic
  analysis on groups; short, deep, and endlessly reread.
* **Elias M. Stein and Guido Weiss, *Introduction to Fourier Analysis
  on Euclidean Spaces***. Hardy spaces, conjugate functions, spherical
  harmonics, and genuinely several-variable Fourier analysis.
* **Javier Duoandikoetxea, *Fourier Analysis***. Short and excellent
  on real-variable methods, singular integrals, and weights.
* **Loukas Grafakos, *Classical Fourier Analysis*** and ***Modern
  Fourier Analysis***. The current classroom standard: maximal
  functions, singular integrals, multipliers, weighted estimates,
  Hardy spaces, Littlewood–Paley theory, and multilinear operators.
* **Mark A. Pinsky, *Introduction to Fourier Analysis and
  Wavelets***. A gentler modern route.
* **T. W. Körner, *Fourier Analysis***. Idiosyncratic, classical, and
  full of the counterexamples that motivate the general theory.
* **Anton Deitmar and Siegfried Echterhoff, *Principles of Harmonic
  Analysis***. A modern unified treatment of the classical and
  abstract theories.
* **Henry Helson, *Harmonic Analysis***. A short, elegant course in
  Fourier series, the Fourier transform, and harmonic analysis on groups.

#### Research monographs and references

* **Elias M. Stein, *Singular Integrals and Differentiability
  Properties of Functions***. The foundational mature account of
  Calderón–Zygmund theory and its relation to Sobolev spaces.
* **Elias M. Stein, *Harmonic Analysis: Real-Variable Methods,
  Orthogonality, and Oscillatory Integrals***. The advanced synthesis
  of maximal functions, restriction, oscillatory integrals, and almost
  everything the Stein school built.
* **Antoni Zygmund, *Trigonometric Series I–II***. The classical bible
  of Fourier series; still the reference for sharp one-dimensional
  results.
* **N. K. Bari, *A Treatise on Trigonometric Series***. The
  complementary Russian treatise, particularly on uniqueness,
  convergence, and basis questions.
* **Alberto Torchinsky, *Real-Variable Methods in Harmonic
  Analysis***. Hardy spaces, maximal functions, and singular integrals
  in a readable systematic account.
* **José García-Cuerva and José L. Rubio de Francia, *Weighted Norm
  Inequalities and Related Topics***. The fundamental monograph on
  Muckenhoupt weights, extrapolation, and vector-valued inequalities.
* **Christoph Thiele, *Wave Packet Analysis***. The concise research
  account of time-frequency analysis after Carleson and Lacey–Thiele.
* **Thomas H. Wolff, *Lectures on Harmonic Analysis***. Restriction,
  Kakeya, and geometric harmonic analysis, in notes that shaped a
  generation.
* **Christopher D. Sogge, *Fourier Integrals in Classical
  Analysis***. Oscillatory integrals, spectral clusters, and
  eigenfunction estimates.
* **Elias M. Stein, *Topics in Harmonic Analysis Related to the
  Littlewood–Paley Theory***. A short classic on square functions and
  semigroup methods.
* **Gerald B. Folland and Elias M. Stein, *Hardy Spaces on Homogeneous
  Groups***. The nilpotent-group setting where much of the modern
  theory was first tested.
* **Alexander Nagel and Elias M. Stein, *Lectures on
  Pseudo-Differential Operators***, and their work on
  Carnot–Carathéodory metrics. The subelliptic strand of real-variable
  theory.
* **E. C. Titchmarsh, *Introduction to the Theory of Fourier
  Integrals***. A classical reference for the Fourier transform of a
  single variable.
* **Jean-Pierre Kahane, *Some Random Series of Functions***. Random
  Fourier series, and a model of how probability enters harmonic
  analysis.
* **Hugh L. Montgomery, *Ten Lectures on the Interface Between
  Analytic Number Theory and Harmonic Analysis***. Included despite
  the number-theoretic framing: the large-sieve and uncertainty
  material is standard harmonic analysis.
* **Terence Tao and Van H. Vu, *Additive Combinatorics*** (2006,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511755149); later corrected printings). Freiman's
  theorem, Szemerédi's theorem, and the Fourier-analytic arguments that
  became standard equipment.
* **Salomon Bochner, *Lectures on Fourier Integrals***. The classical
  lectures on Fourier integrals, positive-definite functions, and Bochner's
  theorem.
* **Salomon Bochner and Komaravolu Chandrasekharan, *Fourier
  Transforms***. A compact classical account of the L¹ and L²
  theory of Fourier transforms.
* **R. E. Edwards and G. I. Gaudry, *Littlewood–Paley and Multiplier
  Theory***. Littlewood–Paley theory and Fourier multipliers on groups.
* **Frederick W. King, *Hilbert Transforms I–II***. The comprehensive
  reference on the Hilbert transform: properties, inequalities, and
  applications.
* **B. Golubov, A. Efimov, and V. Skvortsov, *Walsh Series and
  Transforms***. The standard reference on Walsh series, dyadic harmonic
  analysis, and its analogues of classical Fourier analysis.
* **John E. Gilbert and Margaret A. M. Murray, *Clifford Algebras and Dirac
  Operators in Harmonic Analysis***. Clifford algebras, Dirac operators, and
  their use in Euclidean harmonic analysis and representation theory.

### More recent works

#### Courses

* **Loukas Grafakos, *Fundamentals of Fourier Analysis*** (2024,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-56500-7)). A graduate course in
  Fourier transforms, multipliers, and singular integrals, with extensive
  exercises. A separate, more classroom-oriented work than the author's
  *Classical* and *Modern Fourier Analysis*.

#### Real-variable theory: research monographs

* **David Cruz-Uribe, José Maria Martell, and Carlos Pérez, *Weights,
  Extrapolation and the Theory of Rubio de Francia*** (2011, Birkhäuser,
  Operator Theory: Advances and Applications). The modern
  successor, covering the sharp-constant era.
* **Camil Muscalu and Wilhelm Schlag, *Classical and Multilinear
  Harmonic Analysis I–II*** (2013, Cambridge University Press, Cambridge
  Studies in Advanced Mathematics). The modern route to paraproducts,
  time-frequency methods, and multilinear estimates.
* **Vasily Vasyunin and Alexander Volberg, *The Bellman Function Technique in
  Harmonic Analysis*** (2020, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108764469)). Sharp inequalities and
  harmonic-analysis estimates via Bellman functions. A systematic research
  treatment of a technique often learned from individual papers.
* **Brian Street, *Multi-parameter Singular Integrals*** (2014,
  [Princeton University Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400852758), Annals of Mathematics Studies). The
  multi-parameter Calderón–Zygmund theory in the form developed with
  Stein.
* **Brian Street, *Maximal Subellipticity*** (2023, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783111085647)). Listed
  in full in Section 33. Function spaces adapted to Carnot–Carathéodory
  geometries.
* **Ferenc Weisz, *Convergence and Summability of Fourier Transforms and Hardy
  Spaces*** (2017, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-56814-0)).
  Summability, maximal operators, and Hardy-space estimates for Fourier
  transforms. A specialist account of convergence questions beyond the
  elementary L2 theory.
* **Wilfredo Urbina-Romero, *Gaussian Harmonic Analysis*** (2019,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-05597-4)). Gaussian measure,
  Hermite expansions, the Ornstein–Uhlenbeck semigroup, and associated
  operators. The counterpart of Euclidean harmonic analysis for Gaussian
  spaces.
* **Pascal Auscher and Moritz Egert, *Boundary Value Problems and Hardy
  Spaces for Elliptic Systems with Block Structure*** (2023,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-29973-5)). Listed in Section 25. Hardy spaces adapted to operators.
* **Alexei Poltoratski, *Toeplitz Approach to Problems of the
  Uncertainty Principle*** (2015, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cbms/121)). Listed
  in Section 21.
* **Tuomas Hytönen, Jan van Neerven, Mark Veraar, and Lutz Weis,
  *Analysis in Banach Spaces I–III*** (2016–2023, Springer, Ergebnisse der
  Mathematik und ihrer Grenzgebiete). The vector-valued theory,
  listed again from Part IV.

#### Restriction, geometric, and additive-combinatorial methods

* **Ciprian Demeter, *Fourier Restriction, Decoupling, and
  Applications*** (2020, Cambridge University Press, Cambridge Studies in
  Advanced Mathematics). The standard book on decoupling and its arithmetic
  and PDE consequences.
* **Pertti Mattila, *Fourier Analysis and Hausdorff
  Dimension*** (2015, Cambridge University Press, Cambridge Studies in
  Advanced Mathematics). Projections, distance sets, and the
  Fourier-analytic side of fractal geometry.
* **Larry Guth, *Polynomial Methods in Combinatorics*** (2016, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/ulect/064), University Lecture Series). The polynomial
  method, the joints problem, and the incidence geometry used in
  restriction and decoupling.
* **Giancarlo Travaglini, *Number Theory, Fourier Analysis and Geometric
  Discrepancy*** (2014, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781107358379)). Fourier methods for
  lattice-point counts and discrepancy of geometric sets. Included for its
  harmonic-analysis tools, with number theory providing the applications.
* **Terence Tao, *Higher Order Fourier Analysis*** (2012, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/142), Graduate Studies in Mathematics). Gowers norms,
  nilsequences, and the inverse theorems, written as analysis.

---

## 28. Abstract Harmonic Analysis on Groups

Haar measure, unitary representations, and the Plancherel theorem, on
locally compact abelian groups, compact and Lie groups, and symmetric
spaces. The recent books treat nilpotent and solvable groups, property (T),
unitary representations, positive definite functions, random walks on
reductive groups, and finite harmonic analysis.

### Foundational works

* **Gerald B. Folland, *A Course in Abstract Harmonic Analysis***. The
  best single graduate introduction to Haar measure, unitary
  representations, and the Plancherel theorem.
* **Edwin Hewitt and Kenneth A. Ross, *Abstract Harmonic Analysis
  I–II***. The definitive classical reference.
* **Walter Rudin, *Fourier Analysis on Groups***. A compact classic on
  locally compact abelian groups, idempotent measures, Sidon sets, and
  Fourier–Stieltjes theory.
* **Hans Reiter and Jan D. Stegeman, *Classical Harmonic Analysis and
  Locally Compact Groups***. A systematic modern account with careful
  treatment of the Segal-algebra viewpoint.
* **Lynn H. Loomis, *An Introduction to Abstract Harmonic
  Analysis***. Short, classical, and algebra-first.
* **Jacques Faraut, *Analysis on Lie Groups: An Introduction*** (2008,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511755170)).
  Matrix Lie groups, Haar measure, the Peter–Weyl theorem, and spherical
  harmonics, as a first course in harmonic analysis on groups.
* **Brian C. Hall, *Lie Groups, Lie Algebras, and Representations: An
  Elementary Introduction*** (2nd ed., 2015, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-13467-3), Graduate Texts
  in Mathematics; first edition 2003). The usual first book on compact groups and their
  representations for analysts.
* **Daniel Bump, *Lie Groups*** (2nd ed., 2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-8024-2), Graduate Texts
  in Mathematics; first edition 2004). A broader graduate text, reaching the infinite-
  dimensional representations analysts meet later.
* **Sigurður Helgason, *Groups and Geometric Analysis*** and
  ***Geometric Analysis on Symmetric Spaces***. Spherical functions,
  invariant differential operators, Radon transforms, and analysis on
  symmetric spaces.
* **Anthony W. Knapp, *Representation Theory of Semisimple Groups: An
  Overview Based on Examples***. Representation theory in the form
  most useful to harmonic analysts.
* **Anthony W. Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction***. The
  structural background that harmonic analysis on groups assumes.
* **Nolan R. Wallach, *Real Reductive Groups I–II***. The
  research-level treatment of harmonic analysis on reductive groups.
* **V. S. Varadarajan, *An Introduction to Harmonic Analysis on
  Semisimple Lie Groups***. A concise bridge into the noncommutative
  theory.
* **Roe Goodman and Nolan R. Wallach, *Symmetry, Representations, and
  Invariants*** (2009, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-79852-3), Graduate Texts in Mathematics). A
  large course in the invariant theory and representation theory used
  in harmonic analysis.
* **Audrey Terras, *Harmonic Analysis on Symmetric Spaces*** (2 volumes, 2nd
  ed., 2013–2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-7972-7); [volume II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4939-3408-9); first edition 1985–1988).
  Fourier analysis on Euclidean space, the sphere, the upper half-plane, and
  the space of positive matrices, with applications to number theory and
  physics. The link is to volume I.
* **I. M. Gel'fand, M. I. Graev, and N. Ya. Vilenkin, *Generalized
  Functions*, volume 5**. Integral geometry and representation theory
  in the Gelfand style.
* **Gerald B. Folland, *Harmonic Analysis in Phase Space***. The
  Heisenberg group, Weyl calculus, and the metaplectic representation.
* **Michael E. Taylor, *Noncommutative Harmonic Analysis***. Analysis
  on Lie groups oriented toward PDE and spectral theory.
* **Mitsuo Sugiura, *Unitary Representations and Harmonic
  Analysis***. A readable route into the concrete examples.
* **Alain Robert, *Introduction to the Representation Theory of
  Compact and Locally Compact Groups***. Short and useful for the
  compact case.
* **A. A. Kirillov, *Lectures on the Orbit Method*** (2004, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/064), Graduate Studies
  in Mathematics). Coadjoint orbits as a guide to unitary representations,
  by the founder of the method.
* **Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe, and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan's
  Property (T)*** (2008, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511542749)). The standard
  monograph on property (T).
* **Ramesh Gangolli and V. S. Varadarajan, *Harmonic Analysis of Spherical
  Functions on Real Reductive Groups***. Harish-Chandra's theory of
  spherical functions and the Plancherel formula.
* **Garth Warner, *Harmonic Analysis on Semi-Simple Lie Groups
  I–II***. A detailed account of Harish-Chandra's work.
* **Gerrit Heckman and Henrik Schlichtkrull, *Harmonic Analysis and
  Special Functions on Symmetric Spaces***. Hypergeometric functions
  associated with root systems and harmonic analysis on symmetric spaces.
* **Sundaram Thangavelu, *Harmonic Analysis on the Heisenberg
  Group***. The Weyl transform, Hermite and Laguerre expansions, and
  restriction theorems on the Heisenberg group.
* **Colin C. Graham and O. Carruth McGehee, *Essays in Commutative Harmonic
  Analysis***. Thin sets, Helson sets, and the structure of measure
  algebras on locally compact abelian groups.
* **Herbert Heyer, *Probability Measures on Locally Compact
  Groups***. Convolution semigroups, Lévy–Khintchine formulas, and
  central limit theorems on groups.
* **Peter D. Lax and Ralph S. Phillips, *Scattering Theory for
  Automorphic Functions***. The Lax–Phillips scattering framework applied
  to the spectral theory of the modular group.

### More recent works

* **Emmanuel Kowalski, *An Introduction to the Representation Theory of
  Groups*** (2014, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/155), Graduate Studies in
  Mathematics). A graduate course in representations with harmonic
  analysis in view.
* **Bachir Bekka and Pierre de la Harpe, *Unitary Representations of Groups,
  Duals, and Characters*** (2020, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://www.ams.org/books/surv/250/)). Unitary duals, characters,
  and representation theory of locally compact and discrete groups. A research
  reference connecting abstract harmonic analysis with operator algebras.
* **Eberhard Kaniuth and Anthony To-Ming Lau, *Fourier and
  Fourier–Stieltjes Algebras on Locally Compact Groups*** (2018, American
  Mathematical Society, Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). The
  algebra-theoretic modern reference.
* **Eberhard Kaniuth and Keith F. Taylor, *Induced Representations of
  Locally Compact Groups*** (2013, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139045391)). Mackey's induction
  theory, the imprimitivity theorem, and the topology of duals.
* **Antoine Derighetti, *Convolution Operators on Groups*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-20656-6), Lecture Notes in Mathematics). Convolution operators,
  Figà-Talamanca–Herz algebras, and amenability.
* **Kate Juschenko, *Amenability of Discrete Groups by Examples*** (2022,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/266),
  Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). Criteria for amenability and the
  recent examples, including topological full groups and extensive
  amenability.
* **Véronique Fischer and Michael Ruzhansky, *Quantization on Nilpotent
  Lie Groups*** (2016, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-29558-9), Progress in Mathematics). The Weyl
  calculus on nilpotent groups, at research level.
* **Michael Ruzhansky and Ville Turunen, *Pseudo-Differential Operators
  and Symmetries: Background Analysis and Advanced Topics*** (2010,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8514-9)). Global pseudo-differential operators adapted to
  symmetries and groups.
* **Michael Ruzhansky and Durvudkhan Suragan, *Hardy Inequalities on
  Homogeneous Groups*** (2019, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-02895-4)). Listed in Section 11.
* **Hidenori Fujiwara and Jean Ludwig, *Harmonic Analysis on Exponential
  Solvable Lie Groups*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-4-431-55288-8)). Representation
  theory, the orbit method, and harmonic analysis on exponential solvable
  groups. A research monograph for a class not covered in detail by the
  compact and semisimple texts.
* **Palle Jorgensen, Steen Pedersen, and Feng Tian, *Extensions of Positive
  Definite Functions: Applications and Their Harmonic Analysis*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-39780-1)). Positive definite
  kernels, extension problems, and unitary representations. A specialised
  treatment connecting harmonic analysis with reproducing kernels and spectral
  measures.
* **Sigurdur Helgason, *Integral Geometry and Radon Transforms*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4419-6055-9)). Radon transforms
  on Euclidean and symmetric spaces, support theorems, and inversion
  formulas.
* **Valery V. Volchkov and Vitaly V. Volchkov, *Offbeat Integral Geometry on
  Symmetric Spaces*** (2013,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0572-8)). Integral
  transforms, support and uniqueness questions, and mean-value problems on
  symmetric spaces. A research continuation of the classical Radon-transform
  theory.
* **Tullio Ceccherini-Silberstein, Fabio Scarabotti, and Filippo Tolli,
  *Discrete Harmonic Analysis: Representations, Number Theory,
  Expanders, and the Fourier Transform*** (2018, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781316856383)). Finite and discrete harmonic analysis in a form useful beyond
  group theory. Their *Harmonic Analysis on Finite Groups* (2008) and
  *Representation Theory and Harmonic Analysis of Wreath Products of
  Finite Groups* (2014), both Cambridge, are the more specialised
  companions.
* **Yves Benoist and Jean-François Quint, *Random Walks on Reductive
  Groups*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-47721-3)). Listed in Section 10.
* **David Applebaum, *Probability on Compact Lie Groups*** (2014,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-07842-7)). Listed in Section 8.
* **Paul Garrett, *Modern Analysis of Automorphic Forms by Example
  I–II*** (2018, Cambridge University Press; [I](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781316650332), [II](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108571814)).
  Spectral theory, Sobolev spaces, and distributions applied to automorphic
  forms on concrete examples.

---

## 29. Wavelets, Frames, Time–Frequency Analysis, and Sampling

Wavelets and multiresolution analysis, frames and Riesz bases, Gabor and
time–frequency analysis, and sampling and interpolation. The recent books
treat modulation spaces, frames, compressed sensing, and sampling beyond
the bandlimited model.

### Foundational works

* **Ingrid Daubechies, *Ten Lectures on Wavelets***. The foundational
  graduate monograph on orthonormal wavelets and multiresolution
  analysis.
* **Yves Meyer, *Wavelets and Operators*** (with the second volume by
  Meyer and Coifman, *Calderón–Zygmund and Multilinear
  Operators*). Wavelets as an instrument of singular-integral theory.
* **Eugenio Hernández and Guido Weiss, *A First Course on
  Wavelets***. The cleanest mathematical treatment of the
  Fourier-analytic construction.
* **Przemysław Wojtaszczyk, *A Mathematical Introduction to
  Wavelets***. Wavelets from the function-space and approximation
  viewpoint.
* **Stéphane Mallat, *A Wavelet Tour of Signal Processing***. Broader
  and more computational, but mathematically substantial and still an
  important reference.
* **Albert Cohen, *Numerical Analysis of Wavelet
  Methods***. Approximation spaces, nonlinear approximation, and the
  link to Besov scales.
* **Karlheinz Gröchenig, *Foundations of Time-Frequency
  Analysis***. The standard mathematical monograph on Gabor analysis,
  modulation spaces, and frames.
* **Ole Christensen, *An Introduction to Frames and Riesz
