  Bases***. The functional-analytic foundation of frame theory.
* **Robert M. Young, *An Introduction to Nonharmonic Fourier
  Series***. The classical account of Riesz bases of exponentials and
  completeness problems.
* **Kristian Seip, *Interpolation and Sampling in Spaces of Analytic
  Functions***. Sampling and interpolation via complex analysis and
  density conditions.
* **Hans G. Feichtinger and Thomas Strohmer (eds.), *Gabor Analysis
  and Algorithms*** and ***Advances in Gabor Analysis***. Collections
  that function as references for the subject.

### More recent works

* **Christopher Heil, *A Basis Theory Primer*** (expanded ed., 2011,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4687-5)). Bases, frames, and the functional analysis behind
  time-frequency expansions.
* **Árpád Bényi and Kasso A. Okoudjou, *Modulation Spaces: With
  Applications to Pseudodifferential Operators and Nonlinear
  Schrödinger Equations*** (2020, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-0716-0332-1)). The graduate monograph
  on modulation spaces after Gröchenig.
* **Elena Cordero and Luigi Rodino, *Time-Frequency Analysis of
  Operators*** (2020, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110532456)). Pseudodifferential and Fourier
  integral operators in the time-frequency calculus.
* **Maurice A. de Gosson, *Symplectic Methods in Harmonic Analysis and
  in Mathematical Physics*** (2011, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-9992-4)). The Weyl calculus,
  the Wigner distribution, and symplectic covariance.
* **Peter G. Casazza and Gitta Kutyniok (eds.), *Finite Frames: Theory
  and Applications*** (2013, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-8373-3)). A reference volume for finite
  frame theory.
* **Shayne F. D. Waldron, *An Introduction to Finite Tight Frames*** (2018,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4815-2)). Finite tight
  frames, equiangular lines, and frames arising from group representations.
* **Bin Han, *Framelets and Wavelets: Algorithms, Analysis, and
  Applications*** (2017, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-68530-4)). A large monograph on framelets,
  with the analysis separated from the algorithms.
* **Aleksandr Krivoshein, Vladimir Protasov, and Maria Skopina, *Multivariate
  Wavelet Frames*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-981-10-3205-9)). Refinement
  equations, matrix masks, and construction of multivariate wavelet frames. A
  specialised reference beyond the one-dimensional wavelet theory.
* **Gitta Kutyniok and Demetrio Labate (eds.), *Shearlets: Multiscale Analysis
  for Multivariate Data*** (2012,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-8316-0)). Directional
  multiscale systems, sparse approximation, and geometric singularities. A
  substantial collection of lecture-style treatments extending the wavelet
  framework to anisotropic features, and the most accessible entry to the
  curvelet and shearlet literature of Emmanuel Candès, David Donoho, and
  others.
* **Alexander Olevskii and Alexander Ulanovskii, *Functions with
  Disconnected Spectrum: Sampling, Interpolation, Translates*** (2016,
  American Mathematical Society, University Lecture Series). Sampling and
  interpolation for functions with spectrum in a given set.
* **Yonina C. Eldar, *Sampling Theory: Beyond Bandlimited Systems***
  (2015, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511762321)). Sampling of structured signals,
  written with the harmonic analysis visible.
* **Simon Foucart and Holger Rauhut, *A Mathematical Introduction to
  Compressive Sensing*** (2013, Birkhäuser, Applied and Numerical Harmonic
  Analysis). Included because its concentration and
  random-matrix techniques have become part of applied harmonic
  analysis.

---

## 30. Distributions, Pseudodifferential Operators, Microlocal and Semiclassical Analysis

Hörmander's treatise is the central reference, surrounded by the
symbolic and Fourier-integral calculi, Weyl quantization, and the
semiclassical theory of spectral asymptotics and resonances. The more
recent works include Zworski's semiclassical text, Dyatlov–Zworski on
resonances, Ivrii's five-volume treatise, several specialised
monographs, and the hypocoercivity theory.

### Foundational works

* **Lars Hörmander, *The Analysis of Linear Partial Differential
  Operators I***. The working reference for distributions, Fourier
  transforms, wavefront sets, and constant-coefficient equations.
* **Lars Hörmander, *The Analysis of Linear Partial Differential
  Operators II–IV***. Differential operators with variable
  coefficients, pseudodifferential operators, propagation of
  singularities, and Fourier integral operators.
* **Lars Hörmander, *Linear Partial Differential Operators***. The
  earlier, shorter route into the constant-coefficient and
  distributional theory.
* **François Trèves, *Introduction to Pseudodifferential and Fourier
  Integral Operators I–II***. A large systematic presentation of
  symbolic and Fourier-integral methods.
* **Michael E. Taylor, *Pseudodifferential Operators***. A concise
  geometric-analytic treatment.
* **Michael E. Taylor, *Pseudodifferential Operators and Nonlinear
  PDE***. Paradifferential calculus and microlocal methods for
  nonlinear equations.
* **Guy Métivier, *Para-Differential Calculus and Applications to the
  Cauchy Problem for Nonlinear Systems*** (2008, [Edizioni della
  Normale](https://edizioni.sns.it/prodotto/para-differential-calculus-and-applications-to-the-cauchy-problem-for-nonlinear-systems/)). Paradifferential calculus in the form used for nonlinear
  hyperbolic equations.
* **Alain Grigis and Johannes Sjöstrand, *Microlocal Analysis for
  Differential Operators***. Compact and research-oriented.
* **J. J. Duistermaat, *Fourier Integral Operators***. The classic
  geometric treatment of canonical relations and oscillatory
  integrals.
* **M. A. Shubin, *Pseudodifferential Operators and Spectral
  Theory***. Global pseudodifferential analysis and spectral
  asymptotics.
* **Xavier Saint Raymond, *Elementary Introduction to the Theory of
  Pseudodifferential Operators***. An accessible short entry point.
* **Serge Alinhac and Patrick Gérard, *Pseudo-differential Operators and the
  Nash–Moser Theorem*** (2007, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/082), Graduate Studies in
  Mathematics). Pseudodifferential and paradifferential calculus, followed
  by the Nash–Moser theorem and its applications. Short and well chosen.
* **Mouez Dimassi and Johannes Sjöstrand, *Spectral Asymptotics in the
  Semi-Classical Limit***. A major research reference.
* **André Martinez, *An Introduction to Semiclassical and Microlocal
  Analysis***. A concise route into the same material.
* **Gerald B. Folland, *Harmonic Analysis in Phase Space***. Weyl
  quantization and the Heisenberg-group foundation.
* **Victor Guillemin and Shlomo Sternberg, *Geometric Asymptotics***
  and ***Semi-Classical Analysis***. The symplectic side of
  oscillatory integrals and spectral asymptotics.
* **Richard B. Melrose, *The Atiyah–Patodi–Singer Index Theorem*** and
  ***Geometric Scattering Theory***. Microlocal analysis on manifolds
  with boundary and at infinity.
* **Frédéric Pham, *Singularities of Integrals: Homology, Hyperfunctions and
  Microlocal Analysis*** (2011; English translation of the 2005 French work,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-85729-603-0)). Integration cycles,
  singularities, and microlocal ideas developed through geometry and topology.
  Useful background for asymptotic analysis and analytic continuation of
  parameter-dependent integrals.
* **Bernard Helffer and Francis Nier, *Hypoelliptic Estimates and Spectral
  Theory for Fokker–Planck Operators and Witten Laplacians*** (2005,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/b104762), Lecture Notes in
  Mathematics). Hypoellipticity and return to equilibrium for kinetic
  Fokker–Planck operators, and semiclassical analysis of Witten Laplacians.
* **Cédric Villani, *Hypocoercivity*** (2009, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0065-9266-09-00567-5), Memoirs). The
  abstract framework for exponential convergence to equilibrium of
  degenerate dissipative equations such as the kinetic Fokker–Planck
  equation.
* **Masaki Kashiwara, Takahiro Kawai, and Tatsuo Kimura, *Foundations of
  Algebraic Analysis***. Hyperfunctions, microfunctions, and
  microlocal analysis in the Sato school's approach.
* **V. P. Maslov and M. V. Fedoriuk, *Semi-Classical Approximation in
  Quantum Mechanics***. The canonical operator and Maslov index; the
  founding work on global WKB approximation.

### More recent works

* **Maciej Zworski, *Semiclassical Analysis*** (2012, American
  Mathematical Society). The standard graduate text for semiclassical
  calculus, microlocalization, and applications to spectral theory and
  resonances.
* **Nicolas Lerner, *Metrics on the Phase Space and Non-Selfadjoint
  Pseudo-Differential Operators*** (2010, Birkhäuser). The modern
  Weyl–Hörmander calculus, done thoroughly.
* **Jean-Michel Bony's paradifferential calculus**, most accessible
  through **Hajer Bahouri, Jean-Yves Chemin, and Raphaël Danchin,
  *Fourier Analysis and Nonlinear Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2011, Springer). Also the standard modern reference for
  Littlewood–Paley methods in PDE.
* **Fabio Nicola and Luigi Rodino, *Global Pseudo-Differential Calculus on
  Euclidean Spaces*** (2010,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8512-5)). Shubin and SG
  symbol classes, global ellipticity, and spectral asymptotics on R^n.
* **Semyon Dyatlov and Maciej Zworski, *Mathematical Theory of
  Scattering Resonances*** (2019, American Mathematical Society). The
  modern treatment of resonances, a subject that now organizes much
  semiclassical analysis.
* **Victor Ivrii, *Microlocal Analysis, Sharp Spectral Asymptotics and
  Applications*** (5 volumes, 2019, Springer; [I](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30557-4), [II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30541-3), [III](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30537-6), [IV](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30545-1), [V](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30561-1)). The expanded treatise on
  sharp Weyl laws, propagation, and applications to quantum theory.
  It extends Ivrii's 1998 *Microlocal Analysis and Precise Spectral
  Asymptotics*, listed in Sections 50 and 53.
* **Nicolas Lerner, *Carleman Inequalities: An Introduction and More*** (2019,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-15993-1)). A systematic
  treatment of weighted estimates, unique continuation, and applications. A
  distinct later monograph beside Lerner's phase-space book listed above.
* **Alberto Parmeggiani, *Spectral Theory of Non-commutative Harmonic
  Oscillators: An Introduction*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-11922-4), Lecture Notes in
  Mathematics). A short research introduction to a model non-commuting
  system.
* **Véronique Fischer and Michael Ruzhansky, *Quantization on Nilpotent
  Lie Groups*** (2016, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-29558-9)). Listed in Section 28.
* **Michael Ruzhansky and Ville Turunen, *Pseudo-Differential Operators
  and Symmetries*** (2010, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8514-9)). Listed in Section 28.
* **Brian Street, *Maximal Subellipticity*** (2023, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783111085647)). Listed
  in Section 33.
* **Monique Combescure and Didier Robert, *Coherent States and
  Applications in Mathematical Physics*** (2nd ed., 2021, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-70845-0)).
  Listed in Section 22.
* **Gabriel P. Paternain, Mikko Salo, and Gunther Uhlmann, *Geometric
  Inverse Problems*** (2023, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009039901)). Listed in
  Section 22. Microlocal analysis applied to integral geometry.

---

# Part VI. Differential Equations, Variational Methods, and Geometric Measure Theory

---

## 31. Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical Systems

The classical ODE references are Coddington–Levinson, Hartman, and Hale;
the qualitative and dynamical side runs from Arnold and
Guckenheimer–Holmes to Katok–Hasselblatt, with attractors of dissipative
evolution equations and the analytic theory in the complex domain. The
more recent books treat nonuniform hyperbolicity, Hamiltonian
instability, attractors and infinite-dimensional bifurcation, Hamiltonian
PDE, and several textbook expositions.

### Foundational works

* **E. A. Coddington and Norman Levinson, *Theory of Ordinary
  Differential Equations***. The classical graduate reference for
  existence, linear systems, asymptotics, and boundary problems.
* **Philip Hartman, *Ordinary Differential Equations***. A
  foundational qualitative treatment of flows, stability, invariant
  manifolds, and asymptotic behaviour.
* **Jack K. Hale, *Ordinary Differential Equations***. Dynamical and
  stability methods, especially influential for the qualitative
  theory.
* **V. I. Arnold, *Ordinary Differential Equations*** and
  ***Geometrical Methods in the Theory of Ordinary Differential
  Equations***. Short, geometric, and conceptually decisive.
* **Carmen Chicone, *Ordinary Differential Equations with
  Applications***. Strong on qualitative methods, averaging, and
  perturbation.
* **Lawrence Perko, *Differential Equations and Dynamical
  Systems***. A standard source for planar and finite-dimensional
  dynamics.
* **Morris W. Hirsch, Stephen Smale, and Robert L. Devaney,
  *Differential Equations, Dynamical Systems, and an Introduction to
  Chaos***. A geometric first graduate introduction.
* **James D. Meiss, *Differential Dynamical Systems*** (revised ed.,
  2017, [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611974645)). A geometric graduate course in finite-dimensional
  dynamics, with careful treatment of invariant manifolds.
* **Jack K. Hale and Hüseyin Koçak, *Dynamics and Bifurcations***. A
  clear route into bifurcation theory.
* **Yuri A. Kuznetsov, *Elements of Applied Bifurcation Theory***. The
  standard systematic reference for normal forms and bifurcations.
* **Stephen Wiggins, *Introduction to Applied Nonlinear Dynamical
  Systems and Chaos***. Broad coverage of invariant manifolds,
  perturbation, and chaotic dynamics.
* **John Guckenheimer and Philip Holmes, *Nonlinear Oscillations,
  Dynamical Systems, and Bifurcations of Vector Fields***. The classic
  that set the modern agenda.
* **Jan A. Sanders, Ferdinand Verhulst, and James Murdock, *Averaging
  Methods in Nonlinear Dynamical Systems*** (2nd ed., 2007,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-48918-6), Applied
  Mathematical Sciences). Averaging with error estimates, normal forms, and
  passage through resonance. Listed again in Section 50.
* **Anatole Katok and Boris Hasselblatt, *Introduction to the Modern
  Theory of Dynamical Systems***. The standard large graduate
  reference for smooth and hyperbolic dynamics.
* **Clark Robinson, *Dynamical Systems: Stability, Symbolic Dynamics,
  and Chaos***. A thorough alternative with careful proofs.
* **Christian Bonatti, Lorenzo J. Díaz, and Marcelo Viana, *Dynamics
  Beyond Uniform Hyperbolicity*** (2005, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/b138174)). Listed in Section
  10.
* **Jürgen Moser and Eduard J. Zehnder, *Notes on Dynamical Systems***
  (2005, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cln/012),
  Courant Lecture Notes). Hamiltonian systems, KAM theory, and periodic
  orbits, from Moser's lectures.
* **Vladimir I. Arnold, Valery V. Kozlov, and Anatoly I. Neishtadt,
  *Mathematical Aspects of Classical and Celestial Mechanics*** (3rd ed.,
  2006, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-48926-9)).
  Integrability, perturbation theory, averaging, and KAM theory, surveyed
  with the precision of a reference.
* **Heinz Hanßmann, *Local and Semi-Local Bifurcations in Hamiltonian
  Dynamical Systems: Results and Examples*** (2007, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-38894-X), Lecture
  Notes in Mathematics). Bifurcations that preserve a Hamiltonian
  structure.
* **Jack K. Hale, *Asymptotic Behavior of Dissipative
  Systems***. Attractors and long-time behaviour of dissipative
  evolution equations.
* **Roger Temam, *Infinite-Dimensional Dynamical Systems in Mechanics
  and Physics***. Attractors for the evolution equations of mechanics
  and physics.
* **James C. Robinson, *Infinite-Dimensional Dynamical Systems: An
  Introduction to Dissipative Parabolic PDEs and the Theory of Global
  Attractors*** (2001, [Cambridge University Press](https://www.cambridgebookshop.co.uk/products/infinite-dimensional-dynamical-systems)). Global attractors, their
  dimension, and inertial manifolds, with the Navier–Stokes equations as the
  main example.
* **Vladimir V. Chepyzhov and Mark I. Vishik, *Attractors for Equations of
  Mathematical Physics*** (2002, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/coll/049), Colloquium Publications).
  Global and trajectory attractors, including nonautonomous problems and
  equations without uniqueness.
* **Sergei B. Kuksin, *Analysis of Hamiltonian PDEs*** (2000, [Oxford
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/oso/9780198503958.001.0001)).
  KAM theory for Hamiltonian PDEs and their long-time behaviour.
* **Massimiliano Berti, *Nonlinear Oscillations of Hamiltonian PDEs***
  (2007, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4681-3)). Periodic
  solutions of nonlinear wave equations by variational methods and
  Nash–Moser iteration, small divisors included.
* **Wolfgang Wasow, *Asymptotic Expansions for Ordinary Differential
  Equations***. The classical reference for singular points and
  asymptotic ODE theory; see also Part VIII.
* **Earl A. Coddington and Robert Carlson, *Linear Ordinary
  Differential Equations***. Linear ordinary differential equations
  and their spectral theory in modern form.
* **Anton Zettl, *Sturm–Liouville Theory***. The modern systematic
  account of the Sturm–Liouville problem.
* **Yulij Ilyashenko and Sergei Yakovenko, *Lectures on Analytic
  Differential Equations***. The complex-analytic theory: monodromy,
  normal forms, and limit cycles.
* **Einar Hille, *Ordinary Differential Equations in the Complex
  Domain***. The classical treatment of the analytic theory.
* **V. V. Nemytskii and V. V. Stepanov, *Qualitative Theory of
  Differential Equations***. The classical treatise on qualitative theory,
  stability, and abstract dynamical systems.
* **Lamberto Cesari, *Asymptotic Behavior and Stability Problems in
  Ordinary Differential Equations***. The classical survey of stability,
  asymptotics, and periodic solutions.
* **Carl Ludwig Siegel and Jürgen K. Moser, *Lectures on Celestial
  Mechanics***. The three-body problem, periodic solutions, and small
  divisors, by two founders of KAM theory.
* **Stephen Wiggins, *Normally Hyperbolic Invariant Manifolds in Dynamical
  Systems***. Fenichel's theory of persistence of invariant manifolds.

### More recent works

* **Gerald Teschl, *Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical
  Systems*** (2012, American Mathematical Society). A modern rigorous
  graduate course connecting ODE, dynamical systems, and spectral
  theory; freely available.
* **Luis Barreira and Claudia Valls, *Ordinary Differential Equations:
  Qualitative Theory*** (2012, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/137), Graduate
  Studies in Mathematics). A modern qualitative ODE course: conjugacies,
  centre manifolds, and normal forms.
* **David G. Schaeffer and John W. Cain, *Ordinary Differential Equations:
  Basics and Beyond*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4939-6389-8)). A broad graduate
  course combining classical ODE theory with bifurcations and dynamical
  systems. Strong on examples that explain the qualitative theory.
* **Thomas C. Sideris, *Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical
  Systems*** (2013, [Atlantis
  Press](https://doi.org/10.2991/978-94-6239-021-8)). A concise graduate
  introduction to existence, stability, phase-space geometry, and dynamical
  methods. Useful as preparation for PDE evolution and hyperbolic dynamics.
* **Henk Broer and Floris Takens, *Dynamical Systems and Chaos*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4419-6870-8)). Smooth dynamics,
  bifurcations, hyperbolicity, and chaotic behaviour in a compact graduate
  treatment. Offers a bridge from ODE courses to the structural theory.
* **Lan Wen, *Differentiable Dynamical Systems: An Introduction to
  Structural Stability and Hyperbolicity*** (2016, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/173), Graduate Studies in Mathematics). Structural stability and
  hyperbolicity, in a self-contained graduate account.
* **Christian Kuehn, *Multiple Time Scale Dynamics*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-12316-5)). Slow–fast systems,
  geometric singular perturbation, bifurcations, and stochastic extensions. A
  substantial graduate-to-research synthesis of methods scattered across the
  perturbation literature.
* **Mariana Haragus and Gérard Iooss, *Local Bifurcations, Center Manifolds,
  and Normal Forms in Infinite-Dimensional Dynamical Systems*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-85729-112-7), Universitext).
  Center manifolds and normal forms for evolution equations and for spatial
  dynamics, with water waves and pattern formation as applications.
* **Peter E. Kloeden and Martin Rasmussen, *Nonautonomous Dynamical
  Systems*** (2011, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/176), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). Processes, skew products, pullback attraction, and
  nonautonomous bifurcation.
* **Igor Chueshov, *Dynamics of Quasi-Stable Dissipative Systems*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-22903-4)). Quasi-stability,
  attractors, and long-time behaviour of nonlinear evolution equations. A
  specialised continuation of infinite-dimensional dynamical-systems theory.
* **Dmitry Treschev and Oleg Zubelevich, *Introduction to the
  Perturbation Theory of Hamiltonian Systems*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-03028-4)).
  Averaging, KAM, and separatrix splitting in a short course.
* **Alfonso Sorrentino, *Action-Minimizing Methods in Hamiltonian
  Dynamics: An Introduction to Aubry–Mather Theory*** (2015, [Princeton
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400866618), Annals of Mathematics Studies). Aubry–Mather theory
  and weak KAM, as a graduate monograph. Albert Fathi's long-circulated
  lecture notes remain the other standard entry to weak KAM.
* **Vadim Kaloshin and Ke Zhang, *Arnold Diffusion for Smooth Systems of
  Two and a Half Degrees of Freedom*** (2020, [Princeton University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9780691204932), Annals of Mathematics Studies). A complete account of Arnold
  diffusion in the lowest dimension where it occurs.
* **Hal L. Smith, *An Introduction to Delay Differential Equations with
  Applications to the Life Sciences*** (2011, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4419-7646-8)). A graduate
  introduction to functional differential equations.
* **Shangjiang Guo and Jianhong Wu, *Bifurcation Theory of Functional
  Differential Equations*** (2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-6992-6)). Normal forms and
  bifurcations for delay equations.
* **Todd Fisher and Boris Hasselblatt, *Hyperbolic Flows*** (2019,
  [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/200)). Listed in Section 10.
* **José F. Alves, *Nonuniformly Hyperbolic Attractors*** (2020,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-62814-7)). Listed in Section 10.
* **Marcelo Viana, *Lectures on Lyapunov Exponents*** (2014, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139976602)). Listed in Section 10.
* **Anatole Katok and Viorel Niţică, *Rigidity in Higher Rank Abelian
  Group Actions***, volume I (2011, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511803550)). Listed
  in Section 10.

---

## 32. Partial Differential Equations: General and Linear Theory

Evans, Taylor, and Hörmander anchor the general and linear theory,
with a range of first and second graduate courses and the classical
functional-analytic treatments of boundary value problems. The more
recent books are alternative first and second courses, with monographs
on higher-order problems, boundary behaviour in general domains, and the
formal theory of differential systems.

### Foundational works

* **Lawrence C. Evans, *Partial Differential Equations***. The
  standard first graduate course: characteristics, the classical
  equations, Sobolev spaces, weak solutions, elliptic regularity, and
  variational methods.
* **Michael E. Taylor, *Partial Differential Equations
  I–III***. Broader and more geometric, extending to spectral theory,
  microlocal analysis, and nonlinear problems.
* **Gerald B. Folland, *Introduction to Partial Differential
  Equations***. A compact analytic course centred on distributions,
  the Fourier transform, and constant-coefficient equations.
* **Michael Renardy and Robert C. Rogers, *An Introduction to Partial
  Differential Equations***. A balanced graduate text covering
  functional analysis, distributions, elliptic theory, and evolution
  equations.
* **Jeffrey Rauch, *Partial Differential Equations***. Strong on
  hyperbolic equations, energy estimates, and propagation.
* **Vladimir I. Arnold, *Lectures on Partial Differential Equations***.
  Short and geometric; the classical equations seen through
  characteristics, symmetry, and physical intuition.
* **Walter A. Strauss, *Partial Differential Equations: An
  Introduction***. Readable and model-driven; useful for intuition
  before the abstract machinery.
* **Fritz John, *Partial Differential Equations***. Short, geometric,
  and influential.
* **L. Bers, F. John, and M. Schechter, *Partial Differential
  Equations***. A classical Courant Institute text with unusually
  broad coverage.
* **François Trèves, *Basic Linear Partial Differential
  Equations***. Linear PDE through distributions and Fourier methods.
* **Friedrich Sauvigny, *Partial Differential Equations*** (2 volumes, 2nd
  ed., 2012: *Foundations and Integral Representations*; *Functional Analytic
  Methods*, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-2981-3); [volume II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-2984-4)). A
  substantial course developing classical representations and
  functional-analytic methods together. Useful for readers wanting the
  potential-theoretic and operator approaches in one sequence.
* **Lars Hörmander, *The Analysis of Linear Partial Differential
  Operators I–IV***. The research-level completion of the linear
  theory.
* **S. Mizohata, *The Theory of Partial Differential Equations***. A
  classical Japanese-school treatment strong on energy methods and
  well-posedness.
* **Vladimir Maz'ya and others, *Elliptic Boundary Value Problems in
  Domains with Point Singularities*** (with Kozlov and Rossmann). The
  reference for corner and edge asymptotics.
* **Pierre Grisvard, *Elliptic Problems in Nonsmooth Domains***. The
  standard source for regularity on polygonal and Lipschitz domains.
* **J.-L. Lions and E. Magenes, *Non-Homogeneous Boundary Value
  Problems and Applications*** (3 volumes). The functional-analytic
  theory of boundary value problems and evolution equations.
* **Robert C. McOwen, *Partial Differential Equations: Methods and
  Applications***. A first graduate course emphasizing methods and
  applications.
* **Emmanuele DiBenedetto, *Partial Differential Equations***. A
  rigorous first course with a more classical emphasis.
* **Sandro Salsa, *Partial Differential Equations in
  Action***. Modern, applied-facing, and good for modelling context.
* **Peter J. Olver, *Applications of Lie Groups to Differential
  Equations***. Symmetry groups, conservation laws, and Noether's theorem
  for differential equations.
* **Jindřich Nečas, *Direct Methods in the Theory of Elliptic
  Equations***. The classical treatment of variational methods for elliptic
  boundary-value problems in Sobolev spaces.

### More recent works

* **Qing Han, *A Basic Course in Partial Differential Equations***
  (2011, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/120), Graduate Studies in Mathematics).
  A first graduate course organised by method rather than by equation.
* **Alberto Bressan, *Lecture Notes on Functional Analysis: With Applications
  to Linear Partial Differential Equations*** (2013, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://www.ams.org/books/gsm/143/)). Functional analysis, Sobolev
  spaces, and semigroups developed toward elliptic, parabolic, and hyperbolic
  equations. A concise graduate bridge between the abstract theory and PDE.
* **Dorina Mitrea, *Distributions, Partial Differential Equations, and
  Harmonic Analysis*** (2nd ed., 2018, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-03296-8)). Listed in Section 16.
* **Luigi Ambrosio, Alessandro Carlotto, and Annalisa Massaccesi,
  *Lectures on Elliptic Partial Differential Equations*** (2018,
  [Edizioni della Normale](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-88-7642-651-3)). A short modern course from Laplace's equation
  to De Giorgi–Nash–Moser theory.
* **Xavier Fernández-Real and Xavier Ros-Oton, *Regularity Theory for
  Elliptic PDE*** (2022, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/zlam/28)). Listed in full
  in Section 33. The usual bridge from Evans to a regularity course.
* **Filippo Gazzola, Hans-Christoph Grunau, and Guido Sweers,
  *Polyharmonic Boundary Value Problems*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-12245-3), Lecture
  Notes in Mathematics). Higher-order elliptic equations, positivity,
  and the failure of the maximum principle.
* **Vladimir Maz'ya, *Boundary Behavior of Solutions to Elliptic Equations
  in General Domains*** (2018, [European Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/190)). Wiener-type criteria, boundary
  regularity, and capacity methods for elliptic equations in irregular
  domains.

---

## 33. Elliptic and Parabolic Equations: Regularity, Free Boundaries, and Viscosity Solutions

Gilbarg–Trudinger, Han–Lin, and the Russian and Krylov schools give the
regularity theory; viscosity solutions, free boundaries, variational
inequalities, and degenerate equations complete the classical picture.
The more recent books include variational existence theory,
reaction–diffusion equations, regularity courses, Monge–Ampère and
free-boundary monographs, nonlocal equations, Hamilton–Jacobi equations,
and tug-of-war games.

### Foundational works

* **David Gilbarg and Neil S. Trudinger, *Elliptic Partial
  Differential Equations of Second Order***. The central reference:
  Schauder estimates, maximum principles, De Giorgi–Nash–Moser theory,
  and quasilinear equations.
* **Qing Han and Fanghua Lin, *Elliptic Partial Differential
  Equations***. A concise second course emphasizing modern regularity
  arguments.
* **Olga A. Ladyzhenskaya and Nina N. Ural'tseva, *Linear and
  Quasilinear Elliptic Equations***. The foundational source of the
  Russian regularity theory.
* **O. A. Ladyzhenskaya, V. A. Solonnikov, and N. N. Ural'tseva,
  *Linear and Quasilinear Equations of Parabolic Type***. The
  classical large reference for parabolic estimates.
* **Avner Friedman, *Partial Differential Equations of Parabolic
  Type***. A classical systematic account.
* **Gary M. Lieberman, *Second Order Parabolic Differential
  Equations***. A modern regularity reference, especially strong on
  boundary estimates.
* **N. V. Krylov, *Lectures on Elliptic and Parabolic Equations in
  Hölder Spaces*** and ***…in Sobolev Spaces***. Schauder and Sobolev
  theory from the Krylov school, with the fully nonlinear techniques
  included.
* **N. V. Krylov, *Nonlinear Elliptic and Parabolic Equations of the
  Second Order***. The Krylov–Safonov theory and fully nonlinear
  regularity.
* **Luis A. Caffarelli and Xavier Cabré, *Fully Nonlinear Elliptic
  Equations***. The standard short monograph on viscosity solutions
  and nonlinear elliptic regularity.
* **Mariano Giaquinta, *Multiple Integrals in the Calculus of
  Variations and Nonlinear Elliptic Systems***. The reference for
  partial regularity of systems and Morrey/Campanato technique.
* **Mariano Giaquinta and Luca Martinazzi, *An Introduction to the
  Regularity Theory for Elliptic Systems, Harmonic Maps and Minimal
  Graphs***. A modern, more readable route to the same material.
* **Enrico Giusti, *Direct Methods in the Calculus of
  Variations***. Regularity for minimizers, with the De Giorgi
  technique developed carefully.
* **Michael G. Crandall, Hitoshi Ishii, and Pierre-Louis Lions,
  *User's Guide to Viscosity Solutions***. Not a book, but the single
  most-cited item in the subject and the natural starting point.
* **Guy Barles, *Solutions de viscosité des équations de
  Hamilton–Jacobi***. The standard monograph on viscosity solutions
  for first-order equations.
* **Martino Bardi and Italo Capuzzo-Dolcetta, *Optimal Control and
  Viscosity Solutions of Hamilton–Jacobi–Bellman Equations***. The
  control-theoretic treatment.
* **Shigeaki Koike, *A Beginner's Guide to the Theory of Viscosity
  Solutions***. Short and genuinely introductory.
* **Piermarco Cannarsa and Carlo Sinestrari, *Semiconcave Functions,
  Hamilton–Jacobi Equations, and Optimal Control*** (2004,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/b138356)). Semiconcavity, the
  singularities of viscosity solutions, and their propagation.
* **Luis Caffarelli and Sandro Salsa, *A Geometric Approach to Free
  Boundary Problems***. The modern entry to free-boundary regularity.
* **David Kinderlehrer and Guido Stampacchia, *An Introduction to
  Variational Inequalities and Their Applications***. The foundational
  book for obstacle problems and variational inequalities.
* **Avner Friedman, *Variational Principles and Free-Boundary
  Problems***. A classical bridge between variational methods and free
  boundaries.
* **Cristian E. Gutiérrez, *The Monge–Ampère Equation***. The standard
  earlier treatment of Aleksandrov solutions and Caffarelli's
  regularity theory.
* **Peter Lindqvist, *Notes on the p-Laplace Equation***. A concise
  introduction to the p-Laplace equation.
* **Juha Heinonen, Tero Kilpeläinen, and Olli Martio, *Nonlinear
  Potential Theory of Degenerate Elliptic Equations***. The standard
  monograph on degenerate elliptic equations.
* **Juan Luis Vázquez, *The Porous Medium Equation: Mathematical Theory***
  (2007, [Oxford University Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/acprof:oso/9780198569039.001.0001)). The monograph on the porous-medium
  equation: existence, regularity, and asymptotics.
* **Juan Luis Vázquez, *Smoothing and Decay Estimates for Nonlinear
  Diffusion Equations: Equations of Porous Medium Type*** (2006, [Oxford
  University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/acprof:oso/9780199202973.001.0001)). Fast
  diffusion and porous medium equations: smoothing effects, extinction, and
  asymptotics. The companion to *The Porous Medium Equation*.
* **José Miguel Urbano, *The Method of Intrinsic Scaling: A Systematic
  Approach to Regularity for Degenerate and Singular PDEs*** (2008,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-75932-4), Lecture Notes in Mathematics). Intrinsic scaling as a
  method, in a short research course.
* **Pavol Quittner and Philippe Souplet, *Superlinear Parabolic Problems:
  Blow-up, Global Existence and Steady States*** (2nd ed., 2019,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-18222-9)). A substantial
  reference for reaction–diffusion equations, blow-up, and global behaviour.
  Combines regularity and comparison methods with long-time dynamics.
* **Andrea Bonfiglioli, Ermanno Lanconelli, and Francesco Uguzzoni,
  *Stratified Lie Groups and Potential Theory for Their
  Sub-Laplacians***. Subelliptic equations in the group setting.
* **Murray H. Protter and Hans F. Weinberger, *Maximum Principles in
  Differential Equations***. The classical account of a technique used
  everywhere in this section.

### More recent works

* **Xavier Fernández-Real and Xavier Ros-Oton, *Regularity Theory for
  Elliptic PDE*** (2022, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/zlam/28), Zurich Lectures
  in Advanced Mathematics). A self-contained regularity course:
  Schauder estimates, Hilbert's nineteenth problem, fully nonlinear
  equations, and the obstacle problem.
* **Luigi Ambrosio, Alessandro Carlotto, and Annalisa Massaccesi,
  *Lectures on Elliptic Partial Differential Equations*** (2018,
  [Edizioni della Normale](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-88-7642-651-3)). Listed in Section 32.
* **Marino Badiale and Enrico Serra, *Semilinear Elliptic Equations for
  Beginners: Existence Results via the Variational Approach*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-85729-227-8)). Variational
  construction of semilinear elliptic solutions, including critical-point
  methods. A graduate bridge from functional analysis to nonlinear elliptic
  existence theory.
* **Hervé Le Dret, *Nonlinear Elliptic Partial Differential Equations: An
  Introduction*** (2018; English translation,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-78390-1)). Weak formulations,
  monotonicity, and variational methods for nonlinear elliptic problems. A
  course-length introduction before the specialised regularity monographs.
* **Qing Han, *Nonlinear Elliptic Equations of the Second Order*** (2016,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/171), Graduate
  Studies in Mathematics). Quasilinear and fully nonlinear equations,
  including the minimal surface and Monge–Ampère equations, with the a
  priori estimates done carefully.
* **N. V. Krylov, *Sobolev and Viscosity Solutions for Fully Nonlinear
  Elliptic and Parabolic Equations*** (2018, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/233), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). W^{2,p} solvability and viscosity solutions for fully
  nonlinear equations, by one of the founders of the regularity theory.
* **Alessio Figalli, *The Monge–Ampère Equation and Its
  Applications*** (2017, European Mathematical Society). Regularity
  theory for Monge–Ampère, with the optimal-transport connection.
* **Nam Q. Le, *Analysis of Monge–Ampère Equations*** (2024, [American
  Mathematical
  Society](https://www.ams.org/books/gsm/240/gsm240-endmatter.pdf)). Interior
  and boundary regularity, the linearized equation, and geometric
  applications. A substantial later synthesis extending the graduate
  treatments of Gutiérrez and Figalli listed in this section.
* **Arshak Petrosyan, Henrik Shahgholian, and Nina Uraltseva,
  *Regularity of Free Boundaries in Obstacle-Type Problems*** (2012,
  American Mathematical Society). A research reference for obstacle
  problems.
* **Bozhidar Velichkov, *Regularity of the One-phase Free Boundaries***
  (2023, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-13238-4), Lecture Notes of the Unione Matematica Italiana). An
  open-access introduction to one-phase free-boundary regularity,
  organised around four theorems.
* **Xavier Fernández-Real and Xavier Ros-Oton, *Integro-Differential
  Elliptic Equations*** (2024,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-54242-8), Progress in
  Mathematics). Regularity for the fractional Laplacian and general nonlocal
  operators, including fully nonlinear and obstacle problems. The nonlocal
  sequel to their *Regularity Theory for Elliptic PDE*.
* **Claudia Bucur and Enrico Valdinoci, *Nonlocal Diffusion and
  Applications*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-28739-3)). Listed in Section 26.
* **Louis Dupaigne, *Stable Solutions of Elliptic Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2011, [CRC Press](https://doi.org/10.1201/b10802)). Stable solutions, extremal solutions,
  and the regularity theory around them.
* **Emmanuele DiBenedetto, Ugo Gianazza, and Vincenzo Vespri,
  *Harnack's Inequality for Degenerate and Singular Parabolic
  Equations*** (2012, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-1584-8)). Intrinsic scaling and Harnack
  inequalities for degenerate parabolic equations.
* **Ansgar Jüngel, *Entropy Methods for Diffusive Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-34219-1)).
  Entropy dissipation, existence, and convergence to equilibrium for diffusion
  systems. A short introduction to estimates particularly useful when maximum
  principles are unavailable.
* **Vitaly Volpert, *Elliptic Partial Differential Equations*** (2 volumes:
  *Fredholm Theory of Elliptic Problems in Unbounded Domains* (2011);
  *Reaction-Diffusion Equations* (2014),
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0346-0537-3); [volume II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0813-2)). Elliptic problems
  on unbounded domains, Fredholm properties, and reaction–diffusion models. A
  research treatise complementing bounded-domain regularity books.
* **Hung Vinh Tran, *Hamilton–Jacobi Equations: Theory and Applications***
  (2021, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/213),
  Graduate Studies in Mathematics). Viscosity solutions, optimal control,
  weak KAM theory, and periodic homogenization of Hamilton–Jacobi equations.
* **Marta Lewicka, *A Course on Tug-of-War Games with Random Noise:
  Introduction and Basic Constructions*** (2020,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-46209-3), Universitext). The
  game-theoretic approach to the p-Laplacian and the infinity Laplacian.
* **Brian Street, *Maximal Subellipticity*** (2023, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783111085647), Studies
  in Mathematics). Interior regularity for maximally subelliptic
  equations, linear and fully nonlinear, with the adapted function
  spaces.
* **Diogo A. Gomes, Edgard A. Pimentel, and Vardan Voskanyan,
  *Regularity Theory for Mean-Field Game Systems*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-38934-9)).
  A short account of the elliptic and parabolic regularity of
  mean-field game systems.
* **Pierre Cardaliaguet, François Delarue, Jean-Michel Lasry, and
  Pierre-Louis Lions, *The Master Equation and the Convergence Problem
  in Mean Field Games*** (2019, [Princeton University Press](https://doi.org/10.23943/princeton/9780691190716.001.0001)). Listed in
  Section 8.
* **Augusto C. Ponce, *Elliptic PDEs, Measures and Capacities*** (2016,
  [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/140)). Listed in Section 26.
* **Pascal Auscher and Moritz Egert, *Boundary Value Problems and Hardy
  Spaces for Elliptic Systems with Block Structure*** (2023,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-29973-5)). Listed in Section 25.
* **Mourad Choulli, *Applications of Elliptic Carleman Inequalities to
  Cauchy and Inverse Problems*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-33642-8)). Carleman estimates
  as a tool for unique continuation and inverse problems.

---

## 34. Hyperbolic Equations, Conservation Laws, Dispersive PDE, Fluids, and Kinetic Theory

Dafermos, Bressan, and Lax organise the theory of conservation laws;
Tao, Cazenave, and Shatah–Struwe that of dispersive and geometric wave
equations; Temam, Galdi, and Majda–Bertozzi that of incompressible flow;
and Cercignani–Illner–Pulvirenti and Villani that of kinetic theory. The
more recent works include Bahouri–Chemin–Danchin's Littlewood–Paley
toolkit, new fluid-dynamics courses, the shock, water-wave, and kinetic
monographs, and the hyperbolic theory of the Einstein equations.

### Foundational works

#### Hyperbolic equations and conservation laws

* **Constantine M. Dafermos, *Hyperbolic Conservation Laws in
  Continuum Physics***. The encyclopedic research reference: entropy
  solutions, shocks, BV methods, and continuum-mechanical structure.
* **Alberto Bressan, *Hyperbolic Systems of Conservation Laws: The
  One-Dimensional Cauchy Problem***. Front tracking, semigroup
  solutions, and stability.
* **Helge Holden and Nils Henrik Risebro, *Front Tracking for
  Hyperbolic Conservation Laws*** (2nd ed., 2015, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-662-47507-2)). Front
  tracking as an existence method, updated.
* **Denis Serre, *Systems of Conservation Laws I–II***. A broad
  analytic and structural treatment.
* **Peter D. Lax, *Hyperbolic Systems of Conservation Laws and the
  Mathematical Theory of Shock Waves***. The short foundational
  monograph.
* **Philippe G. LeFloch, *Hyperbolic Systems of Conservation Laws: The
  Theory of Classical and Nonclassical Shock Waves*** (2002,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-8150-0)). Undercompressive
  shocks, kinetic relations, and well-posedness in the nonclassical regime.
* **Andrew Majda, *Compressible Fluid Flow and Systems of Conservation
  Laws in Several Space Variables***. Hyperbolic systems from the
  compressible-flow viewpoint.
* **Sylvie Benzoni-Gavage and Denis Serre, *Multidimensional Hyperbolic
  Partial Differential Equations: First-Order Systems and Applications***
  (2007, [Oxford University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/acprof:oso/9780199211234.001.0001)).
  Well-posedness of hyperbolic initial–boundary value problems, Kreiss
  symmetrizers, and the stability of shocks.
* **Lars Hörmander, *Lectures on Nonlinear Hyperbolic Differential
  Equations***. High-level energy and microlocal methods for nonlinear
  waves.
* **Serge Alinhac, *Hyperbolic Partial Differential Equations*** and
  ***Geometric Analysis of Hyperbolic Differential
  Equations***. Blow-up, lifespan, and the geometric approach to
  nonlinear waves.
* **Randall J. LeVeque, *Numerical Methods for Conservation
  Laws***. Numerical in title, but among the clearest expositions of
  the structure of Riemann problems.
* **Joel Smoller, *Shock Waves and Reaction–Diffusion Equations***.
  Conservation laws, shock waves, reaction–diffusion equations, and the
  Conley index. The classical graduate text.

#### Dispersive and wave equations

* **Terence Tao, *Nonlinear Dispersive Equations: Local and Global
  Analysis***. The modern toolkit: Strichartz estimates, conservation
  laws, concentration compactness, and criticality.
* **Thierry Cazenave, *Semilinear Schrödinger Equations***. The
  standard monograph for nonlinear Schrödinger equations.
* **Felipe Linares and Gustavo Ponce, *Introduction to Nonlinear
  Dispersive Equations***. A practical graduate introduction.
* **Christopher D. Sogge, *Lectures on Nonlinear Wave
  Equations***. Energy and dispersive estimates for nonlinear waves.
* **Jalal Shatah and Michael Struwe, *Geometric Wave
  Equations***. Wave maps and geometric hyperbolic PDE.
* **Sergiu Klainerman and Francesco Nicolò, *The Evolution Problem in
  General Relativity***, and **Demetrios Christodoulou and Sergiu
  Klainerman, *The Global Nonlinear Stability of the Minkowski
  Space***. Where hyperbolic analysis becomes geometry.
* **Yvonne Choquet-Bruhat, *General Relativity and the Einstein Equations***
  (2009, [Oxford University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/acprof:oso/9780199230723.001.0001)). The
  Cauchy problem and the constraint equations by the founder of the subject,
  with long appendices on the analysis.
* **Hans Ringström, *The Cauchy Problem in General Relativity*** (2009,
  [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/053)). Local
  existence, maximal globally hyperbolic developments, and the hyperbolic
  PDE theory they require.
* **Demetrios Christodoulou, *The Formation of Black Holes in General
  Relativity*** (2009, [European Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/068)). Trapped-surface formation for the
  vacuum Einstein equations from regular data. A landmark of hyperbolic PDE.
* **Reinhard Racke, *Lectures on Nonlinear Evolution Equations: Initial
  Value Problems***. Global existence for small data in nonlinear wave,
  heat, and elasticity equations.

#### Fluids and kinetic theory

* **Roger Temam, *Navier–Stokes Equations***. The classical
  functional-analytic treatment of incompressible flow.
* **Giovanni P. Galdi, *An Introduction to the Mathematical Theory of
  the Navier–Stokes Equations***. The large modern reference for
  steady and evolutionary viscous flow.
* **Andrew Majda and Andrea Bertozzi, *Vorticity and Incompressible
  Flow***. Euler and Navier–Stokes through vorticity, transport, and
  harmonic analysis.
* **Jean-Yves Chemin, *Perfect Incompressible
  Fluids***. Paradifferential and geometric methods for the Euler
  equations.
* **Pierre-Louis Lions, *Mathematical Topics in Fluid Mechanics
  I–II***. Incompressible and compressible flow from the weak-solution
  viewpoint.
* **Peter Constantin and Ciprian Foias, *Navier–Stokes
  Equations***. Dynamical-systems and functional-analytic aspects.
* **Ciprian Foias, Oscar Manley, Ricardo Rosa, and Roger Temam,
  *Navier–Stokes Equations and Turbulence*** (2001, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511546754)). Statistical solutions,
  and the connection between the mathematical theory of the Navier–Stokes
  equations and conventional turbulence theory.
* **Marco Cannone, *Ondelettes, paraproduits et
  Navier–Stokes***. Critical spaces and harmonic-analytic
  well-posedness.
* **Jean-Yves Chemin, Benoit Desjardins, Isabelle Gallagher, and
  Emmanuel Grenier, *Mathematical Geophysics: An Introduction to
  Rotating Fluids and the Navier–Stokes Equations*** (2006, [Oxford
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/oso/9780198571339.001.0001)). Rotating fluids and the analysis of the primitive
  equations.
* **Eduard Feireisl and Antonín Novotný, *Singular Limits in Thermodynamics
  of Viscous Fluids*** (2nd ed., 2017,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-63781-5)). Weak solutions
  of compressible Navier–Stokes–Fourier systems and their low-Mach-number
  and other singular limits.
* **Carlo Cercignani, Reinhard Illner, and Mario Pulvirenti, *The
  Mathematical Theory of Dilute Gases***. The standard reference for
  Boltzmann-equation analysis.
* **Cédric Villani, *A Review of Mathematical Topics in Collisional
  Kinetic Theory***. The survey that organized the modern field.
* **Robert T. Glassey, *The Cauchy Problem in Kinetic Theory***. A
  careful treatment of Vlasov and Boltzmann well-posedness.
* **Laure Saint-Raymond, *Hydrodynamic Limits of the Boltzmann
  Equation*** (2009, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-92847-8), Lecture Notes in Mathematics). The
  passage from Boltzmann to fluid equations.
* **Benoît Perthame, *Transport Equations in Biology*** (2007,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-7842-4)) and ***Parabolic Equations in Biology*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-19500-1)). Structured population models and their analytic theory.
  Perthame's *Kinetic Formulation of Conservation Laws* (2002, [Oxford
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/oso/9780198509134.001.0001)) remains the kinetic-formulation reference.
* **Charles R. Doering and J. D. Gibbon, *Applied Analysis of the
  Navier–Stokes Equations***. Energy estimates, attractors, and regularity
  questions for the Navier–Stokes equations.
* **Alexandre J. Chorin and Jerrold E. Marsden, *A Mathematical
  Introduction to Fluid Mechanics***. A short mathematical introduction to
  the equations of fluid dynamics.

### More recent works

#### Hyperbolic equations and conservation laws

* **Demetrios Christodoulou, *The Shock Development Problem*** (2019,
  [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/192)). The free-boundary problem for the
  development of shocks in compressible Euler flow, continuing his
  *Formation of Shocks in 3-Dimensional Fluids* (2007, European
  Mathematical Society).
* **Jared Speck, *Shock Formation in Small-Data Solutions to 3D
  Quasilinear Wave Equations*** (2016, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/214),
  Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). Shock formation for quasilinear
  waves, in the Christodoulou framework.

#### Dispersive and wave equations

* **Hajer Bahouri, Jean-Yves Chemin, and Raphaël Danchin, *Fourier
  Analysis and Nonlinear Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2011, Springer). Littlewood–Paley and paradifferential
  methods applied systematically to nonlinear PDE; now the standard
  technical reference.
* **M. Burak Erdoğan and Nikolaos Tzirakis, *Dispersive Partial Differential
  Equations: Wellposedness and Applications*** (2016, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781316563267)). Fourier restriction
  norms, smoothing, and low-regularity well-posedness. A graduate account
  concentrating on techniques for nonlinear dispersive equations.
* **Herbert Koch, Daniel Tataru, and Monica Vişan, *Dispersive
  Equations and Nonlinear Waves*** (2014, Birkhäuser). Lecture notes
  covering the modern concentration-compactness and
  profile-decomposition machinery.
* **Carlos E. Kenig, *Lectures on the Energy Critical Nonlinear Wave
  Equation*** (2015, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cbms/122), CBMS Regional Conference
  Series). Concentration compactness and rigidity, and the soliton
  resolution programme for the energy-critical wave equation.
* **Kenji Nakanishi and Wilhelm Schlag, *Invariant Manifolds and
  Dispersive Hamiltonian Evolution Equations*** (2011, [European
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/095)). Invariant manifolds for nonlinear Klein–Gordon
  and Schrödinger equations.
* **Joachim Krieger and Wilhelm Schlag, *Concentration Compactness for
  Critical Wave Maps*** (2012, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/106)).
  Concentration compactness and blow-up for wave maps.
* **Christian Klein and Jean-Claude Saut, *Nonlinear Dispersive Equations:
  Inverse Scattering and PDE Methods*** (2022; copyright 2021,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-91427-1)). Dispersive equations
  treated through both inverse scattering and PDE estimates, with emphasis on
  Benjamin–Ono, Davey–Stewartson, and Kadomtsev–Petviashvili models. Useful
  for comparing integrable and nonintegrable behaviour.
* **Todd Kapitula and Keith Promislow, *Spectral and Dynamical Stability of
  Nonlinear Waves*** (2013,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-6995-7)). Evans functions,
  Hamiltonian spectral theory, and orbital stability. A systematic bridge from
  linearized spectra to the dynamics of nonlinear waves.
* **Gadi Fibich, *The Nonlinear Schrödinger Equation: Singular Solutions
  and Optical Collapse*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-12748-4)). Blow-up and
  collapse for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation, combining rigorous and
  asymptotic analysis.

#### Fluids and kinetic theory

* **Jacob Bedrossian and Vlad Vicol, *The Mathematical Analysis of the
  Incompressible Euler and Navier–Stokes Equations: An Introduction***
  (2022, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/225), Graduate Studies in Mathematics).
  A one-semester graduate introduction to well-posedness, vorticity, and
  blow-up criteria.
* **Franck Boyer and Pierre Fabrie, *Mathematical Tools for the Study of the
  Incompressible Navier–Stokes Equations and Related Models*** (2013,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-5975-0)). Sobolev spaces, weak
  formulations, compactness, and evolution estimates for viscous flow. Useful
  for learning the analytic infrastructure before more specialised fluid
  monographs.
* **James C. Robinson, José L. Rodrigo, and Witold Sadowski, *The
  Three-Dimensional Navier–Stokes Equations: Classical Theory*** (2016,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139095143)). Mild solutions, weak solutions, and the
  classical regularity theory in one volume.
* **Tai-Peng Tsai, *Lectures on Navier–Stokes Equations*** (2018, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/192), Graduate Studies
  in Mathematics). Weak and mild solutions, partial regularity, self-similar
  solutions, and ε-regularity.
* **Pierre Gilles Lemarié-Rieusset, *The Navier–Stokes Problem in the
  21st Century*** (2016, CRC Press). A comprehensive modern account
  organized around the regularity problem.
* **David Lannes, *The Water Waves Problem: Mathematical Analysis and
  Asymptotics*** (2013, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/188), Mathematical
  Surveys and Monographs). The well-posedness and asymptotic theory of
  the water-wave equations.
* **Sergei Kuksin and Armen Shirikyan, *Mathematics of Two-Dimensional
  Turbulence*** (2012, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139137119)). Invariant measures
  for the stochastic two-dimensional Navier–Stokes equations.
* **Dominic Breit, Eduard Feireisl, and Martina Hofmanová, *Stochastically
  Forced Compressible Fluid Flows*** (2018, [De
  Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110492552)). Martingale solutions,
  compactness, and stochastic forcing in compressible fluid equations. A
  research monograph joining nonlinear PDE with stochastic evolution.
* **Isabelle Gallagher, Laure Saint-Raymond, and Benjamin Texier, *From
  Newton to Boltzmann: Hard Spheres and Short-range Potentials***
  (2014, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/129), Zurich Lectures in Advanced
  Mathematics). Lanford's theorem and the low-density limit, in a
  self-contained monograph.

---

## 35. Nonlinear Functional Analysis, Monotone Operators, and Fixed Points

Degree theory, fixed points, monotone operators, nonlinear semigroups, and
bifurcation. The foundational works include Deimling, Zeidler, Brezis, and the
topological fixed-point tradition. The more recent books are courses that join
the topological and the variational points of view, the modern synthesis of
monotone operator theory, and monographs on degree and bifurcation.

### Foundational works

* **Klaus Deimling, *Nonlinear Functional Analysis***. A standard
  graduate text on degree theory, fixed points, monotone operators,
  and nonlinear equations.
* **Eberhard Zeidler, *Nonlinear Functional Analysis and Its
  Applications I–IV*** and ***Applied Functional Analysis*** (2
  volumes). Encyclopedic coverage of fixed points, monotonicity,
  variational methods, and applications.
* **Louis Nirenberg, *Topics in Nonlinear Functional
  Analysis***. Short, influential notes on degree, implicit-function
  methods, and bifurcation.
* **Kung-Ching Chang, *Methods in Nonlinear Analysis*** (2005,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-29232-2), Springer Monographs in
  Mathematics). Linearisation, fixed points, degree, minimax, and Morse
  theory, with applications, in one volume.
* **Pavel Drábek and Jaroslav Milota, *Methods of Nonlinear Analysis:
  Applications to Differential Equations*** (2nd ed., 2013,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0387-8)). Degree,
  bifurcation, variational methods, and nonlinear differential equations in
  one graduate reference. A broad practical companion to the abstract
  nonlinear-analysis classics.
* **R. E. Showalter, *Monotone Operators in Banach Space and Nonlinear
  Partial Differential Equations***. A systematic treatment of
  monotonicity and nonlinear evolution.
* **Haim Brezis, *Opérateurs maximaux monotones et semi-groupes de
  contractions dans les espaces de Hilbert***. The foundational
  monograph on maximal monotone operators and nonlinear semigroups.
* **Viorel Barbu, *Nonlinear Semigroups and Differential Equations in
  Banach Spaces*** and ***Nonlinear Differential Equations of Monotone
  Types in Banach Spaces***. Monotone methods in nonlinear evolution
  equations.
* **Stephen Simons, *From Hahn–Banach to Monotonicity*** (2nd ed., 2008,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4020-6919-2)). Listed in Section 12.
* **Regina S. Burachik and Alfredo N. Iusem, *Set-Valued Mappings and
  Enlargements of Monotone Operators*** (2008, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-69757-4)). Listed in
  Section 12.
* **M. A. Krasnosel'skii, *Topological Methods in the Theory of
  Nonlinear Integral Equations***, and **Krasnosel'skii and Zabreiko,
  *Geometrical Methods of Nonlinear Analysis***. The classical
  topological fixed-point tradition.
* **Andrzej Granas and James Dugundji, *Fixed Point Theory***. A
  comprehensive topological treatment.
* **Kazimierz Goebel and W. A. Kirk, *Topics in Metric Fixed Point
  Theory***. Nonexpansive mappings and the geometry of Banach spaces.
* **N. G. Lloyd, *Degree Theory***, and **Jean Mawhin, *Topological
  Degree Methods in Nonlinear Boundary Value Problems*** (CBMS 40). The
  standard short references for topological degree and coincidence
  degree.
* **Robert D. Nussbaum, *The Fixed Point Index and Some
  Applications***. A research-level reference on index methods.
* **Melvyn S. Berger, *Nonlinearity and Functional Analysis***. A
  broad older synthesis, still useful for its range of applications.
* **Shui-Nee Chow and Jack K. Hale, *Methods of Bifurcation
  Theory***. The analytic theory of bifurcation in infinite
  dimensions.
* **Hansjörg Kielhöfer, *Bifurcation Theory: An Introduction with
  Applications to Partial Differential Equations*** (2nd ed., 2012,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-0502-3)). Local and global
  bifurcation, the Crandall–Rabinowitz theorem, Hopf bifurcation, and degree
  methods, applied to elliptic and parabolic problems.
* **Boris Buffoni and John Toland, *Analytic Theory of Global Bifurcation:
  An Introduction*** (2003, [Princeton University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400884339)). Global bifurcation in
  real-analytic settings, with water waves as the motivating application.
* **Tomáš Roubíček, *Nonlinear Partial Differential Equations with
  Applications*** (2nd ed., 2013,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0513-1)). Monotone
  operators, weak solutions, compactness, and nonlinear evolution with
  applications. A graduate-to-research synthesis of functional-analytic PDE
  methods.

### More recent works

* **Antonio Ambrosetti and David Arcoya, *An Introduction to Nonlinear
  Functional Analysis and Elliptic Problems*** (2011, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-8114-2)).
  Degree, bifurcation, and variational methods aimed at elliptic
  equations.
* **George Dinca and Jean Mawhin, *Brouwer Degree: The Core of Nonlinear
  Analysis*** (2021, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-63230-4)). A modern reconstruction of Brouwer
  degree as the root of the topological theory.
* **Heinz H. Bauschke and Patrick L. Combettes, *Convex Analysis and
  Monotone Operator Theory in Hilbert Spaces*** (2011, Springer). The modern synthesis
  of monotone operators, resolvents, and proximal methods.
* **Nikolaos S. Papageorgiou, Vicenţiu D. Rădulescu, and Dušan D. Repovš,
  *Nonlinear Analysis — Theory and Methods*** (2019,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-03430-6), Springer Monographs
  in Mathematics). A large synthesis of nonlinear operators, degree theory,
  critical point theory, and Morse theory, with applications to problems
  driven by the p-Laplacian.
* **Zhitao Zhang, *Variational, Topological, and Partial Order Methods with
  Their Applications*** (2013,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-30709-6)). Variational
  arguments, degree theory, and cone methods for nonlinear equations. Useful
  for comparing several existence techniques within the same applications.
* **Martin Väth, *Topological Analysis: From the Basics to the Triple Degree
  for Nonlinear Fredholm Inclusions*** (2012, [De
  Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110277333)). Topological degree,
  set-valued maps, and nonlinear Fredholm problems. A specialised extension of
  the standard single-valued degree theory.
* **Jean-Paul Penot, *Calculus Without Derivatives*** (2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-4538-8)).
  Listed in Section 12.
* **Juan Peypouquet, *Convex Optimization in Normed Spaces*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-13710-0)). Listed in Section 12.

---

## 36. Calculus of Variations, Γ-Convergence, and Homogenization

Direct methods, lower semicontinuity and relaxation, critical point theory,
Γ-convergence, and homogenization. Beyond the standard treatments, the list
includes the vortex and Coulomb-gas monographs, rate-independent evolution,
quantitative and periodic homogenization, composites, shape optimisation, and
optimal control.

### Foundational works

* **Bernard Dacorogna, *Direct Methods in the Calculus of
  Variations***. The standard treatment of weak lower semicontinuity,
  convexity, polyconvexity, and quasiconvexity.
* **Charles B. Morrey, Jr., *Multiple Integrals in the Calculus of
  Variations***. The foundational monograph for quasiconvexity and
  regularity of multiple integrals.
* **Mariano Giaquinta and Stefan Hildebrandt, *Calculus of Variations
  I–II***. A broad classical treatment of direct methods, regularity,
  and geometric problems.
* **Enrico Giusti, *Direct Methods in the Calculus of
  Variations***. Concise and classical; an excellent entry point.
* **Irene Fonseca and Giovanni Leoni, *Modern Methods in the Calculus
  of Variations: L^p Spaces***. A rigorous modern route through lower
  semicontinuity, relaxation, and Young measures.
* **Ivar Ekeland and Roger Temam, *Convex Analysis and Variational
  Problems***. Convex duality and variational principles with PDE
  applications.
* **Hedy Attouch, Giuseppe Buttazzo, and Gérard Michaille, *Variational
  Analysis in Sobolev and BV Spaces*** (2nd ed., 2014, [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611973488)). Listed in
  Section 12.
* **Michael Struwe, *Variational Methods***. Critical point theory,
  concentration compactness, Palais–Smale conditions, and geometric
  PDE.
* **Paul H. Rabinowitz, *Minimax Methods in Critical Point Theory with
  Applications to Differential Equations***. The classic CBMS account.
* **Michel Willem, *Minimax Theorems***. A compact systematic
  reference for modern critical-point arguments.
* **Antonio Ambrosetti and Andrea Malchiodi, *Nonlinear Analysis and
  Semilinear Elliptic Problems***. Variational and perturbative
  methods for elliptic equations.
* **Jean Mawhin and Michel Willem, *Critical Point Theory and
  Hamiltonian Systems***. The standard reference on the Hamiltonian
  side.
* **Andrea Braides, *Γ-Convergence for Beginners***. The cleanest
  first introduction to variational convergence.
* **Gianni Dal Maso, *An Introduction to Γ-Convergence***. The
  standard research reference.
* **Andrea Braides and Anneliese Defranceschi, *Homogenization of
  Multiple Integrals***. Γ-convergence applied to homogenization.
* **Alain Bensoussan, Jacques-Louis Lions, and George Papanicolaou,
  *Asymptotic Analysis for Periodic Structures***. The founding
  treatise of mathematical homogenization.
* **V. V. Jikov, S. M. Kozlov, and O. A. Oleinik, *Homogenization of
  Differential Operators and Integral Functionals***. The
  comprehensive reference, including random and degenerate cases.
* **Doina Cioranescu and Patrizia Donato, *An Introduction to
  Homogenization***. A readable modern textbook.
* **Luc Tartar, *The General Theory of Homogenization: A Personalized
  Introduction***. Compensated compactness and H-measures from one of
  the originators.
* **Grigorios A. Pavliotis and Andrew M. Stuart, *Multiscale Methods:
  Averaging and Homogenization*** (2008,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-73829-1), Texts in Applied
  Mathematics). Averaging and homogenization for ODEs, SDEs, and PDEs,
  treated first formally and then rigorously.
* **Graeme W. Milton, *The Theory of Composites*** (2002, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511613357)). Effective
  moduli, bounds, and microstructures. Reissued by SIAM in 2022.
* **Luigi Ambrosio, Nicola Fusco, and Diego Pallara, *Functions of
  Bounded Variation and Free Discontinuity Problems***. The
  variational theory of BV and SBV energies.
* **Mariano Giaquinta, Giuseppe Modica, and Jiří Souček, *Cartesian
  Currents in the Calculus of Variations I–II***. Currents and weak
  convergence for variational problems involving mappings.
* **Jürgen Jost and Xianqing Li-Jost, *Calculus of Variations***. A
  geometric-analysis-oriented introduction.
* **Pablo Pedregal, *Parametrized Measures and Variational
  Principles***. The standard short account of Young measures.
* **Etienne Sandier and Sylvia Serfaty, *Vortices in the Magnetic
  Ginzburg–Landau Model*** (2007, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4550-2)). The research monograph
  on vortex concentration in Ginzburg–Landau.
* **Gabriella Tarantello, *Selfdual Gauge Field Vortices: An Analytical
  Approach*** (2008, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4608-0)). Self-dual vortices and the associated
  elliptic systems.
* **Antoine Henrot and Michel Pierre, *Shape Variation and
  Optimization: A Geometrical Analysis*** (2018, [European Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/178)). Shape derivatives and existence in shape optimisation.
  Listed again in Section 53.
* **Andrei A. Agrachev and Yuri L. Sachkov, *Control Theory from the
  Geometric Viewpoint*** (2004,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-662-06404-7)). Controllability,
  the Pontryagin maximum principle, and second-order conditions in the
  language of differential geometry.
* **Ivar Ekeland, *Convexity Methods in Hamiltonian Mechanics***.
  Convex duality and variational methods for periodic solutions of
  Hamiltonian systems.
* **N. S. Bakhvalov and G. P. Panasenko, *Homogenisation: Averaging
  Processes in Periodic Media***. Asymptotic expansions for periodic
  homogenization problems.
* **Jürgen Moser, *Selected Chapters in the Calculus of
  Variations***. Weierstrass fields, minimal geodesics, and Aubry–Mather
  theory.

### More recent works

* **Filip Rindler, *Calculus of Variations*** (2018, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-77637-8)). A
  modern graduate course: lower semicontinuity, Young measures,
  rigidity, and BV.
* **Hansjörg Kielhöfer, *Calculus of Variations: An Introduction to the
  One-Dimensional Theory with Examples and Exercises*** (2018,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-71123-2)). Classical
  variational calculus, second variations, and existence in one dimension. A
  focused graduate introduction that develops the foundational examples in
  detail.
* **Daniel Liberzon, *Calculus of Variations and Optimal Control Theory: A
  Concise Introduction*** (2012, [Princeton University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400842643)). From the Euler–Lagrange
  equation to the maximum principle and dynamic programming.
* **Frank H. Clarke, *Functional Analysis, Calculus of Variations and
  Optimal Control*** (2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-4820-3)). Listed in Section 12.
* **Andrea Braides, *Local Minimization, Variational Evolution and
  Γ-Convergence*** (2014, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-01982-6), Lecture Notes in Mathematics).
  Variational evolution beyond global Γ-convergence.
* **Alexander Mielke and Tomáš Roubíček, *Rate-Independent Systems: Theory
  and Application*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4939-2706-7)). Energetic
  solutions, evolutionary Γ-convergence, and applications to plasticity,
  damage, and fracture.
* **Zhongwei Shen, *Periodic Homogenization of Elliptic Systems*** (2018,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-91214-1)). Convergence
  rates and uniform regularity estimates in periodic homogenization, in the
  line of Avellaneda–Lin and Kenig–Lin–Shen.
* **Scott Armstrong, Tuomo Kuusi, and Jean-Christophe Mourrat,
  *Quantitative Stochastic Homogenization and Large-Scale
  Regularity*** (2019, Springer). The modern quantitative theory.
* **Sylvia Serfaty, *Coulomb Gases and Ginzburg–Landau Vortices***
  (2015, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/152), Zurich Lectures in Advanced
  Mathematics). Mean-field limits, Coulomb gases, and the
  Ginzburg–Landau energy, in a research course.

---

## 37. Geometric Measure Theory, BV, Rectifiability, and Currents

Hausdorff measure, rectifiability, currents and varifolds, functions of
bounded variation and sets of finite perimeter, and fractal dimension. Among
the more recent books, Mattila's survey records the later theory of
rectifiability; David's Mumford–Shah monograph is the main specialised
application of uniform rectifiability.

### Foundational works

* **Herbert Federer, *Geometric Measure Theory***. The foundational
  treatise on Hausdorff measure, rectifiability, currents, and the
  area and coarea formulas; dense, forbidding, and definitive.
* **Leon Simon, *Lectures on Geometric Measure Theory***. The standard
  serious introduction: varifolds, monotonicity, compactness, and
  minimal surfaces.
* **Pertti Mattila, *Geometry of Sets and Measures in Euclidean
  Spaces***. The analyst's GMT, developed without current theory.
* **Luigi Ambrosio, Nicola Fusco, and Diego Pallara, *Functions of
  Bounded Variation and Free Discontinuity Problems***. The definitive
  BV and SBV reference.
* **Lawrence C. Evans and Ronald F. Gariepy, *Measure Theory and Fine
  Properties of Functions***. The compact working reference.
* **Enrico Giusti, *Minimal Surfaces and Functions of Bounded
  Variation***. The classical route from BV to perimeter minimization.
* **Frank Morgan, *Geometric Measure Theory: A Beginner's Guide***. A
  short, visual entry point before Simon or Federer.
* **Steven G. Krantz and Harold R. Parks, *Geometric Integration
  Theory***. Currents, area and coarea, and Plateau-type problems in a
  gentler presentation.
* **Fanghua Lin and Xiaoping Yang, *Geometric Measure Theory: An
  Introduction***. A modern introduction with geometric-analysis
  applications.
* **Camillo De Lellis, *Rectifiable Sets, Densities and Tangent
  Measures***. Research-level notes on modern rectifiability.
* **Guy David and Stephen Semmes, *Analysis of and on Uniformly
  Rectifiable Sets*** and ***Singular Integrals and Rectifiable Sets
  in R^n***. The quantitative rectifiability programme and its link
  with singular integrals.
* **Guy David, *Singular Sets of Minimizers for the Mumford–Shah
  Functional*** (2005, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/b137039)).
  Regularity of the discontinuity set of Mumford–Shah minimizers, using
  uniform rectifiability.
* **Kenneth Falconer, *The Geometry of Fractal Sets*** and ***Fractal
  Geometry: Mathematical Foundations and Applications***. Dimension
  theory in its most usable form.
* **Hassler Whitney, *Geometric Integration Theory***. The prehistory of
  currents and flat chains.

### More recent works

* **Francesco Maggi, *Sets of Finite Perimeter and Geometric
  Variational Problems*** (2012, Cambridge University Press). The modern route through Caccioppoli sets,
  compactness, and perimeter regularity; the best current textbook.
* **Pertti Mattila, *Rectifiability: A Survey*** (2023, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009288057), London Mathematical Society Lecture Note Series).
  Rectifiable sets, measures, currents, and varifolds, with the
  applications in harmonic analysis, free boundaries, and geometric
  flows. It assumes his earlier monograph and records the later theory.
* **Xavier Tolsa, *Analytic Capacity, the Cauchy Transform, and
  Non-homogeneous Calderón–Zygmund Theory*** (2014, Birkhäuser). The resolution of the
  Painlevé and Vitushkin problems, and a model of how GMT and harmonic
  analysis interact.
* **Pertti Mattila, *Fourier Analysis and Hausdorff
  Dimension*** (2015, Cambridge University Press). Projections, intersections, and the Fourier-analytic
  methods.
* **Christopher J. Bishop and Yuval Peres, *Fractals in Probability
  and Analysis*** (2017, Cambridge University Press). A modern synthesis of dimension, Brownian motion,
  and analytic techniques.
* **Washek F. Pfeffer, *The Divergence Theorem and Sets of Finite
  Perimeter*** (2012, [CRC Press](https://doi.org/10.1201/b11919)). Listed in Section 5.
* **Bozhidar Velichkov, *Regularity of the One-phase Free Boundaries***
  (2023, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-13238-4)). Listed in Section 33. Free boundaries as a GMT
  problem.
* **William H. Meeks III and Joaquín Pérez, *A Survey on Classical
  Minimal Surface Theory*** (2012, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/ulect/060),
  University Lecture Series). Listed in Section 52.
* **Luigi Ambrosio (ed.), *Geometric Measure Theory and Real Analysis***
  (2014, [Edizioni della
  Normale/Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-88-7642-523-3)). Substantial
  lecture courses on infinite-dimensional Sobolev analysis, Heisenberg
  geometry, and regularity of minimal currents. A research-school volume
  rather than a general GMT textbook.

---

## 38. Potential Theory and Capacity

Harmonic and subharmonic functions, capacities, and the Dirichlet problem, in
classical, axiomatic, probabilistic, and nonlinear forms. More recent
contributions include potential theory on Lie groups and hyperbolic space,
discrete energy, potential theory in probability, and treatments inside PDE
and harmonic-analysis books listed elsewhere. The heat-kernel side is in
Section 39.

### Foundational works

* **Lester L. Helms, *Potential Theory***. A standard graduate route
  through harmonic, subharmonic, and superharmonic functions and the
  Dirichlet problem.
* **David H. Armitage and Stephen J. Gardiner, *Classical Potential
  Theory***. The modern comprehensive treatment.
* **Sheldon Axler, Paul Bourdon, and Wade Ramey, *Harmonic Function
  Theory***. Concrete, clean, and the best place for the Euclidean
  facts about harmonic functions.
* **N. S. Landkof, *Foundations of Modern Potential Theory***. The
  classical research monograph on Riesz kernels, capacities, and
  energy.
* **Joseph L. Doob, *Classical Potential Theory and Its Probabilistic
  Counterpart***. A deep and demanding synthesis of potential theory
  and Markov processes.
* **Marcel Brelot, *Éléments de la théorie classique du potentiel***
  and ***Lectures on Potential Theory***. The French axiomatic
  tradition.
* **Sidney C. Port and Charles J. Stone, *Brownian Motion and
  Classical Potential Theory***. The probabilistic treatment.
* **Krzysztof Bogdan, Tomasz Byczkowski, Tadeusz Kulczycki, Michał Ryznar,
  Renming Song, and Zoran Vondraček, *Potential Analysis of Stable Processes
  and its Extensions*** (2009,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-02141-1), Lecture Notes in
  Mathematics). Potential theory of the fractional Laplacian: the boundary
  Harnack principle, Green functions, and Martin boundaries.
* **David R. Adams and Lars Inge Hedberg, *Function Spaces and
  Potential Theory***. Nonlinear capacities tied to Sobolev and Besov
  spaces.
* **Juha Heinonen, Tero Kilpeläinen, and Olli Martio, *Nonlinear
  Potential Theory of Degenerate Elliptic Equations***. The standard
  nonlinear reference.
* **W. K. Hayman and P. B. Kennedy, *Subharmonic Functions
  I–II***. The large classical reference on subharmonicity.
* **Thomas Ransford, *Potential Theory in the Complex
  Plane***. Concise, modern, and the standard reference for complex
  analysts.
* **Edward B. Saff and Vilmos Totik, *Logarithmic Potentials with
  External Fields***. Weighted potential theory, equilibrium measures,
  and the link to orthogonal polynomials and random matrices.
* **M. Tsuji, *Potential Theory in Modern Function Theory***. The
  classical bridge to one-variable complex analysis.
